### [abolish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abolish) *(verb)*

- **Examples:**

### [abortion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abortion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbɔːʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈbɔːrʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abo/abort/abortion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abo/abort/abortion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [absence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/absence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæbsəns/` · **US:** `/ˈæbsəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abs/absen/absence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abs/absen/absence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in somebody's absence The decision was made in my absence (= while I was not there).
  - We did not receive any news during his long absence.
  - absence from… absence from work
  - repeated absences from school

### [absent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/absent_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæbsənt/` · **US:** `/ˈæbsənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abs/absen/absent__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abs/absen/absent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was absent from work for two weeks.
  - Local people were conspicuously absent from the meeting.

### [absurd](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/absurd) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əbˈsɜːd/` · **US:** `/əbˈsɜːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abs/absur/absurd__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abs/absur/absurd__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That uniform makes the guards look absurd.
  - Of course it's not true, what an absurd idea.

### [abundance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abundance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbʌndəns/` · **US:** `/əˈbʌndəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abu/abund/abundance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abu/abund/abundance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The brochure promised beautiful walks with an abundance of wildlife.
  - The country has an abundance of natural resources.
  - We were amazed by the sheer abundance of food.

### [abuse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abuse_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbjuːs/` · **US:** `/əˈbjuːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abu/abuse/abuse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abu/abuse/abuse__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - alcohol/drug abuse
  - The system of paying cash bonuses is open to abuse (= might be used in the wrong way).
  - The legal system is open to abuse.
  - abuse of something He was arrested on charges of corruption and abuse of power.
  - What she did was an abuse of her position as manager.

### [abuse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abuse_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbjuːz/` · **US:** `/əˈbjuːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abu/abuse/abuse__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abu/abuse/abuse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to abuse alcohol/drugs
  - He systematically abused his body with heroin and cocaine.
  - People who abuse alcohol over a long period will develop health problems.

### [acceptance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/acceptance) *(noun)*

### [accountable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/accountable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkaʊntəbl/` · **US:** `/əˈkaʊntəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/accou/accountable__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/accou/accountable__us_1.mp3)

  - Lack of money will have an adverse effect on our research programme.
  - They have attracted strong adverse criticism.
  - This drug is known to have adverse side effects.

### [advocate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advocate_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈædvəkət/` · **US:** `/ˈædvəkət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/advoc/advocate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/advoc/advocate__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - advocate for something/somebody an advocate for hospital workers
  - advocate of something/somebody a staunch advocate of free speech

### [advocate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advocate_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈædvəkeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈædvəkeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/advoc/advocate__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/advoc/advocate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - advocate something The group does not advocate the use of violence.
  - Heart specialists strongly advocate low-cholesterol diets.
  - These policies have been widely advocated.
  - advocate (somebody) doing something Many experts advocate rewarding your child for good behaviour.
  - advocate that… The report advocated that all buildings be fitted with smoke detectors.
  - (British English also) The report advocated that all buildings should be fitted with smoke detectors.

### [aesthetic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aesthetic_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/esˈθetɪk/` · **US:** `/esˈθetɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aes/aesth/aesthetic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aes/aesth/aesthetic__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the aesthetic appeal of the songs
  - an aesthetic appreciation of the landscape
  - The works of art are judged on purely aesthetic grounds.
  - The benefits of conservation are both financial and aesthetic.

### [affection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/affection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈfekʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈfekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aff/affec/affection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aff/affec/affection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Children need lots of love and affection.
  - He didn't show his wife any affection.
  - She was held in deep affection by all her students.
  - affection for somebody/something Mr Darcy’s affection for his sister
  - I have a great affection for New York.

### [aftermath](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aftermath) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːftəmɑːθ/` · **US:** `/ˈæftərmæθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aft/after/aftermath__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aft/after/aftermath__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in the aftermath of something A lot of rebuilding took place in the aftermath of the war.
  - the assassination of the prime minister and its immediate aftermath
  - How does a country cope with the aftermath of war?
  - The president visited the region in the immediate aftermath of the disaster.

### [aggression](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aggression) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈɡreʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈɡreʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/agg/aggre/aggression__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/agg/aggre/aggression__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The research shows that computer games may cause aggression.
  - Nowadays our aggression is channelled into sports.

### [agricultural](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/agricultural) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæɡrɪˈkʌltʃərəl/` · **US:** `/ˌæɡrɪˈkʌltʃərəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/agr/agric/agricultural__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/agr/agric/agricultural__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - agricultural policy/land/production/development

### [aide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aide) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/eɪd/` · **US:** `/eɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aid/aid__/aid__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aid/aide_/aide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - White House aides

### [albeit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/albeit) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɔːlˈbiːɪt/` · **US:** `/ˌɔːlˈbiːɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alb/albei/albeit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alb/albei/albeit__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He finally agreed, albeit reluctantly, to help us.

### [alert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alert_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɜːt/` · **US:** `/əˈlɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ale/alert/alert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ale/alert/alert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Suddenly he found himself awake and fully alert.
  - Two alert scientists spotted the mistake.
  - Try to stay alert while driving at night.
  - an alert mind
  - mentally alert

### [alert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alert_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɜːt/` · **US:** `/əˈlɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ale/alert/alert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ale/alert/alert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - on the alert (for something) Police are warning the public to be on the alert for suspicious packages.
  - on alert More than 5 000 troops have been placed on (full) alert.

### [alert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alert_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɜːt/` · **US:** `/əˈlɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ale/alert/alert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ale/alert/alert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - alert somebody Neighbours quickly alerted the emergency services.
  - (be) alerted by something Alerted by a noise downstairs, he sat up and turned on the light.
  - be alerted that… The coastguard were alerted that a yacht had run into difficulties.

### [alien](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alien_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪliən/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪliən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ali/alien/alien__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ali/alien/alien__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an alien environment
  - In a world that had suddenly become alien and dangerous, he was her only security.

### [align](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/align) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaɪn/` · **US:** `/əˈlaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ali/align/align__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ali/align/align__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Make sure the shelf is aligned with the top of the cupboard.
  - The top and bottom line of each column on the page should align.

### [alignment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alignment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaɪnmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈlaɪnmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ali/align/alignment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ali/align/alignment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the alignment of the sun, moon and earth at a particular time
  - out of alignment (with something) A bone in my spine was out of alignment.
  - in alignment (with something) The door needs to be in alignment with the frame before you start work on it.

### [alike](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alike_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaɪk/` · **US:** `/əˈlaɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ali/alike/alike__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ali/alike/alike__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My sister and I do not look alike.
  - Airports are all alike to me.
  - Though John and Andrew look exactly alike, they act quite differently.

### [alike](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alike_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaɪk/` · **US:** `/əˈlaɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ali/alike/alike__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ali/alike/alike__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They tried to treat all their children alike.

### [allegation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allegation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæləˈɡeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌæləˈɡeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/alleg/allegation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/alleg/allegation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to investigate/deny/withdraw an allegation
  - allegation of something Several newspapers made allegations of corruption in the city's police department.
  - allegation (of something) against somebody allegations of dishonesty against him
  - allegation about somebody/something The committee has made serious allegations about interference in its work.
  - allegation that… an allegation that he had been dishonest

### [allege](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allege) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈledʒ/` · **US:** `/əˈledʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/alleg/allege__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/alleg/allege__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - allege (that)… The prosecution alleges (that) she was driving carelessly.
  - it is alleged (that)… It is alleged that he mistreated the prisoners.
  - be alleged to be, have, etc. something He is alleged to have mistreated the prisoners.
  - allege something This procedure should be followed in cases where dishonesty has been alleged.

### [allegedly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allegedly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈledʒɪdli/` · **US:** `/əˈledʒɪdli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/alleg/allegedly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/alleg/allegedly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - crimes allegedly committed during the war

### [alliance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alliance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaɪəns/` · **US:** `/əˈlaɪəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/allia/alliance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/allia/alliance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to form/make an alliance
  - alliance with somebody/something The Social Democrats are now in alliance with the Greens.
  - alliance between A and B an alliance between education and business to develop the use of technology in schools

### [allocate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allocate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæləkeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈæləkeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/alloc/allocate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/alloc/allocate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - allocate something (for something) A large sum has been allocated for buying new books for the library.
  - allocate something (to somebody/something) They intend to allocate more places to mature students this year.
  - More resources are being allocated to the project.
  - allocate somebody/something sth The project is being allocated more resources.
  - allocate something to do something Millions have been allocated to improve students' performance.

### [allocation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allocation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæləˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌæləˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/alloc/allocation__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/alloc/allocation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We have spent our entire allocation for the year.

### [allowance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allowance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaʊəns/` · **US:** `/əˈlaʊəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/allow/allowance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/allow/allowance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an allowance of $20 a day
  - a clothing/living/travel allowance
  - Do you get an allowance for clothing?

### [ally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ally_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈælaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈælaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/ally_/ally__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/ally_/ally__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - our European/NATO allies
  - a loyal ally of the United States
  - Portugal is a traditional ally of England.

### [aluminium](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aluminium) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæləˈmɪniəm/` · **US:** `/ˌæləˈmɪniəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alu/alumi/aluminium__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alu/alumi/aluminium__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - aluminium saucepans/window frames
  - aluminium foil (= for example, for wrapping food in)

### [amateur](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amateur_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæmətʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈæmətʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ama/amate/amateur__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ama/amate/amateur__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an amateur photographer

### [amateur](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amateur_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæmətʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈæmətʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ama/amate/amateur__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ama/amate/amateur__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The tournament is open to both amateurs and professionals.

### [ambassador](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ambassador) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/æmˈbæsədə(r)/` · **US:** `/æmˈbæsədər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/amb/ambas/ambassador__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/amb/ambas/ambassador__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [amend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmend/` · **US:** `/əˈmend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ame/amend/amend__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ame/amend/amend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He asked to see the amended version.
  - The law has been amended to read as follows:…
  - Parliament will vote to amend the constitution.

### [amendment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amendment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmendmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈmendmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ame/amend/amendment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ame/amend/amendment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to introduce/propose/table an amendment (= to suggest it)
  - Parliament passed the bill without further amendment.
  - amendment to something She made several minor amendments to her essay.

### [amid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amid) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmɪd/` · **US:** `/əˈmɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ami/amid_/amid__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ami/amid_/amid__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He finished his speech amid tremendous applause.
  - The firm collapsed amid allegations of fraud.

### [analogy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/analogy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnælədʒi/` · **US:** `/əˈnælədʒi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ana/analo/analogy__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ana/analo/analogy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - analogy (between A and B) The teacher drew an analogy between the human heart and a pump.
  - analogy (with something) There are no analogies with any previous legal cases.

### [anchor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anchor_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæŋkə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈæŋkər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/anc/ancho/anchor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/anc/ancho/anchor__us_1.mp3)

  - to drop anchor

### [angel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/angel) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪndʒl/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪndʒl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ang/angel/angel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ang/angel/angel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a host of angels

### [anonymous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anonymous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnɒnɪməs/` · **US:** `/əˈnɑːnɪməs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ano/anony/anonymous__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ano/anony/anonymous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an anonymous donor
  - The money was donated by a local businessman who wishes to remain anonymous.
  - the largely anonymous perpetrators of terrorist acts

### [apparatus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/apparatus) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæpəˈreɪtəs/` · **US:** `/ˌæpəˈrætəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appar/apparatus__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appar/apparatus__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a piece of laboratory apparatus
  - Firefighters needed breathing apparatus to enter the burning house.
  - Science departments say they are lacking basic apparatus.

### [appealing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appealing) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpiːlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈpiːlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appea/appealing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appea/appealing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Spending the holidays in Britain wasn't a prospect that I found particularly appealing.
  - Brightly coloured packaging made the pens especially appealing to children.
  - The large salary made their offer even more appealing to him.

### [appetite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appetite) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæpɪtaɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈæpɪtaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appet/appetite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appet/appetite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He suffered from headaches and loss of appetite.
  - The walk gave me a good appetite.
  - Don't spoil your appetite by eating between meals.

### [applaud](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/applaud) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈplɔːd/` · **US:** `/əˈplɔːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appla/applaud__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appla/applaud__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He started to applaud and the others joined in.
  - applaud somebody They rose to applaud the speaker.
  - She was applauded as she came on stage.
  - The audience applauded loudly.

### [applicable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/applicable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæplɪkəbl/` · **US:** `/əˈplɪkəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appli/applicable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appli/applicable__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Give details of children where applicable (= if you have any).
  - applicable to somebody/something Much of the form was not applicable (= did not apply) to me.

### [appoint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appoint) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɔɪnt/` · **US:** `/əˈpɔɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appoi/appoint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appoi/appoint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - appoint somebody They have appointed a new head teacher at my son's school.
  - appoint somebody to something She has recently been appointed to the committee.
  - appoint somebody (as) something They appointed him (as) captain of the English team.
  - She's been appointed British ambassador to the UN.
  - appoint somebody to do something A lawyer was appointed to represent the child.

### [appreciation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appreciation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˌpriːʃiˈeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/əˌpriːʃiˈeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appre/appreciation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appre/appreciation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She shows little appreciation of good music.
  - in appreciation The crowd murmured in appreciation.

### [arbitrary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arbitrary) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːbɪtri/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrbɪtreri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arb/arbit/arbitrary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arb/arbit/arbitrary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The choice of players for the team seemed completely arbitrary.
  - He makes unpredictable, arbitrary decisions.

- **UK:** `/ˌɑːkɪˈtektʃərəl/` · **US:** `/ˌɑːrkɪˈtektʃərəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arc/archi/architectural__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arc/archi/architectural__us_1.mp3)

  - architectural features

### [archive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/archive_1) *(noun)*

- **Examples:**

### [arena](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arena) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈriːnə/` · **US:** `/əˈriːnə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/are/arena/arena__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/are/arena/arena__us_1.mp3)

### [arguably](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arguably) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːɡjuəbli/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrɡjuəbli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arg/argua/arguably__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arg/argua/arguably__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [arm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arm_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɑːm/` · **US:** `/ɑːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arm/arm__/arm__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arm/arm__/arm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The country was arming against the enemy.
  - arm yourself/somebody (with something) The men armed themselves with sticks and stones.

- **Examples:**

### [articulate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/articulate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɑːˈtɪkjuleɪt/` · **US:** `/ɑːrˈtɪkjuleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/art/artic/articulate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/art/artic/articulate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [ash](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ash_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/æʃ/` · **US:** `/æʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ash/ash__/ash__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ash/ash__/ash__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cigarette ash
  - black volcanic ash
  - Ash from the volcano fell over a wide area.
  - She flicked ash out of the window carelessly.

### [aspiration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aspiration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæspəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌæspəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/asp/aspir/aspiration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/asp/aspir/aspiration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I didn't realize you had political aspirations.
  - aspiration to do something He has never had any aspiration to earn a lot of money.
  - aspiration for something What changes are needed to meet women's aspirations for employment?

### [aspire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aspire) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈspaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/əˈspaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/asp/aspir/aspire__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/asp/aspir/aspire__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - aspire (to something) She aspired to a scientific career.
  - aspire to be/do something He aspired to be their next leader.

### [assassination](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assassination) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˌsæsɪˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/əˌsæsɪˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assas/assassination__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assas/assassination__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The president survived a number of assassination attempts.
  - the assassination of John F. Kennedy

### [assault](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assault_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɔːlt/` · **US:** `/əˈsɔːlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assau/assault__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assau/assault__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Both men were charged with assault.
  - sexual assaults
  - assault on/upon somebody A significant number of indecent assaults on women go unreported.

### [assault](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assault_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɔːlt/` · **US:** `/əˈsɔːlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assau/assault__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assau/assault__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has been charged with assaulting a police officer.
  - Four women have been sexually assaulted in the area recently.
  - He admitted indecently assaulting the child.
  - Many healthcare workers say they have been physically assaulted by patients.

### [assemble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assemble) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsembl/` · **US:** `/əˈsembl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assem/assemble__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assem/assemble__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - All the students were asked to assemble in the main hall.
  - She then addressed the assembled company (= all the people there).
  - assemble something to assemble evidence/data
  - The manager has assembled a world-class team.
  - He tried to assemble his thoughts.

### [assembly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assembly) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsembli/` · **US:** `/əˈsembli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assem/assembly__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assem/assembly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - state/legislative/federal/local assemblies
  - Power has been handed over to provincial and regional assemblies.
  - The national assembly has voted to adopt the budget.
  - the California Assembly
  - the UN General Assembly
  - assembly elections
  - an elected assembly

### [assert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assert) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɜːt/` · **US:** `/əˈsɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/asser/assert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/asser/assert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - assert that… She continued to assert that she was innocent.
  - The report asserts confidently that the industry will grow.
  - assert something She continued to assert her innocence.
  - The German Chancellor was anxious to assert her government's commitment to European unity.
  - + speech ‘That is wrong,’ he asserted.
  - it is asserted that… It is commonly asserted that older people prefer to receive care from family members.

### [assertion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assertion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɜːʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈsɜːrʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/asser/assertion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/asser/assertion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was correct in his assertion that the minister had been lying.
  - Do you have any evidence to support your assertions?

### [assurance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assurance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈʃɔːrəns/` · **US:** `/əˈʃʊrəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assur/assurance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assur/assurance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They called for assurances that the government is committed to its education policy.
  - Unemployment seems to be rising, despite repeated assurances to the contrary.

### [asylum](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/asylum) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsaɪləm/` · **US:** `/əˈsaɪləm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/asy/asylu/asylum__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/asy/asylu/asylum__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to seek/apply for/be granted asylum
  - There was a nationwide debate on whether the asylum laws should be changed.

### [atrocity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/atrocity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtrɒsəti/` · **US:** `/əˈtrɑːsəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/atr/atroc/atrocity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/atr/atroc/atrocity__us_1.mp3)

### [attain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attain) *(verb)*

### [attendance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attendance) *(noun)*

### [attorney](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attorney) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtɜːni/` · **US:** `/əˈtɜːrni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attor/attorney__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attor/attorney__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/ˈætrɪbjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈætrɪbjuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attri/attribute__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attri/attribute__us_2.mp3)

## B

### [backdrop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/backdrop) *(noun)*

### [backup](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/backup) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbækʌp/` · **US:** `/ˈbækʌp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bac/backu/backup__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bac/backu/backup__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [boundary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/boundary) *(noun)*

### [breach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/breach_2) *(verb)*

  - The national breakdown organizations are on hand to help motorists 24 hours a day.

### [buck](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/buck_1) *(noun)*

  - We're talking big bucks (= a lot of money) here.

### [buddy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/buddy_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbʌdi/` · **US:** `/ˈbʌdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bud/buddy/buddy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bud/buddy/buddy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an old college buddy of mine
  - Howard and Mick were drinking buddies.
  - We were good buddies.
  - I’d like you to meet an old college buddy of mine.

### [buffer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/buffer_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbʌfə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈbʌfər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/buf/buffe/buffer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/buf/buffe/buffer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - buffer against something Support from family and friends acts as a buffer against stress.
  - buffer between A and B She often had to act as a buffer between father and son.
  - The organization acts as a buffer between the management and the union.
  - a buffer state (= a small country between two powerful states that helps keep peace between them)
  - a buffer zone (= an area of land between two opposing armies or countries)
  - Peacekeepers have been sent in to establish a buffer zone between the rival forces.

### [bulk](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bulk_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bʌlk/` · **US:** `/bʌlk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bul/bulk_/bulk__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bul/bulk_/bulk__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The bulk of the population lives in cities.
  - The great bulk of the work has now been done.

### [burden](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/burden_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɜːdn/` · **US:** `/ˈbɜːrdn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bur/burde/burden__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bur/burde/burden__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to bear/carry/ease/reduce/share the burden
  - the burden (of something) The main burden of caring for old people falls on the state.
  - burden on somebody the heavy tax burden on working people
  - a burden to somebody I don't want to become a burden to my children when I'm old.

### [bureaucracy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bureaucracy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bjʊəˈrɒkrəsi/` · **US:** `/bjʊˈrɑːkrəsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bur/burea/bureaucracy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bur/burea/bureaucracy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - unnecessary/excessive bureaucracy
  - We need to reduce paperwork and bureaucracy in the company.

### [burial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/burial) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈberiəl/` · **US:** `/ˈberiəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bur/buria/burial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bur/buria/burial__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a burial place/mound/site
  - Her body was sent home for burial.
  - His family insisted he should be given a proper burial.
  - baptism, marriage and burial services

### [burst](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/burst_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɜːst/` · **US:** `/bɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bur/burst/burst__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bur/burst/burst__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That balloon will burst if you blow it up any more.
  - The dam burst under the weight of water.
  - Shells were bursting (= exploding) all around us.
  - (figurative) He felt he would burst with anger and shame.
  - burst apart There was a danger that the engine would burst apart.
  - burst something Don't burst that balloon!
  - The river burst its banks and flooded nearby towns.
  - a burst pipe

- **UK:** `/ˈkɔːʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈkɔːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cau/cauti/caution__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cau/cauti/caution__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈkɔːʃəs/` · **US:** `/ˈkɔːʃəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cau/cauti/cautious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cau/cauti/cautious__us_1.mp3)

### [circulate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/circulate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːkjəleɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrkjəleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cir/circu/circulate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cir/circu/circulate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The condition prevents the blood from circulating freely.
  - circulate something Cooled air is circulated throughout the building.

### [circulation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/circulation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsɜːkjəˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌsɜːrkjəˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cir/circu/circulation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cir/circu/circulation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [citizenship](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/citizenship) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪtɪzənʃɪp/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪtɪzənʃɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cit/citiz/citizenship__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cit/citiz/citizenship__us_1.mp3)

### [civic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/civic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪvɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪvɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/civ/civic/civic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/civ/civic/civic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - civic buildings/leaders

### [civilian](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/civilian_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səˈvɪliən/` · **US:** `/səˈvɪliən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/civ/civil/civilian__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/civ/civil/civilian__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He left the army and returned to civilian life.
  - The bombs resulted in large numbers of civilian casualties.
  - He condemned the attacks on civilian targets.

### [civilian](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/civilian_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈvɪliən/` · **US:** `/səˈvɪliən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/civ/civil/civilian__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/civ/civil/civilian__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Two soldiers and one civilian were killed in the explosion.
  - Hundreds of innocent civilians have died in the air strikes.
  - Further measures are needed to protect civilians.
  - The group was accused of targeting civilians.

### [clarity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clarity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklærəti/` · **US:** `/ˈklærəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/clari/clarity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/clari/clarity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a lack of clarity in the law
  - The brilliant clarity of his argument could not be faulted.

### [clash](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clash_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klæʃ/` · **US:** `/klæʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/clash/clash__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/clash/clash__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Clashes broke out between police and demonstrators.
  - Eight people were wounded in a clash with border guards.
  - Several people were injured in violent clashes with the police.

### [classification](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/classification) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌklæsɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌklæsɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/class/classification__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/class/classification__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a style of music that defies classification (= is like no other)

### [cling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cling) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/klɪŋ/` · **US:** `/klɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/cling/cling__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/cling/cling__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cling to somebody/something survivors clinging to a raft
  - Leaves still clung to the branches.
  - cling on to somebody/something She clung on to her baby.
  - cling on Cling on tight!
  - cling together They clung together, shivering with cold.

### [clinical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clinical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklɪnɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈklɪnɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/clini/clinical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/clini/clinical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - clinical research (= done on patients, not just considering theory)
  - clinical training (= the part of a doctor’s training done in a hospital)
  - She regarded her patients from a purely clinical standpoint.

### [closure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/closure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkləʊʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkləʊʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/closu/closure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/closu/closure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - factory closures
  - The hospital has been threatened with closure.
  - Spending cuts could mean the closure of the village school.

### [cluster](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cluster_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklʌstə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈklʌstər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clu/clust/cluster__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clu/clust/cluster__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The telescope is focused on a dense cluster of stars at the edge of the galaxy.
  - The plant bears its flowers in clusters.
  - a leukaemia cluster (= an area where there are more cases of the disease than you would expect)

### [coalition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coalition) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkəʊəˈlɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkəʊəˈlɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coali/coalition__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coali/coalition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The two parties have formed a coalition.
  - a two-party coalition
  - a coalition government
  - (in) coalition (with somebody) The two parties governed in coalition for four years.
  - They didn't rule out coalition with the Social Democrats.
  - coalition between A and B a coalition between the Socialists and Communists

### [coastal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coastal) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkəʊstl/` · **US:** `/ˈkəʊstl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coast/coastal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coast/coastal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - coastal waters/resorts/scenery
  - a coastal path (= one that follows the line of the coast)

### [cocktail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cocktail) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒkteɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːkteɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coc/cockt/cocktail__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coc/cockt/cocktail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a cocktail bar/cabinet/lounge/shaker

### [cognitive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cognitive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒɡnətɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːɡnətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cog/cogni/cognitive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cog/cogni/cognitive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a child’s cognitive development
  - cognitive psychology
  - They are studying the links between cognitive and linguistic development in young children.
  - This is both a cognitive and an emotional process.

### [coincide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coincide) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkəʊɪnˈsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌkəʊɪnˈsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coi/coinc/coincide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coi/coinc/coincide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a pity our trips to New York don't coincide.
  - coincide with something The strike was timed to coincide with the party conference.
  - The singer's arrival was timed to coincide with the opening of the festival.

### [collaborate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/collaborate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈlæbəreɪt/` · **US:** `/kəˈlæbəreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colla/collaborate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colla/collaborate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Researchers around the world are collaborating to develop a new vaccine.
  - collaborate (with somebody) (on something) We have collaborated on many projects over the years.
  - collaborate (with somebody) (in something/in doing something) She agreed to collaborate with him in writing her biography.

### [collaboration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/collaboration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˌlæbəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/kəˌlæbəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colla/collaboration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colla/collaboration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was a collaboration that produced extremely useful results.
  - The new plane was a triumph of European industrial and technical collaboration.
  - collaboration with somebody (on something) the results of a fruitful collaboration with the industry
  - in collaboration with somebody She wrote the book in collaboration with one of her students.
  - in collaboration with somebody on something The government worked in close collaboration with teachers on the new curriculum.
  - collaboration between A and B collaboration between the teachers and the government

### [collective](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/collective_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈlektɪv/` · **US:** `/kəˈlektɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colle/collective__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colle/collective__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - collective leadership/decision-making/responsibility
  - collective memory (= things that a group of people or a community know or remember, that are often passed from parents to children)

### [collision](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/collision) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈlɪʒn/` · **US:** `/kəˈlɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colli/collision__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colli/collision__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - collision between A and B a collision between two trains
  - collision with somebody/something Stewart was injured in a collision with another player.
  - a head-on collision (= between two vehicles that are moving towards each other)
  - a mid-air collision (= between two aircraft while they are flying)
  - in collision with somebody/something His car was in collision with a motorbike.
  - Wind and ice were blamed for the collision involving up to 12 vehicles.

### [colonial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/colonial_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈləʊniəl/` · **US:** `/kəˈləʊniəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colon/colonial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colon/colonial__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a colonial power
  - Tunisia achieved independence from French colonial rule in 1956.
  - Western colonial attitudes

### [columnist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/columnist) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒləmnɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːləmnɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colum/columnist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colum/columnist__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a newspaper columnist

### [combat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/combat_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmbæt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmbæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comba/combat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comba/combat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in combat He was killed in combat.
  - armed/unarmed combat (= with/without weapons)
  - combat troops
  - combat boots
  - aerial combat
  - Knightly combats were only a very small part of medieval warfare.

### [combat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/combat_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmbæt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmbæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comba/combat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comba/combat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - measures to combat crime/inflation/unemployment/disease

### [commence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commence) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmens/` · **US:** `/kəˈmens/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/commence__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/commence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The meeting is scheduled to commence at noon.
  - I will be on leave during the week commencing 15 February.
  - commence with something The day commenced with a welcome from the principal.
  - commence something She commenced her medical career in 1956.
  - The company commenced operations in April.
  - to commence bankruptcy proceedings against somebody
  - commence doing something We commence building next week.
  - commence to do something Operators commenced to build pipelines in 1862.

### [commentary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commentary) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒməntri/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmənteri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/commentary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/commentary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sports commentary
  - Our reporters will give a running commentary (= a continuous one) on the election results as they are announced.
  - He kept up a running commentary on everyone who came in or went out.

### [commentator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commentator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmənteɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmənteɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/commentator__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/commentator__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a television/sports commentator
  - He's one of the great sports commentators of our time.

### [commerce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commerce) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmɜːs/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/commerce__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/commerce__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Leaders of industry and commerce met at the summit in Paris.

### [commissioner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commissioner) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmɪʃənə(r)/` · **US:** `/kəˈmɪʃənər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commi/commissioner__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commi/commissioner__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Church Commissioners (= the group of people responsible for controlling the financial affairs of the Church of England)
  - European Commissioners

### [commodity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commodity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmɒdəti/` · **US:** `/kəˈmɑːdəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commo/commodity__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commo/commodity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - rice, flour and other basic commodities
  - a drop in commodity prices
