  - basic agricultural commodities

### [communist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/communist_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmjənɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmjənɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commu/communist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commu/communist__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - communist ideology

### [companion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/companion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpænjən/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpænjən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compa/companion__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compa/companion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - travelling companions
  - Geoff was my companion on the journey.
  - (figurative) Fear was the hostages' constant companion.

### [comparable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comparable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmpərəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmpərəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compa/comparable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compa/comparable__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A comparable house in the south of the city would cost twice as much.
  - comparable to/with somebody/something The situation in the US is not directly comparable to that in the UK.
  - Inflation is now at a rate comparable with that in other European countries.
  - comparable in something The two machines are comparable in size.

### [compassion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compassion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpæʃn/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpæʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compa/compassion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compa/compassion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to feel/show compassion
  - She was known as a hard woman with no compassion, no emotion.
  - In an overworked doctor, feelings of compassion are soon lost.

### [compel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compel) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpel/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/compel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/compel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - compel somebody to do something The law can compel fathers to make regular payments for their children.
  - I feel compelled to write and tell you how much I enjoyed your book.
  - The court has powers to compel witnesses to attend.
  - compel something Last year ill health compelled his retirement.

### [compelling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compelling) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpelɪŋ/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpelɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/compelling__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/compelling__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her latest book makes compelling reading.
  - His eyes were strangely compelling.

### [compensate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compensate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmpenseɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmpenseɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/compensate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/compensate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Nothing can compensate for the loss of a loved one.

### [compensation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compensation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒmpenˈseɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːmpenˈseɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/compensation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/compensation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to claim/award/receive compensation
  - The employer has a duty to pay full compensation for injuries received at work.
  - in compensation to receive £10 000 in compensation
  - She received a cash sum by way of compensation.

### [competence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/competence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmpɪtəns/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmpɪtəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/competence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/competence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to gain a high level of competence in English
  - professional/technical competence

### [competent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/competent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmpɪtənt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmpɪtənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/competent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/competent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's very competent in his work.
  - competent to do something Make sure the firm is competent to carry out the work.
  - I don’t feel competent to comment.
  - competent at something She is highly competent at her job.

### [compile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compile) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpaɪl/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compi/compile__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compi/compile__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We are trying to compile a list of suitable people for the job.
  - The album was compiled from live recordings from last year's tour.
  - The figures were compiled from a survey of 2  000 schoolchildren.

### [complement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complement_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmplɪment/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmplɪment/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The excellent menu is complemented by a good wine list.
  - The team needs players who complement each other.
  - The flavours in the dish complement each other perfectly.

### [complexity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complexity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpleksəti/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpleksəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complexity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complexity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the increasing complexity of modern telecommunication systems
  - I was astonished by the size and complexity of the problem.

### [compliance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compliance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈplaɪəns/` · **US:** `/kəmˈplaɪəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/compliance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/compliance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - compliance (with something) procedures that must be followed to ensure full compliance with the law
  - in compliance with something Safety measures were carried out in compliance with paragraph 6 of the building regulations.
  - (business) The compliance officer is responsible for enforcing policies and procedures.

### [complication](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complication) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒmplɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːmplɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complication__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complication__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The bad weather added a further complication to our journey.

### [comply](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comply) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈplaɪ/` · **US:** `/kəmˈplaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/comply__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/comply__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They refused to comply with the UN resolution.
  - All furniture must comply with the fire safety regulations.
  - When requested to leave, they refused to comply.
  - What sanctions can they take against us if we fail to comply?

### [composition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/composition) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒmpəˈzɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːmpəˈzɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compo/composition__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compo/composition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the chemical composition of the soil
  - the composition of the board of directors
  - the size and composition of an average class

### [compromise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compromise_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmprəmaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmprəmaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compr/compromise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compr/compromise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - After lengthy talks the two sides finally reached a compromise.
  - In any relationship, you have to make compromises.
  - a compromise solution/agreement/candidate

### [compromise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compromise_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmprəmaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmprəmaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compr/compromise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compr/compromise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Neither side is prepared to compromise.
  - compromise (with somebody) (on something) After much argument, the judges finally compromised on (= agreed to give the prize to) the 18-year old pianist.
  - They were unwilling to compromise with the terrorists.

### [compute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compute) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpjuːt/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpjuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compu/compute__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compu/compute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The losses were computed at £5 million.
  - These measurements can be used to compute the average size.

### [conceal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conceal) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsiːl/` · **US:** `/kənˈsiːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - conceal somebody/something The paintings were concealed beneath a thick layer of plaster.
  - Tim could barely conceal his disappointment.
  - She sat down to conceal the fact that she was trembling.
  - conceal somebody/something from somebody/something For a long time his death was concealed from her.

### [concede](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/concede) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsiːd/` · **US:** `/kənˈsiːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcede__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcede__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + speech ‘Not bad,’ she conceded grudgingly.
  - concede (that)… He was forced to concede (that) there might be difficulties.
  - concede something I had to concede the logic of this.
  - concede something to somebody He reluctantly conceded the point to me.
  - concede somebody something He reluctantly conceded me the point.
  - it is conceded that… It must be conceded that different judges have different approaches to these cases.

### [conceive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conceive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsiːv/` · **US:** `/kənˈsiːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconceive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He conceived the idea of transforming the old power station into an arts centre.
  - The dam project was originally conceived in 1977.

### [conception](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conception) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsepʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈsepʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconception__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconception__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - conception of something Marx’s conception of social justice
  - He has no conception of how difficult life is if you're unemployed.
  - conception that… I had to reassess my conception that she was basically an honest person.

### [concession](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/concession) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈseʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈseʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcession__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcession__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The firm will be forced to make concessions if it wants to avoid a strike.
  - to win a concession from somebody
  - a major/an important concession
  - She made no concession to his age; she expected him to work as hard as she did.

### [condemn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/condemn) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈdem/` · **US:** `/kənˈdem/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcond/xcondemn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcond/xcondemn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - condemn somebody/something The government issued a statement condemning the killings.
  - condemn somebody/something for/as something The editor of the newspaper was condemned as lacking integrity.

### [confer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confer) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfɜː(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈfɜːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He wanted to confer with his colleagues before reaching a decision.

### [confession](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confession) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfeʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈfeʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfession__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfession__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - After hours of questioning by police, she made a full confession.
  - His confession was extracted under torture.

### [configuration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/configuration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˌfɪɡəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˌfɪɡjəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfiguration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfiguration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - configuration of something The design is based on four configurations of squares.
  - There was a changing configuration of lights coming from the stadium.
  - in a configuration The stars seemed to appear in a different configuration.

### [confine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confine) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfaɪn/` · **US:** `/kənˈfaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be confined to (doing) something The work will not be confined to the Glasgow area.
  - confine yourself to (doing) something I will confine myself to looking at the period from 1900 to 1916.

### [confirmation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confirmation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒnfəˈmeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːnfərˈmeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfirmation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfirmation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm still waiting for confirmation of the test results.

### [confront](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confront) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfrʌnt/` · **US:** `/kənˈfrʌnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfront__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfront__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What is to be done about the economic problems confronting the country?
  - I was suddenly confronted by the task of rewriting the entire book.
  - The government found itself confronted by massive opposition.

### [confrontation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confrontation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒnfrʌnˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːnfrənˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfrontation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfrontation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - confrontation (with somebody) She wanted to avoid another confrontation with her father.
  - confrontation between A and B Confrontation between employers and unions has resulted in strike action.

### [congratulate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/congratulate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈɡrætʃəleɪt/` · **US:** `/kənˈɡrætʃəleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcong/xcongratulate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcong/xcongratulate__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I congratulated them all on their results.
  - The authors are to be congratulated on producing such a clear and authoritative work.

### [congregation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/congregation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒŋɡrɪˈɡeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːŋɡrɪˈɡeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcong/xcongregation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcong/xcongregation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The congregation stood to sing the hymn.

### [congressional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/congressional) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈɡreʃənl/` · **US:** `/kənˈɡreʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcong/xcongressional__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcong/xcongressional__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a congressional committee/bill
  - the midterm Congressional elections

### [conquer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conquer) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒŋkə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːŋkər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconq/xconquer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconq/xconquer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The Normans conquered England in 1066.
  - In 330 BC Persia was conquered by Alexander the Great.
  - conquered territories/subjects/lands
  - The Roman empire offered citizenship to its conquered peoples.

### [conscience](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conscience) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnʃəns/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnʃəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconscience__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconscience__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to have a clear/guilty conscience (= to feel that you have done right/wrong)
  - This is a matter of individual conscience (= everyone must make their own judgement about it).
  - He won't let it trouble his conscience.

### [consciousness](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consciousness) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnʃəsnəs/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnʃəsnəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsciousness__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsciousness__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can't remember any more—I must have lost consciousness.
  - She did not regain consciousness and died the next day.

### [consecutive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consecutive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsekjətɪv/` · **US:** `/kənˈsekjətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsecutive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsecutive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was absent for nine consecutive days.
  - He is beginning his fourth consecutive term of office.
  - After six consecutive defeats, the team was almost ready to give up.
  - Interest rates rose again for the fifth consecutive month.
  - They achieved their third consecutive year of record sales.

### [consensus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consensus) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsensəs/` · **US:** `/kənˈsensəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsensus__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsensus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - consensus (about/on something) She is skilled at achieving consensus on sensitive issues.
  - There is a growing consensus of opinion on this issue.
  - an attempt to reach a consensus
  - There now exists a broad political consensus in favour of economic reform.
  - consensus politics (= that people in general agree with)
  - consensus (among somebody) (about/on something) There is a general consensus among teachers about the need for greater security in schools.
  - consensus that… There seems to be a consensus that the plan should be rejected.
  - by consensus They have always governed by consensus.

### [consent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consent_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsent/` · **US:** `/kənˈsent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The written consent of a parent is required.
  - consent to something Children under 16 cannot give consent to medical treatment.
  - to refuse/withhold your consent
  - without (somebody's) consent He is charged with taking a car without the owner's consent.

### [consent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consent_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsent/` · **US:** `/kənˈsent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - When she told them what she intended, they readily consented.
  - consent to something He reluctantly consented to the proposal.
  - consent to do something She finally consented to answer our questions.

### [conserve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conserve_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsɜːv/` · **US:** `/kənˈsɜːrv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconserve__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconserve__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - new laws to conserve wildlife in the area

### [consistency](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consistency) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsɪstənsi/` · **US:** `/kənˈsɪstənsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsistency__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsistency__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She has played with great consistency all season.
  - We need to ensure the consistency of service to our customers.

### [consolidate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consolidate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsɒlɪdeɪt/` · **US:** `/kənˈsɑːlɪdeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsolidate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsolidate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - With this new movie he has consolidated his position as the country's leading director.
  - Italy consolidated their lead with a second goal.

### [constituency](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/constituency) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈstɪtʃuənsi/` · **US:** `/kənˈstɪtʃuənsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstituency__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstituency__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Unemployment is high in her constituency.
  - He owns a house in his Darlington constituency.

### [constitute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/constitute) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnstɪtjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnstɪtuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstitute__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstitute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Does such an activity constitute a criminal offence?
  - Climate change constitutes a major threat to life.
  - to constitute a danger/crime/breach/nuisance
  - His action was interpreted as constituting a threat to the community.

### [constitution](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/constitution) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒnstɪˈtjuːʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːnstɪˈtuːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstitution__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstitution__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - your right to vote under the constitution
  - According to the constitution…
  - to propose a new amendment to the Constitution
  - the South African Constitution
  - A two-thirds majority is needed to amend the club’s constitution.

### [constitutional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/constitutional_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒnstɪˈtjuːʃənl/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːnstɪˈtuːʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstitutional__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstitutional__us_1.mp3)

### [constraint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/constraint) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈstreɪnt/` · **US:** `/kənˈstreɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstraint__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconstraint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - constraints of time/money/space
  - financial/economic/legal/political constraints
  - constraint on something This decision will impose serious constraints on all schools.

### [consultation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consultation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒnslˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːnslˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsultation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsultation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a consultation document/paper/period/process
  - consultation with somebody/something The decision was taken after close consultation with local residents.
  - in consultation with somebody/something acting in consultation with all the departments involved

### [contemplate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contemplate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntəmpleɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntəmpleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontemplate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontemplate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - contemplate something You're too young to be contemplating retirement.
  - contemplate doing something I have never contemplated living abroad.
  - contemplate how/what, etc… He continued while she contemplated how to answer.

### [contempt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contempt) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtempt/` · **US:** `/kənˈtempt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontempt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontempt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - with contempt She looked at him with contempt.
  - I shall treat that suggestion with the contempt it deserves.
  - beneath contempt His treatment of his children is beneath contempt (= so bad that it is not even worth feeling contempt for).
  - in contempt Politicians seem to be generally held in contempt by ordinary people.
  - contempt for somebody/something They had shown a contempt for the values she thought important.

### [contend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtend/` · **US:** `/kənˈtend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I would contend that the minister's thinking is flawed on this point.

### [contender](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contender) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtendə(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈtendər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontender__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontender__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - contender (for something) a contender for a gold medal in the Olympics
  - a leading/serious/strong contender for the party leadership
  - contender to do something Germany last night emerged as a contender to stage next year's event.

### [content](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/content2_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtent/` · **US:** `/kənˈtent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontent__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He seemed more content, less bitter.
  - content with something Not content with stealing my boyfriend (= not thinking that this was enough), she has turned all my friends against me.
  - He had to be content with third place.

### [contention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contention) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtenʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈtenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - One area of contention is the availability of nursery care.
  - Privatization of the health service remains a point of contention.
  - There is no contention between the two groups.

### [continually](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/continually) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtɪnjuəli/` · **US:** `/kənˈtɪnjuəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinually__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinually__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They argue continually about money.

### [contractor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contractor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntræktə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntræktər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontractor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontractor__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a building/roofing/electrical contractor
  - a defence/security/military/civilian contractor
  - a private/an independent contractor
  - to employ an outside contractor

### [contradiction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contradiction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒntrəˈdɪkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontradiction__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontradiction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - contradiction (between A and B) There is a contradiction between the two sets of figures.
  - How can we resolve this apparent contradiction?
  - in contradiction to something His public speeches are in direct contradiction to his personal lifestyle.

### [contrary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contrary1_1#contrary1_sng_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntrəri/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntreri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Contrary to popular belief, many cats dislike milk.
  - The government has decided that the publication of the report would be ‘contrary to the public interest’.
  - Contrary to expectations and the weather forecast, we had a fine day for the experiment.

### [contrary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contrary1_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntrəri/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntreri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - In the end the contrary was proved true: he was innocent and she was guilty.

### [contributor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contributor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtrɪbjətə(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈtrɪbjətər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontributor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontributor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a regular contributor to this magazine
  - I am a contributor to several blogs.

### [conversion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conversion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈvɜːʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈvɜːrʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconversion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconversion__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Their main business is the conversion of farm buildings into family homes.
  - Conversion to gas central heating will save you a lot of money.
  - No conversion from analogue to digital data is needed.
  - a metric conversion table (= showing how to change metric amounts into or out of another system)
  - a firm which specializes in house conversions (= turning large houses into several smaller flats)

### [convict](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/convict_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈvɪkt/` · **US:** `/kənˈvɪkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconvict__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconvict__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be convicted (of something) He was convicted of fraud.
  - a convicted murderer
  - convict somebody (of something) There wasn’t enough evidence to convict her.

### [conviction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conviction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈvɪkʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈvɪkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconviction__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconviction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He plans to appeal against his conviction.
  - conviction for something She has six previous convictions for theft.
  - on conviction an offence that carries, on conviction, a sentence of not more than five years’ imprisonment

### [cooperate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cooperate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊˈɒpəreɪt/` · **US:** `/kəʊˈɑːpəreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/coope/cooperate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/coope/cooperate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The two groups agreed to cooperate with each other.
  - They had cooperated closely in the planning of the project.

### [cooperative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cooperative_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊˈɒpərətɪv/` · **US:** `/kəʊˈɑːpərətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/coope/cooperative__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/coope/cooperative__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Cooperative activity is essential to effective community work.
  - The documentary was a cooperative effort by film-makers from five countries.
  - This is a cooperative venture with the University of Copenhagen.

### [coordinate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coordinate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊˈɔːdɪneɪt/` · **US:** `/kəʊˈɔːrdɪneɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/coord/coordinate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/coord/coordinate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - coordinate something They appointed a new manager to coordinate the work of the team.
  - We need to develop a coordinated approach to the problem.
  - a carefully coordinated policy
  - coordinate something with something We try to coordinate our activities with those of other groups.

### [coordination](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coordination) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊˌɔːdɪˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/kəʊˌɔːrdɪˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/coord/coordination__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/coord/coordination__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The aim was to improve the coordination of services.
  - coordination between A and B a need for greater coordination between departments
  - coordination in something a lack of coordination in conservation policy
  - in coordination with somebody/something a pamphlet produced by the government in coordination with (= working together with) the Sports Council
  - advice on colour coordination (= choosing colours that look nice together, for example in clothes or furniture)

### [coordinator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coordinator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊˈɔːdɪneɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/kəʊˈɔːrdɪneɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/coord/coordinator__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/coord/coordinator__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The campaign needs an effective coordinator.

### [cop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cop_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɒp/` · **US:** `/kɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cop/cop__/cop__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cop/cop__/cop__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Somebody call the cops!
  - children playing cops and robbers
  - a TV cop show

### [copper](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/copper) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒpə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːpər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cop/coppe/copper__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cop/coppe/copper__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a copper mine
  - copper pipes
  - copper-coloured hair

### [copyright](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/copyright_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒpiraɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːpiraɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cop/copyr/copyright__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cop/copyr/copyright__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Copyright expires seventy years after the death of the author.
  - They were sued for breach/infringement of copyright.
  - copyright in/on something Who owns the copyright on this song?
  - out of copyright His work is now out of copyright.
  - in copyright The songs remain in copyright.

### [correction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correction_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrekʃn/` · **US:** `/kəˈrekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correction__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've made a few small corrections to your report.
  - The paper had to publish a correction to the story.

### [correlate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correlate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒrəleɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɔːrəleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correlate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correlate__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The figures do not seem to correlate.
  - correlate with something A high-fat diet correlates with a greater risk of heart disease.
  - The average speed of the vehicles correlates closely with the severity of the accident caused.

### [correlation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correlation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒrəˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɔːrəˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correlation__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correlation__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - correlation between A and B There is a direct correlation between exposure to sun and skin cancer.
  - correlation of A with B the correlation of social power with wealth

### [correspond](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correspond) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒrəˈspɒnd/` · **US:** `/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correspond__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correspond__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Your account and hers do not correspond.
  - correspond with something Your account of events does not correspond with hers.
  - correspond to something The written record of the conversation doesn't correspond to (= is different from) what was actually said.

### [correspondence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correspondence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒrəˈspɒndəns/` · **US:** `/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːndəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correspondence__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correspondence__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - personal/private correspondence
  - The editor welcomes correspondence from readers on any subject.
  - the correspondence column/page (= in a newspaper)
  - correspondence with somebody Jane Austen's correspondence with her sister

### [correspondent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correspondent) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒrəˈspɒndənt/` · **US:** `/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːndənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correspondent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correspondent__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's the BBC's political correspondent.
  - a foreign/war/sports correspondent
  - our Delhi correspondent

### [corresponding](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/corresponding) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒrəˈspɒndɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːndɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/corresponding__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/corresponding__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A change in the money supply brings a corresponding change in expenditure.
  - Profits have risen by 15 per cent compared with the corresponding period last year.
  - The Redskins lost to the Cowboys in the corresponding game last year.
  - Fewer houses are available, but there is no corresponding decrease in demand.
  - corresponding to something Give each picture a number corresponding to its position on the page.

### [corrupt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/corrupt_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrʌpt/` · **US:** `/kəˈrʌpt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corru/corrupt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corru/corrupt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was seen as the only way to overthrow a corrupt regime.
  - They promised to seek out the corrupt officials who had accepted the bribes.
  - one of the most notoriously corrupt city councils

### [corruption](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/corruption) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrʌpʃn/` · **US:** `/kəˈrʌpʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corru/corruption__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corru/corruption__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - allegations of bribery and corruption
  - The new district attorney has promised to fight police corruption.

### [costly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/costly) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒstli/` · **US:** `/ˈkɔːstli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cos/costl/costly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cos/costl/costly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Buying new furniture may prove too costly.

### [councillor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/councillor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkaʊnsələ(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkaʊnsələr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/counc/councillor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/counc/councillor__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Councillor Ann Jones
  - Talk to your local councillor about the problem.

### [counselling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/counselling) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkaʊnsəlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈkaʊnsəlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/couns/counselling__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/couns/counselling__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The couple decided to go for relationship counselling.
  - Some groups offer bereavement counselling.
  - a student counselling service

### [counsellor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/counsellor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkaʊnsələ(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkaʊnsələr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/couns/counsellor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/couns/counsellor__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I went to see a debt counsellor and she agreed to come to the bank with me.

### [counter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/counter_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkaʊntə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkaʊntər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/counter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/counter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - counter somebody/something Such arguments are not easily countered.
  - counter that… I tried to argue but he countered that the plans were not yet finished.
  - counter (somebody) + speech ‘But I was standing right here!’ he countered.
  - counter (somebody/something) with something Butler has countered with a lawsuit against the firm.

### [counterpart](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/counterpart) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkaʊntəpɑːt/` · **US:** `/ˈkaʊntərpɑːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/counterpart__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/counterpart__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The Foreign Minister held talks with his Chinese counterpart.
  - The women's shoe, like its male counterpart, is specifically designed for the serious tennis player.

### [countless](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/countless) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkaʊntləs/` · **US:** `/ˈkaʊntləs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/countless__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/countless__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've warned her countless times.
  - The new treatment could save Emma's life and the lives of countless others.

### [coup](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coup) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kuː/` · **US:** `/kuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/coup_/coup__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/coup_/coup__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He seized power in a military coup in 2008.
  - to stage/mount a coup
  - He was sentenced to death for his part in the attempted coup.
  - a failed/an abortive coup
  - She lost her position in a boardroom coup (= a sudden change of power among senior managers in a company).

### [courtesy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/courtesy_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɜːtəsi/` · **US:** `/ˈkɜːrtəsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/court/courtesy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/court/courtesy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was treated with the utmost courtesy by the staff.
  - We asked them, as a matter of courtesy, if we could photograph their house.
  - It's only common courtesy to tell the neighbours that we'll be having a party (= the sort of behaviour that people would expect).

### [craft](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/craft_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/krɑːft/` · **US:** `/kræft/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cra/craft/craft__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cra/craft/craft__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be crafted (from something) All the furniture is crafted from natural materials.
  - a carefully crafted speech

### [crawl](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/crawl_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/krɔːl/` · **US:** `/krɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cra/crawl/crawl__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cra/crawl/crawl__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [creator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/creator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kriˈeɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/kriˈeɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/creat/creator__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/creat/creator__us_1.mp3)

### [credibility](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/credibility) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkredəˈbɪləti/` · **US:** `/ˌkredəˈbɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/credi/credibility__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/credi/credibility__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to gain/lack/lose credibility
  - The prosecution did its best to undermine the credibility of the witness.
  - After the recent scandal, the government has lost all credibility.
  - Newspapers were talking of a credibility gap between what he said and what he did.

### [credible](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/credible) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkredəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈkredəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/credi/credible__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/credi/credible__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - It is just not credible that she would cheat.

- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/ˈkʌltɪveɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌltɪveɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cul/culti/cultivate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cul/culti/cultivate__us_1.mp3)

### [curiosity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/curiosity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkjʊəriˈɒsəti/` · **US:** `/ˌkjʊriˈɑːsəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cur/curio/curiosity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cur/curio/curiosity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Children show curiosity about everything.
  - I felt a certain curiosity to see what would happen next.
  - The letter wasn't addressed to me but I opened it out of curiosity.
  - His answer did not satisfy my curiosity at all.
  - Sophie's curiosity was aroused by the mysterious phone call.
  - intellectual curiosity
  - ‘Why do you ask?’ ‘Oh, just idle curiosity’ (= no particular reason).

### [custody](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/custody) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌstədi/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌstədi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cus/custo/custody__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cus/custo/custody__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who will have custody of the children?
  - The parents were given joint custody of the two children.
  - The divorce court awarded custody to the child's mother.
  - The parents were locked in a bitter battle for custody.
  - The bank provides safe custody for valuables.
  - in the custody of somebody/something The castle is now in the custody of the state.

### [cutting](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cutting_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cut/cutti/cutting__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cut/cutti/cutting__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - newspaper/press cuttings

### [cynical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cynical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪnɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪnɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cyn/cynic/cynical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cyn/cynic/cynical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a cynical view/smile

## D

### [dam](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dam_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dæm/` · **US:** `/dæm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dam/dam__/dam__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dam/dam__/dam__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Narmada dam in India
  - The dam burst.
  - The dam burst and the valley was flooded.

### [damaging](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/damaging) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdæmɪdʒɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈdæmɪdʒɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dam/damag/damaging__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dam/damag/damaging__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - damaging consequences/effects
  - damaging to somebody/something Lead is potentially damaging to children's health.

### [dawn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dawn_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɔːn/` · **US:** `/dɔːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/daw/dawn_/dawn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/daw/dawn_/dawn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at dawn They start work at dawn.
  - It's almost dawn.
  - We arrived in Sydney as dawn broke (= as the first light could be seen).
  - I woke up just before dawn.
  - summer’s early dawns
  - He works from dawn till dusk (= from morning till night).
  - The plane took off, leaving London behind in a blue-grey dawn.

### [debris](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/debris) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdeɪbriː/` · **US:** `/dəˈbriː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/deb/debri/debris__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/deb/debri/debris__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Emergency teams are still clearing the debris from the plane crash.
  - Several people were injured by flying debris in the explosion.

### [debut](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/debut_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdebjuː/` · **US:** `/deɪˈbjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/deb/debut/debut__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/deb/debut/debut__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - She’s making her New York debut at Carnegie Hall.

- **UK:** `/dɪˈsɪʒn meɪkɪŋ/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsɪʒn meɪkɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decis/decision_making_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decis/decision_making_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - All the staff should be involved in the decision-making process.

### [decisive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/decisive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈsaɪsɪv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsaɪsɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decis/decisive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decis/decisive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a decisive factor/victory/battle
  - Climate was a decisive factor in establishing where the tournament should be held.
  - She has played a decisive role in the peace negotiations.
  - a decisive step (= an important action that will change a situation) towards a cleaner environment

### [declaration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/declaration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdekləˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdekləˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decla/declaration__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decla/declaration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to issue/sign a declaration
  - the declaration of war
  - the Declaration of Independence (= of the United States)
  - The declaration asked governments to consider introducing new environmental taxes.

### [dedicated](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dedicated) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdedɪkeɪtɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈdedɪkeɪtɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/ded/dedic/dedicated__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/ded/dedic/dedicated__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dedicated teacher
  - dedicated to something She is dedicated to her job.
  - dedicated to doing something The charity is dedicated to serving young people with special needs.

### [dedication](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dedication) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdedɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdedɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/ded/dedic/dedication__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/ded/dedic/dedication__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hard work and dedication
  - dedication to somebody/something I really admire Gina for her dedication to her family.

### [deed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deed_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/diːd/` · **US:** `/diːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dee/deed_/deed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dee/deed_/deed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a stirring tale of heroic deeds.
  - a brave/charitable deed
  - She tried to do a good deed every day.
  - They will be punished for their evil deeds.

### [deem](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deem) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/diːm/` · **US:** `/diːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dee/deem_/deem__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dee/deem_/deem__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - deem somebody/something + noun The evening was deemed a great success.
  - deem somebody/something + adj. She deemed it prudent not to say anything.
  - I deem it an honour to be invited.
  - They would take any action deemed necessary.
  - deem somebody/something to be something The strike was deemed to be illegal.
  - deem (that)… Inspectors deemed that the standard of teaching was unsatisfactory.

### [default](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/default_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdiːfɔːlt/` · **US:** `/ˈdiːfɔːlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defau/default__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defau/default__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The default is fifty lines.
  - The default option is to save your work every five minutes.
  - What is your default browser?
  - All my life, envy has been my default emotion.

### [defect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/defect_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdiːfekt/` · **US:** `/ˈdiːfekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defec/defect__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defec/defect__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a defect in the glass

### [defensive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/defensive_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈfensɪv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈfensɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defen/defensive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defen/defensive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As a defensive measure he built a series of coastal forts and watchtowers.
  - These are purely defensive measures.
  - Troops took up a defensive position around the town.

### [deficiency](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deficiency) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈfɪʃnsi/` · **US:** `/dɪˈfɪʃnsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defic/deficiency__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defic/deficiency__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - deficiency (in something) Vitamin deficiency in the diet can cause illness.
  - deficiency of something a deficiency of Vitamin B

### [deficit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deficit) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdefɪsɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈdefɪsɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defic/deficit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defic/deficit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a budget/trade deficit

### [defy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/defy) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈfaɪ/` · **US:** `/dɪˈfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defy_/defy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defy_/defy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Hundreds of people today defied the ban on political gatherings.

### [delegate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/delegate_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdelɪɡət/` · **US:** `/ˈdelɪɡət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/del/deleg/delegate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/del/deleg/delegate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Congress delegates rejected the proposals.

### [delegation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/delegation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdelɪˈɡeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdelɪˈɡeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/del/deleg/delegation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/del/deleg/delegation__us_1.mp3)

### [delicate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/delicate) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdelɪkət/` · **US:** `/ˈdelɪkət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/del/delic/delicate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/del/delic/delicate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - delicate china teacups
  - The eye is one of the most delicate organs of the body.
  - the delicate ecological balance of the rainforest
  - Babies have very delicate skin.
  - a cool wash cycle for delicate fabrics

### [demon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/demon) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdiːmən/` · **US:** `/ˈdiːmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dem/demon/demon__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dem/demon/demon__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The people believed the girl was possessed by demons.

### [denial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/denial) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈnaɪəl/` · **US:** `/dɪˈnaɪəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/den/denia/denial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/den/denia/denial__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - denial (of something) the prisoner’s repeated denials of the charges against him
  - The terrorists issued a denial of responsibility for the attack.
  - denial that… There was an official denial that there would be an election before the end of the year.
  - in denial She shook her head in denial.

### [denounce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/denounce) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈnaʊns/` · **US:** `/dɪˈnaʊns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/den/denou/denounce__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/den/denou/denounce__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - denounce somebody/something She publicly denounced the government's handling of the crisis.
  - denounce somebody/something as something The project was denounced as a scandalous waste of public money.

### [dense](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dense) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dens/` · **US:** `/dens/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/den/dense/dense__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/den/dense/dense__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dense crowd/forest
  - areas of dense population
  - The forest is quite dense at that point.

### [density](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/density) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdensəti/` · **US:** `/ˈdensəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/den/densi/density__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/den/densi/density__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The population density in this city is very high.
  - low density forest
  - an average density of 2.4 people per hectare

### [dependence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dependence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpendəns/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpendəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depen/dependence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depen/dependence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our relationship was based on mutual dependence.
  - financial/economic dependence
  - dependence on/upon somebody/something his dependence on his parents
  - the dependence of Europe on imported foods

### [depict](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/depict) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpɪkt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpɪkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depic/depict__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depic/depict__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - depict somebody/something (as somebody/something) a painting depicting the Virgin and Child
  - depict somebody/something doing something The artist had depicted her lying on a bed.

### [deploy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deploy) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈplɔɪ/` · **US:** `/dɪˈplɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/deplo/deploy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/deplo/deploy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - At least 5 000 missiles were deployed along the border.
  - Tanks were deployed effectively during the long campaign.

### [deployment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deployment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈplɔɪmənt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈplɔɪmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/deplo/deployment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/deplo/deployment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - troop deployments

### [deposit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deposit_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpɒzɪt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpɑːzɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depos/deposit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depos/deposit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - You can withdraw and deposit money in any of our branches.

### [deprive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deprive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpraɪv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpraɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depri/deprive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depri/deprive__us_1.mp3)

### [deputy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deputy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdepjuti/` · **US:** `/ˈdepjuti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/deput/deputy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/deput/deputy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [descend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/descend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈsend/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desce/descend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desce/descend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The plane began to descend.
  - The results, ranked in descending order (= from the highest to the lowest) are as follows…
  - descend something She descended the stairs slowly.

### [descent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/descent) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈsent/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desce/descent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desce/descent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The plane began its descent to Heathrow.
  - (figurative) the country’s swift descent into anarchy

### [designate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/designate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdezɪɡneɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈdezɪɡneɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desig/designate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desig/designate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be designated (as) something This area has been designated (as) a National Park.
  - The school has been designated a specialist science academy.
  - be designated (as being/having something) Several pupils were designated as having moderate or severe learning difficulties.
  - a designated nature reserve
  - designated seats for the elderly

### [desirable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/desirable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈzaɪərəbl/` · **US:** `/dɪˈzaɪərəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desir/desirable__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desir/desirable__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She chatted for a few minutes about the qualities she considered desirable in a secretary.
  - Such measures are desirable, if not essential.
  - The house has many desirable features.
  - Experience of computers is highly desirable.
  - It is desirable that… (British English) It is desirable that interest rates should be reduced.
  - (especially North American English) It is desirable that interest rates be reduced.
  - It is desirable (for somebody) (to do something) It is no longer desirable for such young children to take formal written tests.

### [desktop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/desktop) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdesktɒp/` · **US:** `/ˈdesktɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/deskt/desktop__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/deskt/desktop__us_1.mp3)
