  - desktop icons

### [destructive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/destructive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstrʌktɪv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstrʌktɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/destr/destructive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/destr/destructive__us_1.mp3)

### [detection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/detection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈtekʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈtekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/det/detec/detection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/det/detec/detection__us_1.mp3)

### [detention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/detention) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈtenʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈtenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/det/deten/detention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/det/deten/detention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [deteriorate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deteriorate) *(verb)*

### [devastate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/devastate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdevəsteɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈdevəsteɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dev/devas/devastate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dev/devas/devastate__us_1.mp3)

### [devil](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/devil) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdevl/` · **US:** `/ˈdevl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dev/devil/devil__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dev/devil/devil__us_1.mp3)

  - His strong left-wing views make him the devil incarnate to more extreme Conservatives.

### [devise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/devise) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈvaɪz/` · **US:** `/dɪˈvaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dev/devis/devise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dev/devis/devise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A new system has been devised to control traffic in the city.

### [diagnose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diagnose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdaɪəɡˈnəʊz/` · **US:** `/ˌdaɪəɡˈnəʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dia/diagn/diagnose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dia/diagn/diagnose__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - diagnose something The test is used to diagnose a variety of diseases.
  - diagnose something as something The illness was diagnosed as cancer.
  - diagnose somebody with something He has recently been diagnosed with angina.
  - diagnose somebody/something (as) something I quickly diagnosed the problem as a faulty battery.
  - He was diagnosed (as) a diabetic when he was 64.
  - diagnose somebody + adj./noun He was diagnosed (a) diabetic.

### [diagnosis](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diagnosis) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdaɪəɡˈnəʊsɪs/` · **US:** `/ˌdaɪəɡˈnəʊsɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dia/diagn/diagnosis__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dia/diagn/diagnosis__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a diagnosis of lung cancer
  - They are waiting for the doctor's diagnosis.
  - An accurate diagnosis was made after a series of tests.
  - They are using new methods of diagnosis.

### [dictate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dictate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪkˈteɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪkteɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dic/dicta/dictate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dic/dicta/dictate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - dictate something (to somebody) They are in no position to dictate terms (= tell other people what to do).
  - dictate how, what, etc…/that… What right do they have to dictate how we live our lives?

### [dictator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dictator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪkˈteɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪkteɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dic/dicta/dictator__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dic/dicta/dictator__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The country suffered at the hands of a series of military dictators.

### [differentiate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/differentiate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdɪfəˈrenʃieɪt/` · **US:** `/ˌdɪfəˈrenʃieɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dif/diffe/differentiate__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dif/diffe/differentiate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - differentiate (between) A and B It's difficult to differentiate between the two varieties.
  - differentiate A (from B) I can't differentiate one variety from another.

### [dignity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dignity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪɡnəti/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪɡnəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dig/digni/dignity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dig/digni/dignity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She accepted the criticism with quiet dignity.
  - He brings a quiet dignity to the role.
  - His aristocratic voice gives him an air of dignity and power.

### [dilemma](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dilemma) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/daɪˈlemə/` · **US:** `/daɪˈlemə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dil/dilem/dilemma__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dil/dilem/dilemma__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I could see no way of resolving this moral dilemma.
  - The digital era brings new ethical dilemmas for journalists.
  - in a dilemma They were caught in a real dilemma.
  - dilemma about/over something She faced a dilemma about whether to accept the offer or not.
  - dilemma between A and B the perennial dilemma between work and family commitments

### [dimension](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dimension) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈmenʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈmenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dim/dimen/dimension__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dim/dimen/dimension__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We measured the dimensions of the kitchen.
  - computer design tools that work in three dimensions

### [diminish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diminish) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/` · **US:** `/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dim/dimin/diminish__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dim/dimin/diminish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The world's resources are rapidly diminishing.
  - His influence has diminished with time.
  - diminish something The new law is expected to diminish the government's chances.

### [dip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dip_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪp/` · **US:** `/dɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dip/dip__/dip__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dip/dip__/dip__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - dip something (into something) He dipped the brush into the paint.
  - dip something (in) Dip your hand in to see how hot the water is.
  - The fruit had been dipped in chocolate.

### [diplomat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diplomat) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪpləmæt/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪpləmæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dip/diplo/diplomat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dip/diplo/diplomat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Washington's top diplomat in Havana
  - a Western diplomat in Islamabad

### [diplomatic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diplomatic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdɪpləˈmætɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌdɪpləˈmætɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dip/diplo/diplomatic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dip/diplo/diplomatic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a diplomatic crisis
  - Attempts are being made to settle the dispute by diplomatic means.
  - to break off/establish/restore diplomatic relations with a country

### [directory](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/directory) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/daɪˈrektəri/` · **US:** `/daɪˈrektəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dir/direc/directory__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dir/direc/directory__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a telephone/trade directory
  - a directory of European Trade Associations

### [disastrous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disastrous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈzɑːstrəs/` · **US:** `/dɪˈzæstrəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disas/disastrous__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disas/disastrous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a disastrous harvest/fire/result
  - Lowering interest rates could have disastrous consequences for the economy.
  - It was a disastrous start to the season for the team.

### [discard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/discard_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈskɑːd/` · **US:** `/dɪˈskɑːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disca/discard__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disca/discard__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - discard somebody/something The room was littered with discarded newspapers.
  - He had discarded his jacket because of the heat.
  - (figurative) She could now discard all thought of promotion.
  - discard somebody/something as something 10 per cent of the data was discarded as unreliable.

### [discharge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/discharge_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈtʃɑːdʒ/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈtʃɑːrdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disch/discharge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disch/discharge__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be discharged from something He was discharged from the army following his injury.
  - She was discharged from the police force for bad conduct.
  - He was found guilty and dishonourably discharged from the army.

### [disclose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disclose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈkləʊz/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈkləʊz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/discl/disclose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/discl/disclose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - disclose something (to somebody) The spokesman refused to disclose details of the takeover to the press.
  - disclose that… The report discloses that human error was to blame for the accident.
  - it is disclosed that… It was disclosed that two women were being interviewed by the police.
  - disclose what, whether, etc… I cannot disclose what we discussed.

### [disclosure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disclosure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈkləʊʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈkləʊʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/discl/disclosure__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/discl/disclosure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the newspaper’s disclosure of defence secrets
  - The bank will need full disclosure of your financial situation and assets.
  - The court ruled to prevent public disclosure of the contents of the documents.

### [discourse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/discourse_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪskɔːs/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪskɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disco/discourse__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disco/discourse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - discourse on something a discourse on issues of gender and sexuality
  - He was hoping for some lively political discourse at the meeting.

### [discretion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/discretion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈskreʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈskreʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/discr/discretion__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/discr/discretion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll leave it up to you to use your discretion.
  - How much to tell terminally ill patients is left to the discretion of the doctor.

### [discrimination](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/discrimination) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˌskrɪmɪˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˌskrɪmɪˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/discr/discrimination__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/discr/discrimination__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - age/racial/gender/sex discrimination (= because of somebody’s age, race or sex)
  - discrimination against somebody discrimination against the elderly
  - discrimination in favour of somebody discrimination in favour of the young
  - discrimination by somebody They alleged discrimination by the authorities.
  - discrimination on the basis of something to prohibit/outlaw/ban discrimination on the basis of race, gender or sexual orientation
  - discrimination on the grounds of something legislation to ban discrimination on the grounds of age

### [dismissal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dismissal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈmɪsl/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈmɪsl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/dismi/dismissal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/dismi/dismissal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He still hopes to win his claim against unfair dismissal.
  - The dismissals followed the resignation of the chairman.

### [displace](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/displace) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈpleɪs/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈpleɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/displ/displace__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/displ/displace__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be displaced (by somebody/something) Gradually factory workers have been displaced by machines.

### [disposal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disposal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈspəʊzl/` · **US:** `/dɪˈspəʊzl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/dispo/disposal__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/dispo/disposal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The council is responsible for waste disposal and street cleaning.
  - sewage disposal systems
  - the problems of the ultimate disposal of nuclear waste

### [dispose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dispose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈspəʊz/` · **US:** `/dɪˈspəʊz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/dispo/dispose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/dispo/dispose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The visitors disposed themselves in a circle round the statue.

### [dispute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dispute_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪspjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪspjuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/dispu/dispute__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/dispu/dispute__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - industrial/pay disputes
  - dispute between A and B a dispute between the two countries about the border
  - dispute over/about something the latest dispute over fishing rights
  - in dispute with somebody/something The union is in dispute with management over working hours.
  - in/under dispute The cause of the accident was still in dispute (= being argued about).
  - These are not the issues under dispute.
  - beyond dispute The matter was settled beyond dispute by the court judgment (= it could no longer be argued about).
  - open to dispute His theories are open to dispute (= can be disagreed with).

### [dispute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dispute_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈspjuːt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈspjuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/dispu/dispute__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/dispu/dispute__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - dispute something These figures have been disputed.
  - to dispute a decision/claim
  - The family wanted to dispute the will.
  - dispute that… No one is disputing that there is a problem.
  - dispute whether, how, etc… | it is disputed whether, how, etc… It is disputed whether the law applies in this case.

### [disrupt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disrupt) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈrʌpt/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈrʌpt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disru/disrupt__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disru/disrupt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Demonstrators succeeded in disrupting the meeting.
  - Bus services will be disrupted tomorrow because of the bridge closure.

### [disruption](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disruption) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪsˈrʌpʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪsˈrʌpʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disru/disruption__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disru/disruption__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We aim to help you move house with minimum disruption to yourself.
  - disruptions to rail services
  - The strike caused serious disruptions.

### [dissolve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dissolve) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈzɒlv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈzɑːlv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disso/dissolve__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disso/dissolve__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Salt dissolves in water.
  - Heat gently until the sugar dissolves.
  - The aspirin hasn't dissolved completely yet.

### [distinction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/distinction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstɪŋkʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstɪŋkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disti/distinction__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disti/distinction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - distinctions between traditional and modern societies
  - Philosophers did not use to make a distinction between arts and science.
  - We need to draw a distinction between the two events.

### [distinctive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/distinctive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstɪŋktɪv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstɪŋktɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disti/distinctive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disti/distinctive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - clothes with a distinctive style
  - The male bird has distinctive white markings on its head.

### [distort](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/distort) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstɔːt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/disto/distort__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/disto/distort__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fairground mirror that distorts your shape
  - The loudspeaker seemed to distort his voice.

### [distress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/distress_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstres/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/distr/distress__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/distr/distress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The newspaper article caused the actor considerable distress.
  - She sensed his deep emotional distress.
  - in distress She was obviously in distress after the attack.

### [distress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/distress_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstres/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/distr/distress__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/distr/distress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - distress somebody It was clear that the letter had deeply distressed her.
  - distress yourself Don't distress yourself (= don't worry).

### [disturbing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/disturbing) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstɜːbɪŋ/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstɜːrbɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/distu/disturbing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/distu/disturbing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a disturbing piece of news
  - a profoundly disturbing experience

### [divert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/divert) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/daɪˈvɜːt/` · **US:** `/daɪˈvɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/div/diver/divert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/div/diver/divert__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be diverted The course of the stream has now been diverted.
  - be diverted (from something) (to/into/onto something) Northbound traffic will have to be diverted onto minor roads.

### [divine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/divine_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈvaɪn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈvaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/div/divin/divine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/div/divin/divine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - divine law/love/will
  - divine intervention (= help from God to change a situation)

### [doctrine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/doctrine) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɒktrɪn/` · **US:** `/ˈdɑːktrɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/doc/doctr/doctrine__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/doc/doctr/doctrine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the doctrine of parliamentary sovereignty
  - He challenged the Christian doctrine of Original Sin.
  - He was deeply committed to political doctrines of social equality.

### [documentation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/documentation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdɒkjumenˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdɑːkjumenˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/doc/docum/documentation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/doc/docum/documentation__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I couldn't enter the country because I didn't have all the necessary documentation.

### [domain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/domain) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dəʊˈmeɪn/` · **US:** `/dəʊˈmeɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dom/domai/domain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dom/domai/domain__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Financial matters are her domain.
  - Physics used to be very much a male domain.
  - things that happen outside the domain of the home

### [dominance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dominance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɒmɪnəns/` · **US:** `/ˈdɑːmɪnəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dom/domin/dominance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dom/domin/dominance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - political/economic dominance
  - America's rise to global dominance
  - dominance over somebody/something to achieve/assert/establish dominance over somebody

### [donor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/donor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdəʊnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdəʊnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/don/donor/donor__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/don/donor/donor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - international aid donors (= countries that give money, etc. to help other countries)
  - She is one of the charity's main donors.

### [dose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dose_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dəʊs/` · **US:** `/dəʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dos/dose_/dose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dos/dose_/dose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a high/low/lethal dose
  - Repeat the dose after 12 hours if necessary.
  - Take a single dose at bedtime to help you sleep.
  - I always take my daily dose of vitamin C.

### [drain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/drain_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dreɪn/` · **US:** `/dreɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dra/drain/drain__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dra/drain/drain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Drain and rinse the pasta.
  - The marshes have been drained.
  - You will need to drain the central heating system before you replace the radiator.
  - The swimming pool drains very slowly.
  - Leave the dishes to drain.

### [drift](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/drift_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/drɪft/` · **US:** `/drɪft/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dri/drift/drift__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dri/drift/drift__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Clouds drifted across the sky.
  - The empty boat drifted out to sea.
  - A cool breeze drifted through the open window.
  - Smoke drifted across the room.

### [driving](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/driving_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdraɪvɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈdraɪvɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dri/drivi/driving__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dri/drivi/driving__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who was the driving force (= the person with the strongest influence) in the band?
  - She only got where she is today through hard work and a driving ambition.

### [drown](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/drown) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/draʊn/` · **US:** `/draʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dro/drown/drown__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dro/drown/drown__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Two children drowned after falling into the river.
  - He had attempted to rescue the drowning man.
  - drown somebody/something/yourself She tried to drown herself.
  - They had drowned the unwanted kittens.
  - be drowned He was drowned at sea.

### [dual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dual) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdjuːəl/` · **US:** `/ˈduːəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dua/dual_/dual__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dua/dual_/dual__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - his dual role as composer and conductor
  - She has dual nationality (= is a citizen of two different countries).
  - The piece of furniture serves a dual purpose as a cupboard and as a table.

### [dub](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dub_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dʌb/` · **US:** `/dʌb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dub/dub__/dub__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dub/dub__/dub__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The media dubbed anorexia ‘the slimming disease’.
  - Genetically modified products have been dubbed ‘Frankenfoods’ in the popular press.

### [dumb](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dumb_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dʌm/` · **US:** `/dʌm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dum/dumb_/dumb__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dum/dumb_/dumb__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That was a pretty dumb thing to do.
  - If the police question you, act dumb (= pretend you do not know anything).
  - (offensive) In her early movies she played a dumb blonde.

### [duo](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/duo) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdjuːəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈduːəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/duo/duo__/duo__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/duo/duo__/duo__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the comedy duo Laurel and Hardy
  - the goal-scoring duo of Coates and Winterbotham

### [dynamic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dynamic_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/daɪˈnæmɪk/` · **US:** `/daɪˈnæmɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dyn/dynam/dynamic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dyn/dynam/dynamic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the dynamics of political change
  - group dynamics (= the way in which members of a group react to each other)
  - Market dynamics are working in the company’s favour.


## E

### [eager](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eager) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈiːɡə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈiːɡər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eag/eager/eager__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eag/eager/eager__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - eager crowds outside the stadium
  - Small eager faces looked up and listened.
  - eager for something She is eager for (= wants very much to get) her parents' approval.
  - eager to do something Everyone in the class seemed eager to learn.
  - They're eager to please (= wanting to be helpful).

### [earnings](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/earnings) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɜːnɪŋz/` · **US:** `/ˈɜːrnɪŋz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ear/earni/earnings__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ear/earni/earnings__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a rise in average earnings
  - She is claiming compensation for loss of earnings.

### [ease](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ease_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/iːz/` · **US:** `/iːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eas/ease_/ease__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eas/ease_/ease__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - with ease He passed the exam with ease.
  - The ease with which she learns languages is astonishing.
  - for ease of something All important points are numbered for ease of reference (= so that you can find them easily).
  - This computer is popular for its good design and ease of use.

### [ease](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ease_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/iːz/` · **US:** `/iːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eas/ease_/ease__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eas/ease_/ease__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The pain immediately eased.
  - ease something This should help ease the pain.
  - The plan should ease traffic congestion in the town.
  - It would ease my mind (= make me less worried) to know that she was settled.

### [echo](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/echo_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈekəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈekəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ech/echo_/echo__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ech/echo_/echo__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There was an echo on the phone and I couldn't hear clearly.
  - The hills sent back a faint echo.
  - the echo of footsteps running down the corridor
  - ‘So you love him, do you?’ Magda’s voice was a mocking echo of my own.

### [echo](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/echo_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈekəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈekəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ech/echo_/echo__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ech/echo_/echo__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her footsteps echoed in the empty room.
  - The gunshot echoed through the forest.

### [ecological](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ecological) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌiːkəˈlɒdʒɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˌiːkəˈlɑːdʒɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eco/ecolo/ecological__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eco/ecolo/ecological__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We risk upsetting the ecological balance of the area.
  - an ecological disaster (= one that alters the whole balance of ecology in an area)

### [educator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/educator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈedʒukeɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈedʒukeɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/edu/educa/educator__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/edu/educa/educator__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - adult educators (= who teach adults)

### [effectiveness](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/effectiveness) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈfektɪvnəs/` · **US:** `/ɪˈfektɪvnəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eff/effec/effectiveness_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eff/effec/effectiveness__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to check the effectiveness of the security system

### [efficiency](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/efficiency) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈfɪʃnsi/` · **US:** `/ɪˈfɪʃnsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eff/effic/efficiency__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eff/effic/efficiency__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - improvements in efficiency at the factory
  - I was impressed by the efficiency with which she handled the crisis.

### [ego](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ego) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɡəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈiːɡəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ego/ego__/ego__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ego/ego__/ego__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has the biggest ego of anyone I've ever met.
  - Winning the prize really boosted her ego.

### [elaborate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/elaborate_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈlæbərət/` · **US:** `/ɪˈlæbərət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ela/elabo/elaborate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ela/elabo/elaborate__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - elaborate designs
  - She had prepared a very elaborate meal.
  - an elaborate computer system

### [electoral](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/electoral) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈlektərəl/` · **US:** `/ɪˈlektərəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ele/elect/electoral__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ele/elect/electoral__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - electoral systems/reforms

### [elevate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/elevate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈelɪveɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈelɪveɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ele/eleva/elevate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ele/eleva/elevate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - elevate somebody/something (to something) He elevated many of his friends to powerful positions within the government.
  - elevate something (into/to something) It was an attempt to elevate football to a subject worthy of serious study.
  - He has elevated bad taste into an art form.

### [eligible](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eligible) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈelɪdʒəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈelɪdʒəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eli/eligi/eligible__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eli/eligi/eligible__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - eligible (for something) Only those over 70 are eligible for the special payment.
  - The new regulations made a lot of prisoners eligible for early release.
  - You are not considered eligible for legal aid.
  - eligible (to do something) When are you eligible to vote in your country?

### [elite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/elite_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈliːt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈliːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eli/elite/elite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eli/elite/elite__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a member of the ruling/intellectual elite
  - Public opinion is influenced by the small elite who control the media.
  - In these countries, only the elite can afford an education for their children.

### [embark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/embark) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈbɑːk/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈbɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emb/embar/embark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emb/embar/embark__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We stood on the pier and watched as they embarked.
  - embark somebody/something They embarked the troops by night.

### [embarrassment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/embarrassment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈbærəsmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈbærəsmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emb/embar/embarrassment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emb/embar/embarrassment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I nearly died of embarrassment when he said that.
  - embarrassment of (doing) something I'm glad you offered—it saved me the embarrassment of having to ask.
  - to somebody's embarrassment Much to her embarrassment, she realized that everybody had been listening to her singing.

### [embassy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/embassy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈembəsi/` · **US:** `/ˈembəsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emb/embas/embassy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emb/embas/embassy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - embassy officials
  - to inform the embassy of the situation

### [embed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/embed) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈbed/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈbed/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emb/embed/embed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emb/embed/embed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be embedded in something an operation to remove glass that was embedded in his leg
  - (figurative) These attitudes are deeply embedded in our society (= felt very strongly and difficult to change).
  - embed something/itself (in something) The bullet embedded itself in the wall.

### [embody](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/embody) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈbɒdi/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈbɑːdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emb/embod/embody__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emb/embod/embody__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - embody something a politician who embodied the hopes of black youth
  - be embodied in something the principles embodied in the Declaration of Human Rights

### [emergence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/emergence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈmɜːdʒəns/` · **US:** `/ɪˈmɜːrdʒəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eme/emerg/emergence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eme/emerg/emergence__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the island’s emergence from the sea 3 000 years ago
  - Albania's emergence from its long period of isolation

### [empirical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/empirical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpɪrɪkl/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpɪrɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emp/empir/empirical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emp/empir/empirical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - empirical evidence/knowledge/research
  - an empirical study

### [empower](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/empower) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/emp/empow/empower__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/emp/empow/empower__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be empowered (to do something) The courts were empowered to impose the death sentence for certain crimes.

### [enact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enact) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈnækt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈnækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ena/enact/enact__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ena/enact/enact__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (be) enacted (by somebody/something) legislation enacted by parliament

### [encompass](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/encompass) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkʌmpəs/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkʌmpəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enc/encom/encompass__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enc/encom/encompass__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The job encompasses a wide range of responsibilities.
  - The group encompasses all ages.

### [encouragement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/encouragement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkʌrɪdʒmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enc/encou/encouragement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enc/encou/encouragement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a few words of encouragement
  - He needs all the support and encouragement he can get.
  - With a little encouragement from his parents he should do well.
  - encouragement (to somebody) (to do something) She was given every encouragement to try something new.
  - Her words were a great encouragement to them.

### [encouraging](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/encouraging) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkʌrɪdʒɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enc/encou/encouraging__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enc/encou/encouraging__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This month's unemployment figures are not very encouraging.
  - You could try being a little more encouraging!
  - Despite the encouraging findings, we remain cautious.

### [endeavour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/endeavour_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdevə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/endea/endeavour__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/endea/endeavour__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There have been great advances in the field of scientific endeavour.
  - endeavour to do something Please make every endeavour to arrive on time.
  - The manager is expected to use his or her best endeavours to promote the artist's career.
  - in an endeavour to do something The public bombarded the company with complaints in an endeavour to have the price increases revoked.

### [endless](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/endless) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈendləs/` · **US:** `/ˈendləs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/endle/endless__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/endle/endless__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - endless patience
  - endless opportunities for making money
  - The possibilities are endless.
  - an endless list of things to do
  - We don't have an endless supply of money, you know.
  - I've had enough of their endless arguing.

### [endorse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/endorse) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdɔːs/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/endor/endorse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/endor/endorse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I wholeheartedly endorse his remarks.
  - Members of all parties endorsed a ban on land mines.

### [endorsement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/endorsement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdɔːsmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdɔːrsmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/endor/endorsement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/endor/endorsement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The election victory is a clear endorsement of their policies.
  - a letter of endorsement
  - This is hardly a ringing endorsement of the plan.

### [endure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/endure) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdjʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdʊr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/endur/endure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/endur/endure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - endure something They had to endure a long wait before the case came to trial.
  - She could not endure the thought of parting.
  - The pain was almost too great to endure.
  - (formal) a love that endures all things and never fails
  - He had to endure the racist taunts of the crowd.
  - endure doing something He can't endure being defeated.
  - endure to do something He can't endure to be defeated.

### [enforce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enforce) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈfɔːs/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈfɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enf/enfor/enforce__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enf/enfor/enforce__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - enforce something It's the job of the police to enforce the law.
  - The legislation will be difficult to enforce.
  - United Nations troops enforced a ceasefire in the area.
  - enforce something against/on somebody/something (formal or law) The creditor should be at liberty to enforce his rights against the company's property.

### [enforcement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enforcement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈfɔːsmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈfɔːrsmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enf/enfor/enforcement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enf/enfor/enforcement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - strict enforcement of regulations
  - law enforcement officers

### [engagement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/engagement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eng/engag/engagement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eng/engag/engagement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Their engagement was announced in the local paper.
  - engagement (to somebody) She has broken off her engagement to Charles.
  - an engagement party
  - a long/short engagement
  - He announced his engagement to his long-time girlfriend.

### [engaging](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/engaging) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eng/engag/engaging__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eng/engag/engaging__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an engaging smile

### [enquire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enquire) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkwaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkwaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enq/enqui/enquire__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enq/enqui/enquire__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - enquire about somebody/something I called the garage to enquire about progress on the repairs.
  - enquire as to somebody/something She enquired as to your whereabouts.
  - enquire why, where, etc… Might I enquire why you have not mentioned this until now?
  - enquire something He enquired her name.
  - + speech ‘What is your name?’ he enquired.

### [enrich](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enrich) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈrɪtʃ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈrɪtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enr/enric/enrich__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enr/enric/enrich__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The study of science has enriched all our lives.

### [enrol](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enrol) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈrəʊl/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈrəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enr/enrol/enrol__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enr/enrol/enrol__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You need to enrol before the end of August.
  - (British English) to enrol on a course
  - (North American English) to enroll in a course
  - enrol somebody The centre will soon be ready to enrol candidates for the new programme.

### [ensue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ensue) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈsjuː/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈsuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ens/ensue/ensue__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ens/ensue/ensue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - An argument ensued.
  - The riot police swooped in and chaos ensued.

### [enterprise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enterprise) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈentəpraɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈentərpraɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ent/enter/enterprise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ent/enter/enterprise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He is in charge of an enterprise with a turnover of $26 billion.
  - state-owned/public enterprises
  - The grant is available to small and medium-sized enterprises.

### [enthusiast](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enthusiast) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈθjuːziæst/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈθuːziæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ent/enthu/enthusiast__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ent/enthu/enthusiast__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a football enthusiast
  - enthusiast of something an enthusiast of jazz
  - enthusiast for something She was an enthusiast for early Italian art.

### [entitle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/entitle) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtaɪtl/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtaɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ent/entit/entitle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ent/entit/entitle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be entitled to something You will be entitled to your pension when you reach 65.
  - Everyone's entitled to their own opinion.
  - entitle somebody to do something This ticket does not entitle you to travel first class.

### [entity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/entity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈentəti/` · **US:** `/ˈentəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ent/entit/entity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ent/entit/entity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The unit has become part of a larger department and no longer exists as a separate entity.
  - These countries can no longer be viewed as a single entity.
  - Hip hop is a major commercial entity now (= it is a business, not just a type of music).
  - A corporation is a separate legal entity and it shields you from personal liability for its debts.

### [epidemic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/epidemic_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌepɪˈdemɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌepɪˈdemɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/epi/epide/epidemic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/epi/epide/epidemic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the outbreak of a flu epidemic
  - An epidemic of measles broke out, and over 200 children died.
  - Effectively, tobacco companies will be exporting an epidemic of smoking-related diseases, the campaign suggests.

### [equality](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/equality) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/iˈkwɒləti/` · **US:** `/iˈkwɑːləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/equ/equal/equality__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/equ/equal/equality__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - racial/social/gender equality
  - We need to ensure equality of opportunity in all areas of work.
  - the principle of equality before the law (= the law treats everyone the same)
  - Don't you believe in equality between men and women?

### [equation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/equation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈkweɪʒn/` · **US:** `/ɪˈkweɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/equ/equat/equation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/equ/equat/equation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the numbers on the right-hand side of the equation
  - the equation for a straight line

### [erect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/erect_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈrekt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈrekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ere/erect/erect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ere/erect/erect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The church was erected in 1582.

### [escalate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/escalate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeskəleɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈeskəleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/esc/escal/escalate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/esc/escal/escalate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the escalating costs of healthcare
  - escalate into something The fighting escalated into a full-scale war.
  - escalate something (into something) We do not want to escalate the war.

### [essence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/essence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈesns/` · **US:** `/ˈesns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ess/essen/essence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ess/essen/essence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His paintings capture the essence of France.
  - in essence In essence (= when you consider the most important points), your situation isn't so different from mine.

### [establishment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/establishment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈstæblɪʃmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈstæblɪʃmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/est/estab/establishment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/est/estab/establishment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The visa is for foreign nationals wishing to study at a university, college or similar educational establishment.
  - She's now running a small government research establishment.
  - The hotel is a comfortable and well-run establishment.

### [eternal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eternal) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈtɜːnl/` · **US:** `/ɪˈtɜːrnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ete/etern/eternal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ete/etern/eternal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the promise of eternal life in heaven
  - She's an eternal optimist (= she always expects that the best will happen).
  - eternal truths (= ideas that are always true and never change)
  - You have my eternal gratitude (= very great gratitude).
  - To his eternal credit (= very great credit), he never gave up.
  - Newspapers are simply responding to the eternal fascination of their readers with the private lives of the rich and famous.

### [evacuate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/evacuate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈvækjueɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈvækjueɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eva/evacu/evacuate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eva/evacu/evacuate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - evacuate something Police evacuated nearby buildings.
  - evacuate somebody (from…) (to…) Children were evacuated from London to escape the bombing.
  - Families were evacuated to safer parts of the city.

### [evoke](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/evoke) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈvəʊk/` · **US:** `/ɪˈvəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/evo/evoke/evoke__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/evo/evoke/evoke__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The music evoked memories of her youth.
  - His case is unlikely to evoke public sympathy.

### [evolutionary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/evolutionary) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌevəˈluːʃənri/` · **US:** `/ˌevəˈluːʃəneri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/evo/evolu/evolutionary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/evo/evolu/evolutionary__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - evolutionary theory
  - evolutionary change

### [exaggerate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exaggerate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪɡˈzædʒəreɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪɡˈzædʒəreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exa/exagg/exaggerate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exa/exagg/exaggerate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The hotel was really filthy and I'm not exaggerating.
  - exaggerate something He tends to exaggerate the difficulties.
  - I'm sure he exaggerates his Irish accent (= tries to sound more Irish than he really is).
  - Demand for the product has been greatly exaggerated.

### [excellence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/excellence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeksələns/` · **US:** `/ˈeksələns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/excel/excellence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/excel/excellence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a reputation for academic excellence
  - excellence in something The hospital is recognized as a centre of excellence in research and teaching.

### [exceptional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exceptional) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈsepʃənl/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈsepʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/excep/exceptional__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/excep/exceptional__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - At the age of five he showed exceptional talent as a musician.
  - The quality of the recording is quite exceptional.

### [excess](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/excess_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈekses/` · **US:** `/ˈekses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/exces/excess__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/exces/excess__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Excess food is stored as fat.
  - Driving with excess alcohol in the blood is a serious offence.
  - The book is hard to obtain, because of excess demand following the author’s appearance on TV.

### [excess](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/excess_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈses/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/exces/excess__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/exces/excess__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You can throw away any excess.
  - excess of something Are you suffering from an excess of stress in your life?
  - In an excess of enthusiasm I agreed to work late.
  - to excess He started drinking to excess after losing his job.
  - in excess of something The increase will not be in excess of (= more than) two per cent.

### [exclusion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exclusion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈskluːʒn/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈskluːʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/exclu/exclusion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/exclu/exclusion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - exclusion (of somebody/something) (from something) He was disappointed with his exclusion from the England squad.
  - Exclusion of air creates a vacuum in the bottle.
  - to the exclusion of something Memories of the past filled her mind to the exclusion of all else.

### [exclusive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exclusive_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈskluːsɪv/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈskluːsɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/exclu/exclusive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/exclu/exclusive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The hotel has exclusive access to the beach.
  - exclusive rights to televise the World Cup
  - His mother has told ‘The Times’ about his death in an exclusive interview (= not given to any other newspaper).

### [exclusively](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exclusively) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈskluːsɪvli/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈskluːsɪvli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/exclu/exclusively__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/exclu/exclusively__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The resort caters almost exclusively for a high-society public.
  - Some products are labelled exclusively for indoor or outdoor use.

### [execute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/execute) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeksɪkjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈeksɪkjuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exe/execu/execute__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exe/execu/execute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be executed (for something) He was executed for treason.
  - be executed by something The prisoners were executed by firing squad.

### [execution](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/execution) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌeksɪˈkjuːʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌeksɪˈkjuːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exe/execu/execution__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exe/execu/execution__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He faced execution by hanging for murder.
  - Over 200 executions were carried out last year.

### [exert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exert) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪɡˈzɜːt/` · **US:** `/ɪɡˈzɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exe/exert/exert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exe/exert/exert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He exerted all his authority to make them accept the plan.
  - The moon exerts a force on the earth that causes the tides.

### [exile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exile_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɡzaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈeɡzaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exi/exile/exile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exi/exile/exile__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a place of exile
  - He returned after 40 years of exile.
  - in exile He has lived in exile since 1989.
  - into exile The whole family went into exile.
  - to be forced/sent into exile
  - in exile from… Dante died in exile from Florence.

### [exit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exit_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɡzɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈeɡzɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exi/exit_/exit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exi/exit_/exit__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (+ adv./prep.) The bullet entered her back and exited through her chest.
  - We exited via a fire door.
  - exit something As the actors exited the stage the lights went on.

### [expenditure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/expenditure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspendɪtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspendɪtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expen/expenditure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expen/expenditure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a reduction in public/government/military expenditure
  - plans to increase expenditure on health
  - The budget provided for a total expenditure of £27 billion.
  - Savings have been disposed of in order to meet the growing gap between income and expenditure.

### [experimental](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/experimental) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˌsperɪˈmentl/` · **US:** `/ɪkˌsperɪˈmentl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/exper/experimental__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/exper/experimental__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The school's experimental teaching methods include letting the children decide what to study.
  - experimental theatre/art/music
  - The equipment is still at the experimental stage.
  - Doctors stress that this kind of treatment is still experimental.

### [expire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/expire) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expir/expire__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expir/expire__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - When does your driving licence expire?
  - Our lease on the house expires next month.

### [explicit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/explicit) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expli/explicit__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expli/explicit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He gave me very explicit directions on how to get there.
  - The reasons for the decision should be made explicit.
  - She made some very explicit references to my personal life.

### [explicitly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/explicitly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈsplɪsɪtli/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈsplɪsɪtli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expli/explicitly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expli/explicitly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The report states explicitly that the system was to blame.
  - The text does not explicitly mention him by name.

### [exploitation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exploitation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌeksplɔɪˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌeksplɔɪˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/explo/exploitation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/explo/exploitation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the exploitation of children
  - Starvation and poverty are the result of global economic exploitation, not lack of resources.

### [explosive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/explosive_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspləʊzɪv/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspləʊzɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/explo/explosive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/explo/explosive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an explosive device (= a bomb)
  - an explosive mixture of chemicals
  - Hydrogen is highly explosive.

### [explosive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/explosive_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspləʊzɪv/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspləʊzɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/explo/explosive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/explo/explosive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - plastic explosives
  - The bomb was packed with several pounds of high explosive.

### [extract](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/extract_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈstrækt/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈstrækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ext/extra/extract__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ext/extra/extract__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a machine that extracts excess moisture from the air
  - The Egyptians used a primitive form of distillation to extract the essential oils from plants.
  - Animals take in food and extract nutrients from it.

### [extremist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/extremist_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈstriːmɪst/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈstriːmɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ext/extre/extremist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ext/extre/extremist__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - left-wing/right-wing/political/religious extremists
  - He was assassinated by extremists.


## F

### [facilitate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/facilitate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/` · **US:** `/fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fac/facil/facilitate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fac/facil/facilitate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The new trade agreement should facilitate more rapid economic growth.
  - Structured teaching facilitates learning.

### [faction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/faction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfækʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈfækʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fac/facti/faction__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fac/facti/faction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are rival factions within the administration.

### [faculty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/faculty) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfæklti/` · **US:** `/ˈfæklti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fac/facul/faculty__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fac/facul/faculty__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Faculty of Law
  - students who are doing degrees in the Arts Faculty
  - the Faculty of Arts

### [fade](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fade) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/feɪd/` · **US:** `/feɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fad/fade_/fade__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fad/fade_/fade__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The curtains had faded in the sun.
  - fade from something All colour had faded from the sky.
  - fade something The sun had faded the curtains.
  - He was wearing faded blue jeans.

### [fairness](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fairness) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfeənəs/` · **US:** `/ˈfernəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fai/fairn/fairness__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fai/fairn/fairness__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The fairness of the judicial system is being questioned.
  - She has a strong sense of justice and fairness.

### [fatal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fatal) *(adjective)*
