- **UK:** `/ˈfeɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈfeɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fat/fatal/fatal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fat/fatal/fatal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fatal accident/blow/illness
  - a potentially fatal form of cancer
  - If she gets ill again it could prove fatal.

### [fate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fate) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/feɪt/` · **US:** `/feɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fat/fate_/fate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fat/fate_/fate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The fate of the three men is unknown.
  - She sat outside, waiting to find out her fate.
  - The court will decide our fate/fates.
  - Each of the managers suffered the same fate.
  - He warned about the scam so others could avoid a similar fate.
  - Inhabitants of war-torn areas have been abandoned to their fate.
  - From that moment our fate was sealed (= our future was decided).

### [favourable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/favourable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfeɪvərəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈfeɪvərəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fav/favou/favourable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fav/favou/favourable__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She made a favourable impression on his parents.
  - The biography shows him in a favourable light.
  - She gained a highly favourable impression of the company.

### [feat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/feat) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fiːt/` · **US:** `/fiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fea/feat_/feat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fea/feat_/feat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The tunnel is a remarkable feat of engineering.
  - to accomplish/achieve/perform astonishing feats
  - That was no mean feat (= it was difficult to do).

### [feminist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/feminist_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfemənɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈfemənɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fem/femin/feminist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fem/femin/feminist__us_1.mp3)

### [feminist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/feminist_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfemənɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈfemənɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fem/femin/feminist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fem/femin/feminist__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈfaɪbə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfaɪbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fib/fibre/fibre__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fib/fibre/fibre__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/fɪəs/` · **US:** `/fɪrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fie/fierc/fierce__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fie/fierc/fierce__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈfɪlm meɪkə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪlm meɪkər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fil/film_/film_maker_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fil/film_/film_maker_1_us_1.mp3)

### [filter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/filter_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪltə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪltər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fil/filte/filter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fil/filte/filter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an air/oil filter
  - a coffee/water filter

### [filter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/filter_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪltə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪltər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fil/filte/filter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fil/filte/filter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - All drinking water must be filtered.
  - Use a sun block that filters UVA effectively.

### [fine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fine_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/faɪn/` · **US:** `/faɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/fine_/fine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/fine_/fine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I got a parking fine for parking on double yellow lines.
  - Offenders will be liable to a heavy fine (= one that costs a lot of money).
  - Under the new law, motorists face fines of up to £1 000.
  - She has already paid over $2 000 in fines.

### [fine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fine_4) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/faɪn/` · **US:** `/faɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/fine_/fine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/fine_/fine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - fine somebody (something) The magistrate fined him $400.
  - be fined (something) Any company found to be breaking these rules will be heavily fined.
  - be fined for (doing) something She was fined for speeding.
  - be fined something for (doing) something The company was fined £20 000 for breaching safety regulations.
  - He got fined £200 for parking illegally.

### [firearm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/firearm) *(noun)*

### [flaw](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flaw) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/flɔː/` · **US:** `/flɔː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fla/flaw_/flaw__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fla/flaw_/flaw__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [flawed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flawed) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/flɔːd/` · **US:** `/flɔːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fla/flawe/flawed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fla/flawe/flawed__us_1.mp3)

  - a series of fatally flawed judgements

### [flee](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flee) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fliː/` · **US:** `/fliː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fle/flee_/flee__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fle/flee_/flee__us_1.mp3)

  - flee something He was caught trying to flee the country.

- **UK:** `/frʌˈstreɪtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈfrʌstreɪtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fru/frust/frustrating__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fru/frust/frustrating__us_1.mp3)

### [frustration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frustration) *(noun)*

- **UK:** `/ɡlɑːns/` · **US:** `/ɡlæns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gla/glanc/glance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gla/glanc/glance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to take/have a glance at the newspaper headlines
  - a cursory/brief/casual/furtive glance
  - The sisters exchanged glances (= looked at each other).
  - She shot him a sideways glance.
  - He walked away without a backward glance.
  - She stole a glance (= looked secretly) at her watch.

### [glance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glance_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡlɑːns/` · **US:** `/ɡlæns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gla/glanc/glance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gla/glanc/glance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She glanced at her watch.
  - He glanced around the room.
  - I glanced up quickly to see who had come in.

### [glimpse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glimpse_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡlɪmps/` · **US:** `/ɡlɪmps/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gli/glimp/glimpse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gli/glimp/glimpse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - glimpse (of somebody/something) He caught a glimpse of her in the crowd.
  - I came up on deck to get my first glimpse of the island.
  - Fans crowded the street outside waiting for a glimpse of their hero.
  - glimpse at somebody/something I just got a glimpse at the baby, but she was very cute.

### [glorious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glorious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡlɔːriəs/` · **US:** `/ˈɡlɔːriəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/glo/glori/glorious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/glo/glori/glorious__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We congratulate you on this glorious victory.
  - This is a glorious chapter in our country's history.
  - The film looks back at Spain’s glorious past.

### [glory](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glory_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡlɔːri/` · **US:** `/ˈɡlɔːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/glo/glory/glory__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/glo/glory/glory__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Olympic glory in the 100 metres
  - I do all the work and he gets all the glory.
  - She wanted to enjoy her moment of glory.
  - He came home a rich man, covered in glory.

### [governance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/governance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡʌvənəns/` · **US:** `/ˈɡʌvərnəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gov/gover/governance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gov/gover/governance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He emphasized the company's commitment to high standards of corporate governance.
  - The aim is to include representatives of all ethnic groups in the governance of the new state.
  - The government is stressing the link between aid and good governance in poor countries.

### [grace](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grace_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡreɪs/` · **US:** `/ɡreɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grace/grace__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grace/grace__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She moves with the natural grace of a ballerina.
  - a debut album of uncommon grace and beauty

### [grasp](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grasp_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɑːsp/` · **US:** `/ɡræsp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grasp/grasp__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grasp/grasp__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I grabbed him, but he slipped from my grasp.
  - She felt a firm grasp on her arm.
  - Don't let the situation escape from your grasp.
  - As soon as he relaxed his grasp on the rope, it was pulled out of his hands.

### [grasp](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grasp_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɑːsp/` · **US:** `/ɡræsp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grasp/grasp__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grasp/grasp__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He grasped my hand and shook it warmly.
  - Kay grasped him by the wrist.

### [grave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grave1_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɡreɪv/` · **US:** `/ɡreɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grave/grave__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grave/grave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The police have expressed grave concern about the missing child's safety.
  - The consequences will be very grave if nothing is done.
  - We were in grave danger.
  - I fear you are making a very grave mistake.

### [grave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grave1_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡreɪv/` · **US:** `/ɡreɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grave/grave__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grave/grave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We visited Grandma's grave.
  - British war graves in France and Belgium
  - in a grave The plague victims were buried in a mass grave.
  - on a grave There were flowers on the grave.

### [gravity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/gravity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡrævəti/` · **US:** `/ˈɡrævəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/gravi/gravity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/gravi/gravity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Newton’s law of gravity

### [grid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grid) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɪd/` · **US:** `/ɡrɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grid_/grid__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grid_/grid__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - New York’s grid of streets

### [grief](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grief) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡriːf/` · **US:** `/ɡriːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grief/grief__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grief/grief__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was overcome with grief when her husband died.
  - They were able to share their common joys and griefs.
  - grief for somebody/something her grief for her dead husband
  - grief over something grief over the loss of friends
  - grief at something He could not hide his grief at her death.

### [grin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grin_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɪn/` · **US:** `/ɡrɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grin_/grin__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grin_/grin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She gave a broad grin.
  - a wry/sheepish grin
  - with a grin ‘No,’ he said with a grin.

### [grin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grin_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɪn/` · **US:** `/ɡrɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grin_/grin__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grin_/grin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They grinned with delight when they heard our news.
  - Stop grinning and tell me what happened!
  - grin at somebody She grinned amiably at us.
  - grin something He grinned a wide grin.

### [grind](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grind_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡraɪnd/` · **US:** `/ɡraɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grind/grind__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grind/grind__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to grind coffee/corn
  - The animal has teeth that grind its food into a pulp.

### [grip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grip_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɪp/` · **US:** `/ɡrɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grip_/grip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grip_/grip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Keep a tight grip on the rope.
  - to loosen/release/relax your grip
  - She tried to get a grip on the icy rock.
  - The climber slipped and lost her grip.
  - She struggled from his grip.
  - Try adjusting your grip on the racket.

### [grip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grip_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrɪp/` · **US:** `/ɡrɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gri/grip_/grip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gri/grip_/grip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - grip something ‘Please don't go,’ he said, gripping her arm.
  - grip on to something She gripped on to the railing with both hands.

### [gross](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/gross_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrəʊs/` · **US:** `/ɡrəʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gro/gross/gross__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gro/gross/gross__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - gross weight (= including the container or wrapping)
  - gross income/wage (= before taxes, etc. are taken away)
  - Investments showed a gross profit of 26 per cent.
  - The family have three children and a gross income of £50 000.

### [guerrilla](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/guerrilla_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡəˈrɪlə/` · **US:** `/ɡəˈrɪlə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gue/guerr/guerrilla__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gue/guerr/guerrilla__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - urban guerrillas (= those who fight in towns)
  - guerrilla war/warfare (= fought by guerrillas on one or both sides)
  - a guerrilla movement

### [guidance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/guidance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡaɪdns/` · **US:** `/ˈɡaɪdns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gui/guida/guidance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gui/guida/guidance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - guidance for teachers on how to use video in the classroom
  - under the guidance of somebody Activities all take place under the guidance of an experienced tutor.
  - The helpline was set up for young people in need of guidance and support.

### [guilt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/guilt_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡɪlt/` · **US:** `/ɡɪlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gui/guilt/guilt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gui/guilt/guilt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She had feelings of guilt about leaving her children and going to work.
  - Many survivors were left with a sense of guilt.
  - a guilt complex (= an exaggerated sense of guilt)

### [gut](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/gut_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡʌt/` · **US:** `/ɡʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gut/gut__/gut__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gut/gut__/gut__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It can take up to 72 hours for food to pass through the gut.
  - I had a terrible pain in my guts after eating too many plums.


## H

### [hail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hail_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/heɪl/` · **US:** `/heɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hai/hail_/hail__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hai/hail_/hail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be hailed (as) something The conference was hailed as a great success.
  - The expedition was widely hailed as a success.
  - Teenager Matt Brown is being hailed a hero for saving a young child from drowning.
  - hail somebody/something as something Union leaders hailed the socialists’ victory as a huge step forward.

### [halfway](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/halfway_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌhɑːfˈweɪ/` · **US:** `/ˌhæfˈweɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hal/halfw/halfway__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hal/halfw/halfway__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's about halfway between London and Bristol.
  - He left halfway through the ceremony.
  - I'm afraid we're not even halfway there yet.

### [halt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/halt_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɒlt/` · **US:** `/hɔːlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hal/halt_/halt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hal/halt_/halt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Work came to a halt when the machine broke down.
  - The thought brought her to an abrupt halt.
  - The car skidded to a halt.
  - Strikes have led to a halt in production.
  - They decided it was time to call a halt to the project (= stop it officially).

### [halt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/halt_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hɒlt/` · **US:** `/hɔːlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hal/halt_/halt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hal/halt_/halt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She walked towards him and then halted.
  - ‘Halt!’ the Major ordered (= used as a command to soldiers).
  - halt somebody/something The police were halting traffic on the parade route.
  - The trial was halted after the first week.

### [handful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/handful) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhændfʊl/` · **US:** `/ˈhændfʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/han/handf/handful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/han/handf/handful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a handful of rice
  - She scooped up handfuls of loose earth.

### [handling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/handling) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhændlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈhændlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/han/handl/handling__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/han/handl/handling__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was impressed by his handling of the affair.
  - This horse needs firm handling.
  - Why is he being so polite, as if I need tactful handling?

### [handy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/handy) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhændi/` · **US:** `/ˈhændi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/han/handy/handy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/han/handy/handy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a handy little tool
  - handy hints/tips for removing stains
  - a handy-sized dictionary

### [harassment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/harassment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/həˈræsmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈhærəsmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/haras/harassment__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/haras/harassment__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sexual/racial harassment
  - workplace/street harassment (= that happens at work/in the street)

### [hardware](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hardware) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɑːdweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːrdwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/hardw/hardware__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/hardw/hardware__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We supply computer hardware to businesses.

### [harmony](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/harmony) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɑːməni/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːrməni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/harmo/harmony__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/harmo/harmony__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - social/racial harmony
  - in harmony They lived together in perfect harmony.
  - in harmony with something the need to be in harmony with our environment

### [harsh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/harsh) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hɑːʃ/` · **US:** `/hɑːrʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/harsh/harsh__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/harsh/harsh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The punishment was harsh and unfair.
  - The minister received some harsh criticism.
  - the harsh treatment of prisoners
  - He regretted his harsh words.
  - We had to face up to the harsh realities of life sooner or later.
  - It may seem harsh to criticize him after his death.
  - ‘She's just totally useless!’ ‘That's a bit harsh.’

### [harvest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/harvest_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɑːvɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːrvɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/harve/harvest__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/harve/harvest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - harvest time
  - Farmers are extremely busy during the harvest.

### [harvest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/harvest_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɑːvɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːrvɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/harve/harvest__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/harve/harvest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The nuts are ready to harvest in September.

### [hatred](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hatred) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈheɪtrɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈheɪtrɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hat/hatre/hatred__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hat/hatre/hatred__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He looked at me with intense hatred.
  - There was fear and hatred in his voice.
  - hatred for somebody/something She felt nothing but hatred for her attacker.
  - hatred of somebody/something a profound hatred of war
  - hatred towards somebody feelings of hatred towards the bombers
  - racial hatred (= hatred for people from a different race)
  - The debate simply revived old hatreds.

### [haunt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/haunt_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hɔːnt/` · **US:** `/hɔːnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hau/haunt/haunt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hau/haunt/haunt__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A headless rider haunts the country lanes.
  - I’ll come back to haunt you!

### [hazard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hazard_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhæzəd/` · **US:** `/ˈhæzərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/haz/hazar/hazard__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/haz/hazar/hazard__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fire/safety hazard
  - hazard to somebody/something Growing levels of pollution represent a serious health hazard to the local population.
  - hazard of (doing) something Everybody is aware of the hazards of smoking.

### [heighten](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heighten) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhaɪtn/` · **US:** `/ˈhaɪtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hei/heigh/heighten__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hei/heigh/heighten__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Tension has heightened after the recent bomb attack.
  - heighten something The campaign is intended to heighten public awareness of the disease.
  - His breakdown was followed by a period of heightened activity.
  - Fears of a pandemic were heightened by rising case numbers.
  - The drug is said to give users a heightened sense of well-being.

### [heritage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heritage) *(noun)*

### [hint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hint_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɪnt/` · **US:** `/hɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hin/hint_/hint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hin/hint_/hint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He gave a broad hint (= one that was obvious) that he was thinking of retiring.
  - Should I drop a hint (= give a hint) to Matt?
  - He resented any hint that he might be to blame.

### [hint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hint_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hɪnt/` · **US:** `/hɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hin/hint_/hint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hin/hint_/hint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - hint (that)… They hinted (that) there might be more job losses.
  - + speech ‘I might know something about it,’ he hinted.

### [homeland](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/homeland) *(noun)*

### [hook](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hook_2) *(verb)*

### [hopeful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hopeful_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhəʊpfl/` · **US:** `/ˈhəʊpfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hop/hopef/hopeful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hop/hopef/hopeful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hopeful (that…) I feel hopeful that we'll find a suitable house very soon.
  - hopeful about something He is not very hopeful about the outcome of the interview.
  - hopeful of doing something (British English) She is hopeful of returning to work soon.

### [horizon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/horizon_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/həˈraɪzn/` · **US:** `/həˈraɪzn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hor/horiz/horizon__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hor/horiz/horizon__us_1.mp3)

### [horn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/horn_1) *(noun)*

### [hostage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hostage) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɒstɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːstɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hos/hosta/hostage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hos/hosta/hostage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - The government is negotiating the release of the hostages.
  - The hijackers kept the pilot as a hostage on board the plane.
  - The gunmen took 24 hostages.
  - diplomatic efforts to get the hostages released

### [hostile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hostile) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɒstaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːstaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hos/hosti/hostile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hos/hosti/hostile__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The speaker got a hostile reception from the audience.

### [hostility](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hostility) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɒˈstɪləti/` · **US:** `/hɑːˈstɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hos/hosti/hostility__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hos/hosti/hostility__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There was a barely veiled hostility in her tone.
  - hostility to/towards somebody/something feelings of hostility towards people from other backgrounds
  - hostility between A and B There was open hostility between the two schools.

### [humanitarian](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/humanitarian_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hjuːˌmænɪˈteəriən/` · **US:** `/hjuːˌmænɪˈteriən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hum/human/humanitarian__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hum/human/humanitarian__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [humanity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/humanity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hjuːˈmænəti/` · **US:** `/hjuːˈmænəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hum/human/humanity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hum/human/humanity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [humble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/humble_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhʌmbl/` · **US:** `/ˈhʌmbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hum/humbl/humble__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hum/humbl/humble__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈhaɪdrədʒən/` · **US:** `/ˈhaɪdrədʒən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hyd/hydro/hydrogen__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hyd/hydro/hydrogen__us_1.mp3)


## I

### [identification](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/identification) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/aɪˌdentɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/aɪˌdentɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ide/ident/identification__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ide/ident/identification__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The identification of the crash victims was a long and difficult task.
  - Accurate identification of these birds is difficult.
  - Each product has a number for easy identification.
  - The vehicle's identification number is stamped on the engine.
  - Biometric identification methods include fingerprints and facial recognition scanners.
  - Only one witness could make a positive identification.
  - The hospital had mixed up the patients' identification tags.

### [ideological](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ideological) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaɪdiəˈlɒdʒɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˌaɪdiəˈlɑːdʒɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ide/ideol/ideological__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ide/ideol/ideological__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ideological differences
  - This was rejected for ideological reasons.
  - His agenda has been purely ideological.

### [ideology](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ideology) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaɪdiˈɒlədʒi/` · **US:** `/ˌaɪdiˈɑːlədʒi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ide/ideol/ideology__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ide/ideol/ideology__us_1_rr.mp3)

### [ignorance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ignorance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪɡnərəns/` · **US:** `/ˈɪɡnərəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ign/ignor/ignorance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ign/ignor/ignorance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They fought a long battle against prejudice and ignorance.
  - I hate to admit my ignorance, but how does it work?
  - ignorance of something He showed a remarkable ignorance of the facts.
  - ignorance about something widespread ignorance about the disease
  - in ignorance of something They were kept in ignorance of their government's activities.
  - out of ignorance Children often behave badly out of ignorance.
  - through ignorance Many lives are lost through ignorance.

  - Nothing would induce me to take the job.

### [indulge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/indulge) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdʌldʒ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdʌldʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indul/indulge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indul/indulge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - indulge in something They went into town to indulge in some serious shopping.
  - She has never been one to indulge in gossip.
  - She was free to indulge in a little romantic daydreaming.
  - indulge yourself (with something) I indulged myself with a long hot bath.
  - For a special treat, indulge yourself with one of these luxury desserts.

### [inequality](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inequality) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnɪˈkwɒləti/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnɪˈkwɑːləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ine/inequ/inequality__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ine/inequ/inequality__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We need to tackle inequality of opportunity wherever we find it.
  - economic inequalities between different areas
  - Gender and racial inequality are still widespread throughout society.

### [infamous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/infamous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnfəməs/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnfəməs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/infam/infamous__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/infam/infamous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a general who was infamous for his brutality
  - the most infamous concentration camp
  - (humorous) the infamous British sandwich

### [infant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/infant_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnfənt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnfənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/infan/infant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/infan/infant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a nursery for infants under two
  - their infant son
  - She was seriously ill as an infant.
  - Mozart was an infant prodigy (= a young child with unusual ability).

### [infect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/infect) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈfekt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈfekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/infec/infect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/infec/infect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - infect somebody/something (with something) It is not possible to infect another person through kissing.
  - (be) infected with something people infected with HIV

### [inflict](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inflict) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈflɪkt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈflɪkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/infli/inflict__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/infli/inflict__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - inflict something on/upon somebody/something They inflicted a humiliating defeat on the home team.
  - Heavy casualties were inflicted on the enemy.
  - (humorous) Do you have to inflict that music on us?
  - inflict something They surveyed the damage inflicted by the storm.
  - The rodent’s sharp teeth can inflict a nasty bite.
  - When someone deliberately inflicts damage, it is a matter for the police.

### [influential](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/influential) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnfluˈenʃl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnfluˈenʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/influ/influential__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/influ/influential__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a highly influential book
  - influential in something She is one of the most influential figures in local politics.
  - influential in doing something The committee was influential in formulating government policy on employment.

### [inherent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inherent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈhɪərənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈhɪrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inh/inher/inherent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inh/inher/inherent__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the difficulties inherent in a study of this type
  - Violence is inherent in our society.
  - an inherent weakness in the design of the machine

### [inhibit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inhibit) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inh/inhib/inhibit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inh/inhib/inhibit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A lack of oxygen may inhibit brain development in the unborn child.
  - Financial considerations are inhibiting access to higher education.
  - Alcohol significantly inhibits the action of the drug.

### [initiate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/initiate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈnɪʃieɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈnɪʃieɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ini/initi/initiate__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ini/initi/initiate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to initiate legal proceedings against somebody
  - The government has initiated a programme of economic reform.

### [inject](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inject) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdʒekt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdʒekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inj/injec/inject__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inj/injec/inject__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - inject something (into yourself/somebody/something) Adrenaline was injected into the muscle.
  - The anaesthetic is injected locally.
  - inject (yourself/somebody/something) (with something) She has been injecting herself with insulin since the age of 16.
  - The animals were injected with small amounts of the chemical.
  - She's a former heroin addict who, six months after she stopped injecting, is getting her life back together.

### [injection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/injection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdʒekʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdʒekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inj/injec/injection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inj/injec/injection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to give somebody an injection
  - He was treated with penicillin injections.
  - daily injections of insulin
  - An anaesthetic was administered by injection.
  - In some US states execution is by lethal injection.

### [injustice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/injustice) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdʒʌstɪs/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdʒʌstɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inj/injus/injustice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inj/injus/injustice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We are committed to fighting against poverty and injustice.
  - a burning sense of injustice
  - Social and political injustice seemed to be getting worse rather than better.
  - She was enraged at the injustice of the remark.
  - The report exposes the injustices of the system.

### [inmate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inmate) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnmeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnmeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inm/inmat/inmate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inm/inmat/inmate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The jail has 500 inmates.
  - He was attacked by a fellow inmate.

### [insertion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/insertion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈsɜːʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈsɜːrʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/inser/insertion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/inser/insertion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - An examination is carried out before the insertion of the tube.
  - Acupuncture involves the insertion of needles into specific points of the body.

### [insider](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/insider) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈsaɪdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈsaɪdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insid/insider__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insid/insider__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The situation was described by one insider as ‘absolute chaos’.
  - an insider’s view of the movie industry

### [inspect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inspect) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈspekt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈspekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/inspe/inspect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/inspe/inspect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - inspect something/somebody The teacher walked around inspecting their work.
  - Make sure you inspect the goods before signing for them.
  - inspect something/somebody for something The plants are regularly inspected for disease.

### [inspection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inspection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈspekʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈspekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/inspe/inspection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/inspe/inspection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Regular inspections are carried out at the prison.
  - The head went on a tour of inspection of all the classrooms.

### [inspiration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inspiration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnspəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnspəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/inspi/inspiration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/inspi/inspiration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Dreams can be a rich source of inspiration for an artist.
  - Both poets drew their inspiration from the countryside.
  - Her work lacks inspiration (= it does not contain any exciting new ideas).
  - inspiration for something Looking for inspiration for a new dessert? Try this recipe.
  - inspiration to do something She had the time and the inspiration to develop her talent.

### [instinct](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instinct) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnstɪŋkt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnstɪŋkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insti/instinct__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insti/instinct__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She did not seem to have any of the usual maternal instincts.
  - by instinct Children do not know by instinct the difference between right and wrong.
  - instinct (is) to do something His first instinct was to run away.
  - instinct for (doing) something Horses have a well-developed instinct for fear.
  - Even at school, he showed he had an instinct for (= was naturally good at) business.

### [institutional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/institutional) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnstɪˈtjuːʃənl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnstɪˈtuːʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insti/institutional__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insti/institutional__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - institutional investors

### [instruct](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instruct) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈstrʌkt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈstrʌkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/instr/instruct__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/instr/instruct__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - instruct somebody to do something The letter instructed him to report to headquarters immediately.
  - instruct somebody where, what, etc… You will be instructed where to go as soon as the plane is ready.
  - instruct somebody She arrived at 10 o'clock as instructed.
  - instruct that… He instructed that a wall be built around the city.
  - (British English also) He instructed that a wall should be built around the city.
  - instruct (somebody) + speech ‘Put it there,’ she instructed (them).

### [instrumental](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instrumental_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnstrəˈmentl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnstrəˈmentl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/instr/instrumental__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/instr/instrumental__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The Conservation Trust performs an instrumental role in the protection of rural environments.
  - instrumental in (doing) something He was instrumental in bringing about an end to the conflict.
  - They were highly instrumental in bringing the business to Newtown.

### [insufficient](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/insufficient) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnsəˈfɪʃnt/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnsəˈfɪʃnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insuf/insufficient__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insuf/insufficient__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - insufficient time
  - The management paid insufficient attention to working conditions.
  - There are fears that the existing flood barrier may prove insufficient.
  - I’m afraid we have insufficient evidence.
  - insufficient to do something His salary is insufficient to meet his needs.
  - insufficient for something The plan was to refurbish the whole building but the funds raised were insufficient for the purpose.

### [insult](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/insult_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnsʌlt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnsʌlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insul/insult__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insul/insult__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The crowd were shouting insults at the police.
  - insult to somebody/something His comments were seen as an insult to the president.
  - The questions were an insult to our intelligence (= too easy).

### [insult](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/insult_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈsʌlt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈsʌlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insul/insult__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insul/insult__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I have never been so insulted in my life!
  - She felt insulted by the low offer.
  - You insult my intelligence! (= you are treating me as if I am stupid)

### [intact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intact) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtækt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intac/intact__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intac/intact__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most of the house remains intact even after two hundred years.
  - He emerged from the trial with his reputation intact.

### [intake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intake) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnteɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnteɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intak/intake__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intak/intake__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - high fluid intake
  - to reduce your daily intake of salt

### [integral](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/integral) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈteɡrəl/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈteɡrəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/integ/integral__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/integ/integral__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Music is an integral part of the school's curriculum.
  - integral to something Practical experience is integral to the course.

### [integrated](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/integrated) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪntɪɡreɪtɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈɪntɪɡreɪtɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/integ/integrated__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/integ/integrated__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an integrated programme of patient care
  - an integrated transport system (= including buses, trains, taxis, etc.)
  - a highly integrated approach to planning

### [integration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/integration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntɪˈɡreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntɪˈɡreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/integ/integration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/integ/integration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The aim is to promote closer economic integration.
  - integration of A and/with B His music is an integration of tradition and new technology.

### [integrity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/integrity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈteɡrəti/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈteɡrəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/integ/integrity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/integ/integrity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - personal/professional/artistic integrity
  - She behaved with absolute integrity.
  - a man of great integrity

### [intellectual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intellectual_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈlektʃuəl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntəˈlektʃuəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intel/intellectual__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intel/intellectual__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was a leading intellectual of his day.

### [intensify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intensify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtensɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtensɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inten/intensify__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inten/intensify__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Violence intensified during the night.
  - The fighting in the area has intensified sharply.
  - intensify something The opposition leader has intensified his attacks on the government.
  - The reforms served only to intensify the misery of the poorer peasants.

### [intensity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intensity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtensəti/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtensəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inten/intensity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inten/intensity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - intensity of light/sound/colour
  - intensity of feeling/concentration/relief
  - He was watching her with an intensity that was unnerving.
  - The storm resumed with even greater intensity.

### [intensive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intensive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtensɪv/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtensɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inten/intensive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inten/intensive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an intensive language course
  - two weeks of intensive training
  - intensive diplomatic negotiations
  - highly intensive courses for business and professional people

### [intent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intent_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtent/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inten/intent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inten/intent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She denies possessing the drug with intent to supply.
  - He was charged with wounding with intent.
  - a letter/statement of intent
  - His intent is clearly not to placate his critics.

### [interactive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interactive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntərˈæktɪv/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈæktɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interactive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interactive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - interactive displays/video
  - using ICT to create an interactive learning experience
  - an interactive online map that helps people find bicycle routes through the city
  - A series of interactive exhibits will teach visitors about the history of the bridge.
  - The program is fully interactive.

### [interface](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interface_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪntəfeɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈɪntərfeɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interface__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interface__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the user interface

### [interfere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interfere) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈfɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈfɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interfere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interfere__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I wish my mother would stop interfering and let me make my own decisions.
  - interfere in something The police are very unwilling to interfere in family problems.

### [interference](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interference) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈfɪərəns/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈfɪrəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interference__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interference__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - interference (in something) They resent foreign interference in the internal affairs of their country.
  - political interference in legal proceedings
  - interference with something interference with proper medical procedures

### [interim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interim_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪntərɪm/` · **US:** `/ˈɪntərɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interim__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an interim government/measure/report
  - The vice-president took power in the interim period before the election.
  - He only holds the post on an interim basis.

### [interior](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interior_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtɪəriə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtɪriər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interior__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interior__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - interior walls

### [interior](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interior_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtɪəriə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtɪriər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interior__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interior__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the interior of a building/a car
  - The original interior of the hotel has been replaced.

### [intermediate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intermediate_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈmiːdiət/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈmiːdiət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/intermediate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/intermediate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an intermediate stage/step in a process
  - intermediate between A and B Liquid crystals are considered to be intermediate between liquid and solid.

### [intervene](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intervene) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈviːn/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈviːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/intervene__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/intervene__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She might have been killed if the neighbours hadn't intervened.
  - intervene in something The President intervened personally in the crisis.
  - intervene between A and B She went over to intervene between the two men.
  - intervene against somebody They would not intervene against the rebels themselves.
  - intervene (with somebody) (on behalf of somebody) attempts to intervene with the authorities on the prisoners' behalf
  - intervene to do something They intervened to halt the attack.

### [intervention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intervention) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈvenʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈvenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/intervention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/intervention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - calls for government intervention to save the steel industry
  - intervention in something In the second group of states, direct intervention in the economy was limited.

### [intimate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intimate_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪntɪmət/` · **US:** `/ˈɪntɪmət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intim/intimate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intim/intimate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [intriguing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intriguing) *(adjective)*

- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvɪzəbl/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvɪzəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invis/invisible__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invis/invisible__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvəʊk/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invok/invoke__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invok/invoke__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvɒlvmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvɑːlvmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invol/involvement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invol/involvement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/aɪˈrɒnɪk/` · **US:** `/aɪˈrɑːnɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iro/ironi/ironic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iro/ironi/ironic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [ironically](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ironically) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/aɪˈrɒnɪkli/` · **US:** `/aɪˈrɑːnɪkli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iro/ironi/ironically__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iro/ironi/ironically__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He smiled ironically.

### [irony](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/irony) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaɪrəni/` · **US:** `/ˈaɪrəni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iro/irony/irony__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iro/irony/irony__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was one of life's little ironies.
  - the irony (is) that… The irony is that when he finally got the job, he discovered he didn't like it.

### [irrelevant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/irrelevant) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈreləvənt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈreləvənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/irr/irrel/irrelevant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/irr/irrel/irrelevant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - totally/completely/largely irrelevant
  - irrelevant remarks
  - Whether I believe you or not is irrelevant now.
  - irrelevant to something/somebody That evidence is irrelevant to the case.
  - Many people consider politics irrelevant to their lives.

### [isolation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/isolation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaɪsəˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌaɪsəˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iso/isola/isolation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iso/isola/isolation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - geographical isolation
  - an isolation hospital/ward (= for people with infectious diseases)
  - isolation (from somebody/something) The country has been threatened with complete isolation from the international community unless the atrocities stop.
  - He lives in splendid isolation (= far from, or in a superior position to, everyone else).
  - the isolation of the polio virus


## J

### [judicial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/judicial) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dʒuˈdɪʃl/` · **US:** `/dʒuˈdɪʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jud/judic/judicial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jud/judic/judicial__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - judicial powers
  - the judicial process/system
  - Greenpeace applied for a judicial review to challenge the court’s decision.
  - The Supreme Court ordered a judicial inquiry into the deaths.

### [junction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/junction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒʌŋkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒʌŋkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jun/junct/junction__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jun/junct/junction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was near the junction of City Road and Old Street.
  - Come off the motorway at junction 6.
  - The most complicated motorway interchange in Britain is known as Spaghetti Junction.
  - In the 19th century the town became an important railway junction.

### [jurisdiction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/jurisdiction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdʒʊərɪsˈdɪkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdʒʊrɪsˈdɪkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jur/juris/jurisdiction__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jur/juris/jurisdiction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - jurisdiction over somebody/something The English court had no jurisdiction over the defendants.
  - jurisdiction (of somebody/something) to do something The Court of Appeal exercised its jurisdiction to order a review of the case.
  - within/outside somebody's jurisdiction These matters do not fall within our jurisdiction.

### [just](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/just_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dʒʌst/` · **US:** `/dʒʌst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jus/just_/just__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jus/just_/just__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a just decision/law/society

### [justification](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/justification) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdʒʌstɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdʒʌstɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jus/justi/justification__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jus/justi/justification__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - justification for doing something I can see no possible justification for any further tax increases.
  - justification for something I could find no real justification for the proposed reorganization.
  - with (some) justification He was getting angry—and with some justification.
  - without justification She was arrested entirely without justification.

- **UK:** `/ˈlɔɪəlti/` · **US:** `/ˈlɔɪəlti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loy/loyal/loyalty__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loy/loyal/loyalty__us_1.mp3)

### [mere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mere_1) *(adjective)*

- **Examples:**
  - It took her a mere 20 minutes to win.
  - A mere 2 per cent of their budget has been spent on publicity.
  - He seemed so young, a mere boy.
  - You've got the job. The interview will be a mere formality.

### [merely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/merely) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪəli/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mer/merel/merely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mer/merel/merely__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - He said nothing, merely smiled and watched her.
  - They agreed to go merely because they were getting paid for it.
  - I'm merely stating what everybody knows anyway.

### [merge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/merge) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/mɜːdʒ/` · **US:** `/mɜːrdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mer/merge/merge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mer/merge/merge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The banks are set to merge next year.
  - The two groups have merged to form a new party.
  - merge with something His department will merge with mine.
  - merge into something The villages expanded and merged into one large town.
  - merge (A and B) (together) Fact and fiction merge together in his latest thriller.
  - merge A with B His department will be merged with mine.
  - merge something The company was formed by merging three smaller firms.
  - merge something into something Merge multiple text files into one master file.

### [merger](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/merger) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɜːdʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmɜːrdʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mer/merge/merger__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mer/merge/merger__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If the merger goes through, thousands of jobs will be lost.
  - merger between/of A and B a merger between the two banks
  - merger with something our proposed merger with the university
  - There are local companies ripe for merger or acquisition.
