  - net income/earnings (= after tax has been paid)
  - net of something What do you earn net of tax?

### [newsletter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/newsletter) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnjuːzletə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnuːzletər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/new/newsl/newsletter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/new/newsl/newsletter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our sailing club produces a monthly newsletter.
  - a church newsletter

### [niche](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/niche_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nɪtʃ/` · **US:** `/nɪtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nic/niche/niche__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nic/niche/niche__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He eventually found his niche in sports journalism.

### [noble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/noble_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊbl/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nob/noble/noble__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nob/noble/noble__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a noble leader
  - noble ideals
  - He died for a noble cause.
  - She died in a noble cause.
  - It was very noble of you to go so far to take him home.

### [nod](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nod_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/nɒd/` · **US:** `/nɑːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nod/nod__/nod__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nod/nod__/nod__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I asked him if he would help me and he nodded.
  - ‘Ready?’ She nodded at me and we set off.
  - Her head nodded in agreement.
  - nod something He nodded his head sympathetically.
  - She nodded approval.
  - Everyone nodded their agreement.

### [nominate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nominate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɒmɪneɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈnɑːmɪneɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nom/nomin/nominate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nom/nomin/nominate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - nominate somebody/something (for something) She has been nominated for the presidency.
  - nominate somebody/something (as) something | nominate somebody/something + noun He was nominated (as) best actor.
  - nominate somebody/something to do something I nominated Paul to take on the role of treasurer.

### [nomination](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nomination) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌnɒmɪˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌnɑːmɪˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nom/nomin/nomination__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nom/nomin/nomination__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Membership of the club is by nomination only.
  - He won the nomination as Democratic candidate for the presidency.
  - They opposed her nomination to the post of Deputy Director.
  - He has had nine Oscar nominations.

### [nominee](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nominee) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌnɒmɪˈniː/` · **US:** `/ˌnɑːmɪˈniː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nom/nomin/nominee__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nom/nomin/nominee__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was chosen as the party's presidential nominee.
  - an Oscar nominee

### [non-profit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/non-profit) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌnɒn ˈprɒfɪt/` · **US:** `/ˌnɑːn ˈprɑːfɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/non/non_p/non_profit_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/non/non_p/non_profit_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an independent non-profit organization
  - The centre is run on a non-profit basis.
  - The charity is non-profit-making.

### [nonetheless](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nonetheless) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌnʌnðəˈles/` · **US:** `/ˌnʌnðəˈles/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/non/nonet/nonetheless__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/non/nonet/nonetheless__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The book is too long but, nonetheless, informative and entertaining.
  - The problems are not serious. Nonetheless, we shall need to tackle them soon.

### [nonsense](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nonsense) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɒnsns/` · **US:** `/ˈnɑːnsns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/non/nonse/nonsense__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/non/nonse/nonsense__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Reports that he has resigned are nonsense.
  - You're talking nonsense!
  - ‘I won't go.’ ‘Nonsense! You must go!’
  - It's nonsense to say they don't care.
  - The idea is an economic nonsense.
  - Ministers have dismissed the rumours of a bribery scandal as nonsense.

### [noon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/noon) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nuːn/` · **US:** `/nuːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/noo/noon_/noon__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/noo/noon_/noon__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We should be there by noon.
  - The conference opens at 12 noon on Saturday.
  - the noon deadline for the end of hostilities
  - I'm leaving on the noon train.

### [notable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notable_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊtəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊtəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notab/notable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notab/notable__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a notable success/achievement/example
  - His eyes are his most notable feature.
  - notable (for something) The town is notable for its ancient harbour.
  - With a few notable exceptions, everyone gave something.

### [notably](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notably) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊtəbli/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊtəbli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notab/notably__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notab/notably__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The house had many drawbacks, most notably its price.
  - This argument has been put forward by many politicians, most notably by the Prime Minister and the Foreign Secretary.

### [notify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊtɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊtɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notif/notify__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notif/notify__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - notify somebody Competition winners will be notified by post.
  - You must notify us in writing if you wish to cancel your subscription.
  - notify somebody of something The police must be notified of the date of the demonstration.
  - notify something to somebody The date of the demonstration must be notified to the police.
  - notify somebody that… Members have been notified that there will be a small increase in the fee.

### [notorious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notorious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊˈtɔːriəs/` · **US:** `/nəʊˈtɔːriəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notor/notorious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notor/notorious__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a notorious criminal
  - a location made notorious by battles between local gangs
  - notorious for something/for doing something The country is notorious for its appalling prison conditions.
  - notorious as something The bar has become notorious as a meeting-place for drug dealers.
  - an area which is notorious as a centre for drug trafficking

### [novel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/novel_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɒvl/` · **US:** `/ˈnɑːvl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nov/novel/novel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nov/novel/novel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a novel feature
  - Job-sharing is still a novel concept and it will take a while for employers to get used to it.
  - The plan sounded rather novel.
  - It was an American who came up with the novel idea of drive-in restaurants.

### [nursery](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nursery_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɜːsəri/` · **US:** `/ˈnɜːrsəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nur/nurse/nursery__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nur/nurse/nursery__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at nursery Her youngest child is at nursery now.
  - The children are at nursery three days a week.


## O

### [objection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/objection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əbˈdʒekʃn/` · **US:** `/əbˈdʒekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obj/objec/objection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obj/objec/objection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'd like to come too, if you have no objection.
  - They raised no objections at the time.
  - He did not know how to voice his objections.
  - objection to something The main objection to the plan was that it would cost too much.
  - There was widespread objection to the proposals.
  - objection to (somebody) doing something I have no objection to him coming to stay.
  - I have no objection to staying here.
  - objection from somebody The proposal will go ahead despite strong objections from the public.
  - objection that… He dismissed the objection that the plan was too expensive.

### [oblige](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/oblige) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈblaɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/əˈblaɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obl/oblig/oblige__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obl/oblig/oblige__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Parents are obliged by law to send their children to school.
  - I felt obliged to ask them to dinner.
  - He suffered a serious injury that obliged him to give up work.
  - Libel plaintiffs are virtually obliged to go into the witness box.

### [obsess](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/obsess) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əbˈses/` · **US:** `/əbˈses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obs/obses/obsess__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obs/obses/obsess__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be obsessed by somebody/something He's obsessed by computers.
  - be obsessed with somebody/something She's completely obsessed with him.
  - obsess somebody The need to produce the most exciting newspaper story obsesses most journalists.

### [obsession](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/obsession) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əbˈseʃn/` · **US:** `/əbˈseʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obs/obses/obsession__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obs/obses/obsession__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her fear of flying is bordering on obsession.
  - obsession with somebody/something There's a national obsession with celebrity in England.
  - The media's obsession with the young prince continues.

### [occasional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/occasional) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkeɪʒənl/` · **US:** `/əˈkeɪʒənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/occ/occas/occasional__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/occ/occas/occasional__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He works for us on an occasional basis.
  - The occasional sweet treat won't do you any harm.
  - He spent five years in Paris, with occasional visits to Italy.
  - an occasional smoker (= a person who smokes, but not often)

### [occurrence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/occurrence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkʌrəns/` · **US:** `/əˈkɜːrəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/occ/occur/occurrence__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/occ/occur/occurrence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a common/everyday/frequent/regular occurrence
  - Vandalism used to be a rare occurrence here.
  - The program counts the number of occurrences of any word within the text.

### [odds](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/odds) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɒdz/` · **US:** `/ɑːdz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/odd/odds_/odds__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/odd/odds_/odds__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The odds are very much in our favour (= we are likely to succeed).
  - The odds are heavily against him (= he is not likely to succeed).
  - The odds are that (= it is likely that) she'll win.
  - What are the odds (= how likely is it) he won't turn up?

### [offering](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/offering) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfərɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːfərɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offer/offering__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offer/offering__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the latest offering from the Canadian-born writer

### [offspring](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/offspring) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfsprɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːfsprɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offsp/offspring__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offsp/offspring__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the problems parents have with their teenage offspring
  - to produce/raise offspring

### [operational](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/operational) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɒpəˈreɪʃənl/` · **US:** `/ˌɑːpəˈreɪʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ope/opera/operational__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ope/opera/operational__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - operational activities/costs/difficulties

### [opt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opt) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɒpt/` · **US:** `/ɑːpt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opt/opt__/opt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opt/opt__/opt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - opt for/against something After graduating she opted for a career in music.
  - After a lot of thought, I opted against buying a motorbike.
  - opt to do something Many workers opted to leave their jobs rather than take a pay cut.

### [optical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/optical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒptɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːptɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opt/optic/optical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opt/optic/optical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - optical effects

### [optimism](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/optimism) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒptɪmɪzəm/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːptɪmɪzəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opt/optim/optimism__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opt/optim/optimism__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a mood of cautious optimism
  - There are very real grounds for optimism.
  - with optimism We may now look forward with optimism.
  - optimism about/for something optimism about/for the future
  - Both sides have expressed optimism about the chances of an early agreement.

### [oral](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/oral_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːrəl/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ora/oral_/oral__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ora/oral_/oral__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a test of both oral and written French
  - oral evidence
  - stories passed on by oral tradition

### [organizational](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/organizational) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɔːɡənaɪˈzeɪʃənl/` · **US:** `/ˌɔːrɡənəˈzeɪʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/org/organ/organizational__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/org/organ/organizational__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - organizational changes within the party

### [orientation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/orientation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɔːriənˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɔːriənˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ori/orien/orientation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ori/orien/orientation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - religious/political orientation
  - a person’s sexual orientation (= whether they are attracted to men, women or both)

### [originate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/originate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈrɪdʒɪneɪt/` · **US:** `/əˈrɪdʒɪneɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ori/origi/originate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ori/origi/originate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The disease is thought to have originated in the tropics.
  - The word originated as a marketing term.

### [outbreak](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outbreak) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtbreɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtbreɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outbr/outbreak__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outbr/outbreak__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the outbreak of war
  - an outbreak of typhoid
  - Outbreaks of rain are expected in the afternoon.
  - the events that led to the outbreak of World War I
  - an outbreak of hostilities

### [outing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outin/outing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outin/outing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a family outing
  - on an outing (to…) We went on an outing to London.
  - outing to… Every year she organized an outing to the countryside.

### [outlet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outlet) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtlet/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtlet/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outle/outlet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outle/outlet__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The business has 34 retail outlets in this state alone.

### [outlook](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outlook) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtlʊk/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtlʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outlo/outlook__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outlo/outlook__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Travel broadens your outlook.
  - a/an positive/optimistic outlook
  - a bleak/gloomy/pessimistic outlook
  - outlook on something He had a practical outlook on life.
  - in outlook They value tradition and are basically conservative in outlook.

### [outrage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outrage_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtreɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtreɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outra/outrage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outra/outrage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The judge's remarks caused public outrage.
  - She was filled with a strong sense of moral outrage.
  - Environmentalists have expressed outrage at the ruling.

### [outrage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outrage_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtreɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtreɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outra/outrage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outra/outrage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was outraged at the way he had been treated.
  - I am morally outraged by their actions.
  - The killings have outraged the entire community.

### [outsider](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outsider) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaʊtˈsaɪdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌaʊtˈsaɪdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outsi/outsider__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outsi/outsider__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Here she felt she would always be an outsider.

### [overlook](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/overlook) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌəʊvəˈlʊk/` · **US:** `/ˌəʊvərˈlʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/overl/overlook__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/overl/overlook__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He seems to have overlooked one important fact.
  - In my hurry to finish the exam I had overlooked part of one of the questions.

### [overly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/overly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊvəli/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊvərli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/overl/overly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/overl/overly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm not overly fond of pasta.
  - We think you are being overly optimistic.

### [oversee](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/oversee) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌəʊvəˈsiː/` · **US:** `/ˌəʊvərˈsiː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/overs/oversee__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/overs/oversee__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - United Nations observers oversaw the elections.

### [overturn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/overturn) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌəʊvəˈtɜːn/` · **US:** `/ˌəʊvərˈtɜːrn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/overt/overturn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/overt/overturn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The car skidded and overturned.
  - overturn something He stood up quickly, overturning his chair.

### [overwhelm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/overwhelm) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌəʊvəˈwelm/` · **US:** `/ˌəʊvərˈwelm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/overw/overwhelm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/overw/overwhelm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was overwhelmed by feelings of guilt.
  - The beauty of the landscape overwhelmed me.

### [overwhelming](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/overwhelming) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌəʊvəˈwelmɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˌəʊvərˈwelmɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/overw/overwhelming__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/overw/overwhelming__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The evidence against him was overwhelming.
  - The overwhelming majority of those present were in favour of the plan.
  - an overwhelming sense of loss
  - She had the almost overwhelming desire to tell him the truth.
  - You may find it somewhat overwhelming at first.
  - There was overwhelming support for our policies.

- **UK:** `/pɪˈkjuːliə(r)/` · **US:** `/pɪˈkjuːliər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pec/pecul/peculiar__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pec/pecul/peculiar__us_1.mp3)

### [persist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/persist) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈsɪst/` · **US:** `/pərˈsɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/persi/persist__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/persi/persist__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - persist with something He persisted with his questioning.

### [persistent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/persistent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈsɪstənt/` · **US:** `/pərˈsɪstənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/persi/persistent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/persi/persistent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - The pipeline runs through central Mozambique.

### [pirate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pirate_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpaɪrət/` · **US:** `/ˈpaɪrət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pir/pirat/pirate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pir/pirat/pirate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There were reports that a pirate ship had come looking for treasure in the cove.

### [pit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pit_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɪt/` · **US:** `/pɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pit/pit__/pit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pit/pit__/pit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We dug a deep pit in the yard.
  - The body had been dumped in a pit.
  - (figurative) The human mind is a dark, bottomless pit.

### [plea](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plea) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pliː/` · **US:** `/pliː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/ple/plea_/plea__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/ple/plea_/plea__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - plea for something She made an impassioned plea for help.
  - plea (to somebody) (to do something) a plea to industries to stop pollution
  - He refused to listen to her tearful pleas.

### [plead](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plead) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pliːd/` · **US:** `/pliːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/ple/plead/plead__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/ple/plead/plead__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - plead (with somebody) (to do something) She pleaded with him not to go.
  - plead (with somebody) (for something) I was forced to plead for my child's life.
  - pleading eyes
  - plead to do something He pleaded to be allowed to see his mother one more time.
  - + speech ‘Do something!’ she pleaded.

### [pledge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pledge_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pledʒ/` · **US:** `/pledʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/ple/pledg/pledge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/ple/pledg/pledge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pledge (of something) a pledge of support
  - pledge to do something Will the government honour its election pledge not to raise taxes?
  - to fulfil/break/abandon a pledge
  - pledge that… Management has made a pledge that there will be no job losses this year.

### [pledge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pledge_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pledʒ/` · **US:** `/pledʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/ple/pledg/pledge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/ple/pledg/pledge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pledge something Japan has pledged $100 million in humanitarian aid.
  - The government pledged their support for the plan.
  - pledge something to somebody/something We all had to pledge allegiance to the flag (= state that we are loyal to our country).
  - pledge to do something The group has pledged to continue campaigning.
  - pledge (that)… The group has pledged that they will continue campaigning.

### [plug](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plug_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/plʌɡ/` · **US:** `/plʌɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/plu/plug_/plug__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/plu/plug_/plug__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The iron is fitted with a three-pin plug.
  - I'll have to change the plug on my hairdryer.

### [plug](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plug_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/plʌɡ/` · **US:** `/plʌɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/plu/plug_/plug__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/plu/plug_/plug__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He plugged the hole in the pipe with an old rag.
  - Divers succeeded in plugging the leaks from the tanker.

### [plunge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plunge_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/plʌndʒ/` · **US:** `/plʌndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/plu/plung/plunge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/plu/plung/plunge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adv./prep. She lost her balance and plunged 100 feet to her death.
  - The train left the track and plunged down the embankment.
  - plunge somebody/something + adv./prep. The earthquake plunged entire towns over the edge of the cliffs.

### [pole](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pole_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pəʊl/` · **US:** `/pəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pol/pole_/pole__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pol/pole_/pole__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a tent pole
  - a ski pole
  - a curtain pole

### [poll](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/poll_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pəʊl/` · **US:** `/pəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pol/poll_/poll__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pol/poll_/poll__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to carry out/conduct a poll
  - A recent poll suggests some surprising changes in public opinion.
  - A nationwide poll revealed different food preferences in the North and the South.

### [pond](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pond) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɒnd/` · **US:** `/pɑːnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pon/pond_/pond__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pon/pond_/pond__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fish pond

### [pop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pop_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pɒp/` · **US:** `/pɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pop/pop__/pop__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pop/pop__/pop__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the sound of corks popping
  - Flashbulbs were popping all around them.
  - pop something He popped the cork on the champagne bottle.

### [portfolio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/portfolio) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɔːtˈfəʊliəʊ/` · **US:** `/pɔːrtˈfəʊliəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/por/portf/portfolio__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/por/portf/portfolio__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I left my portfolio on the train.

### [portray](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/portray) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pɔːˈtreɪ/` · **US:** `/pɔːrˈtreɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/por/portr/portray__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/por/portr/portray__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The painting portrays the duke’s third wife.
  - He was one of the first to portray animals from the New World.
  - His war poetry vividly portrays life in the trenches.

### [post-war](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/post-war) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌpəʊst ˈwɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌpəʊst ˈwɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pos/post_/post_war_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pos/post_/post_war_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the post-war years
  - in post-war Europe
  - post-war reconstruction

### [postpone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/postpone) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈspəʊn/` · **US:** `/pəʊˈspəʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pos/postp/postpone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pos/postp/postpone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - postpone something The game has already been postponed three times.
  - postpone something until something We'll have to postpone the meeting until next week.
  - postpone something to something They have agreed to postpone repayment of the loan to a future unspecified date.
  - postpone doing something It was an unpopular decision to postpone building the new hospital.

### [practitioner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/practitioner) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prækˈtɪʃənə(r)/` · **US:** `/prækˈtɪʃənər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pra/pract/practitioner__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pra/pract/practitioner__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - dental practitioners
  - a qualified practitioner

### [preach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/preach) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/priːtʃ/` · **US:** `/priːtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preac/preach__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preac/preach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She preached to the congregation about forgiveness.
  - preach something The minister preached a sermon on the parable of the lost sheep.

### [precedent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/precedent) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpresɪdənt/` · **US:** `/ˈpresɪdənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prece/precedent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prece/precedent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The ruling set a precedent for future libel cases.

### [precision](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/precision) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈsɪʒn/` · **US:** `/prɪˈsɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preci/precision__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preci/precision__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her writing is imaginative but lacks precision.
  - with precision He chose his words with precision.
  - done with mathematical precision
  - Historians can’t estimate the date with any (degree of) precision.
  - precision instruments/tools

### [predator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/predator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpredətə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpredətər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preda/predator__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preda/predator__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Some animals have no natural predators.
  - the relationship between predator and prey

### [predecessor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/predecessor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpriːdəsesə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpredəsesər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prede/predecessor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prede/predecessor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The new president reversed many of the policies of his predecessor.
  - his immediate predecessor in the post
  - my predecessor at the Ministry of Defence

### [predominantly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/predominantly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈdɒmɪnəntli/` · **US:** `/prɪˈdɑːmɪnəntli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/predo/predominantly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/predo/predominantly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She works in a predominantly male environment.

### [pregnancy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pregnancy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpreɡnənsi/` · **US:** `/ˈpreɡnənsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/pregn/pregnancy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/pregn/pregnancy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pregnancy test
  - during pregnancy Many women experience sickness during pregnancy.
  - unplanned/unwanted pregnancies
  - the increase in teenage pregnancies

### [prejudice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prejudice_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpredʒədɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈpredʒədɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preju/prejudice__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preju/prejudice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a victim of racial prejudice
  - Their decision was based on ignorance and prejudice.
  - prejudice against somebody/something There was often prejudice against people with disabilities.
  - prejudice in favour of somebody/something She admitted to a prejudice in favour of British universities.

### [preliminary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/preliminary_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈlɪmɪnəri/` · **US:** `/prɪˈlɪmɪneri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preli/preliminary__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preli/preliminary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - After a few preliminary remarks he announced the winners.
  - preliminary results/findings/enquiries
  - the preliminary rounds of the contest
  - preliminary to something pilot studies preliminary to a full-scale study

### [premier](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/premier_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpremiə(r)/` · **US:** `/prɪˈmɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/premi/premier__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/premi/premier__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - one of the country’s premier chefs
  - (British English, sport) the Premier League/Division

### [premise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/premise) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpremɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈpremɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/premi/premise__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/premi/premise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the basic premise of her argument
  - The argument rests on a false premise.
  - His reasoning is based on the premise that all people are equally capable of good and evil.
  - The premise on which they worked was that there would always be enough natural resources to support them.

### [premium](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/premium_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpriːmiəm/` · **US:** `/ˈpriːmiəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/premi/premium__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/premi/premium__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a monthly premium of £6.25

### [prescribe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prescribe) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈskraɪb/` · **US:** `/prɪˈskraɪb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presc/prescribe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presc/prescribe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - prescribe something Valium is usually prescribed to treat anxiety.
  - prescribe (somebody) something (for something) He may be able to prescribe you something for that cough.

### [prescription](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prescription) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈskrɪpʃn/` · **US:** `/prɪˈskrɪpʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presc/prescription__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presc/prescription__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - prescription for something The doctor gave me a prescription for antibiotics.
  - on prescription (British English) Antibiotics are only available on prescription.
  - by prescription (North American English) Antibiotics are only available by prescription.
  - They are not available without a prescription.
  - prescription drugs/medication(s)

### [presently](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/presently) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprezntli/` · **US:** `/ˈprezntli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prese/presently__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prese/presently__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The crime is presently being investigated by the police.
  - These are the courses presently available.

### [preservation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/preservation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprezəˈveɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌprezərˈveɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prese/preservation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prese/preservation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - building/environmental/food preservation
  - a preservation group/society
  - The council has placed a preservation order on the building.

### [preside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/preside) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈzaɪd/` · **US:** `/prɪˈzaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presi/preside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presi/preside__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the presiding judge
  - preside at/over something They asked if I would preside at the committee meeting.
  - (figurative) The party presided over one of the worst economic declines in the country's history (= it was in power when the decline happened).

### [presidency](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/presidency) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprezɪdənsi/` · **US:** `/ˈprezɪdənsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presi/presidency__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presi/presidency__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the current holder of the EU presidency
  - She hopes to be the first woman elected to the presidency of the United States.
  - He was a White House official during the Bush presidency.

### [presidential](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/presidential) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprezɪˈdenʃl/` · **US:** `/ˌprezɪˈdenʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presi/presidential__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presi/presidential__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a presidential campaign/candidate/election
  - a presidential system of government
  - a presidential year (= one in which the people vote for the next president of the country)

### [prestigious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prestigious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/preˈstɪdʒəs/` · **US:** `/preˈstiːdʒəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prest/prestigious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prest/prestigious__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a prestigious award
  - a highly prestigious university
  - My parents wanted me to go to a more prestigious university.
  - the city’s most prestigious and exclusive hotel
  - The Gold Cup is one of the most prestigious events in the racing calendar.

### [presumably](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/presumably) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈzjuːməbli/` · **US:** `/prɪˈzuːməbli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presu/presumably__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presu/presumably__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Presumably this is where the accident happened.
  - You'll be taking the car, presumably?
  - I couldn't concentrate, presumably because I was so tired.
  - Presumably you’ll be leaving the children at home.

### [presume](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/presume) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈzjuːm/` · **US:** `/prɪˈzuːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presu/presume__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presu/presume__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They are very expensive, I presume?
  - ‘Is he still abroad?’ ‘I presume so.’
  - presume (that)… I presumed (that) he understood the rules.
  - it is presumed that… Little is known of the youngest son; it is presumed that he died young.
  - presume somebody/something to be/have something I presumed him to be her husband.

### [prevail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prevail) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈveɪl/` · **US:** `/prɪˈveɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preva/prevail__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preva/prevail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - prevail in something We were horrified at the conditions prevailing in local prisons.
  - prevail among somebody Those beliefs still prevail among certain social groups.

### [prevalence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prevalence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprevələns/` · **US:** `/ˈprevələns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preva/prevalence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preva/prevalence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an increase in the prevalence of smoking among young people

### [prevention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prevention) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈvenʃn/` · **US:** `/prɪˈvenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preve/prevention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preve/prevention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - accident/crime prevention
  - the prevention of disease
  - a fire prevention officer
  - a drug prevention programme

### [prey](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prey_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/preɪ/` · **US:** `/preɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prey_/prey__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prey_/prey__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The lion will often stalk its prey for hours.
  - birds of prey (= birds that kill for food)

### [principal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/principal_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɪnsəpl/` · **US:** `/ˈprɪnsəpl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/princ/principal__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/princ/principal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Peter Brown, principal of St John’s College

### [privatization](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/privatization) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌpraɪvətaɪˈzeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌpraɪvətəˈzeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/priva/privatization__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/priva/privatization__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There were fears that privatization would lead to job losses.

### [privilege](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/privilege_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɪvəlɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈprɪvəlɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/privi/privilege__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/privi/privilege__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Education should be a universal right and not a privilege.
  - You can enjoy all the benefits and privileges of club membership.
  - Try not to abuse the privilege of being allowed more freedom.

### [probe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/probe_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prəʊb/` · **US:** `/prəʊb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/probe/probe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/probe/probe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a police probe into the financial affairs of the company
  - Arson probe after three die at home.

### [probe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/probe_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəʊb/` · **US:** `/prəʊb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/probe/probe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/probe/probe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - probe (into something) He didn't like the media probing into his past.
  - probe something a TV programme that probed government scandals in the 1990s
  - + speech ‘Then what happened?’ he probed.

### [problematic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/problematic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprɒbləˈmætɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌprɑːbləˈmætɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/probl/problematic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/probl/problematic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The situation is more problematic than we first thought.
  - Which are the most problematic countries in the world for journalists?

### [proceeding](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/proceeding) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈsiːdɪŋ/` · **US:** `/prəˈsiːdɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proce/proceeding__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proce/proceeding__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - court/legal/judicial proceedings
  - bankruptcy/divorce/extradition proceedings
  - proceeding (against somebody) (for something) There was enough evidence to bring criminal proceedings against her.
  - The EU has started legal proceeedings against the country for its failure to implement the directive.

### [proceeds](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/proceeds) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊsiːdz/` · **US:** `/ˈprəʊsiːdz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proce/proceeds__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proce/proceeds__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She sold her car and bought a piano with the proceeds.
  - proceeds of/from something The proceeds of the concert will go to charity.

### [processing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/processing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊsesɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːsesɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proce/processing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proce/processing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the food processing industry
  - a sewage processing plant

### [processor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/processor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊsesə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːsesər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proce/processor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proce/processor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The company is Ireland's largest dairy processor.

### [proclaim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/proclaim) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈkleɪm/` · **US:** `/prəˈkleɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/procl/proclaim__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/procl/proclaim__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - proclaim something The president proclaimed a state of emergency.
  - The day was proclaimed a public holiday.
  - proclaim that… The charter proclaimed that all states would have their own government.
  - proclaim somebody/something/yourself + noun He proclaimed himself emperor.
  - proclaim somebody/something/yourself to be/have something Steve checked the battery and proclaimed it to be dead.
  - proclaim how, what, etc… The senator proclaimed how shocked he was at the news.
  - + speech ‘We will succeed,’ she proclaimed.

### [productive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/productive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈdʌktɪv/` · **US:** `/prəˈdʌktɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/produ/productive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/produ/productive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - highly productive farming land
  - productive workers
  - The aim was to maximize the machine’s productive capacity.
  - The bush must be pruned to remain productive.

### [productivity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/productivity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprɒdʌkˈtɪvəti/` · **US:** `/ˌprɑːdʌkˈtɪvəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/produ/productivity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/produ/productivity__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - high/improved/increased productivity
  - Wage rates depend on levels of productivity.
  - the low agricultural productivity of the country

### [profitable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/profitable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɒfɪtəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːfɪtəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/profi/profitable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/profi/profitable__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a highly profitable business
  - a profitable investment
  - It is usually more profitable to sell direct to the public.

### [profound](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/profound) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈfaʊnd/` · **US:** `/prəˈfaʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/profo/profound__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/profo/profound__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - profound changes in the earth’s climate
  - My father's death had a profound effect on us all.
  - The news came as a profound shock.
  - The report has profound implications for schools.
  - Her sense of disappointment was profound.

### [projection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/projection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈdʒekʃn/` · **US:** `/prəˈdʒekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proje/projection__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proje/projection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to make forward/backward projections of population figures
  - Sales have exceeded our projections.
  - Calculations are based on a projection of existing trends.

### [prominent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prominent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɒmɪnənt/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːmɪnənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/promi/prominent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/promi/prominent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a prominent politician
  - He played a prominent part in the campaign.
  - She was prominent in the fashion industry.
  - Last month a group of prominent citizens of the town formed themselves into a committee to deal with the issue.

### [pronounced](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pronounced) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈnaʊnst/` · **US:** `/prəˈnaʊnst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prono/pronounced__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prono/pronounced__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He walked with a pronounced limp.
  - She has very pronounced views on art.

### [propaganda](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/propaganda) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprɒpəˈɡændə/` · **US:** `/ˌprɑːpəˈɡændə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/propa/propaganda__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/propa/propaganda__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - enemy propaganda
  - a propaganda campaign

### [proposition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/proposition_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprɒpəˈzɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌprɑːpəˈzɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/propo/proposition__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/propo/proposition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'd like to put a business proposition to you.
  - He was trying to make it look like an attractive proposition.

### [prosecute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prosecute) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɒsɪkjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːsɪkjuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prose/prosecute__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prose/prosecute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The police decided not to prosecute.
  - prosecute somebody/something Trespassers will be prosecuted (= a notice telling people to keep out of a particular area).
  - prosecute somebody/something for (doing) something The company was prosecuted for breaching the Health and Safety Act.

### [prosecution](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prosecution) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌprɒsɪˈkjuːʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌprɑːsɪˈkjuːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prose/prosecution__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prose/prosecution__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/ˈprɒsɪkjuːtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːsɪkjuːtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prose/prosecutor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prose/prosecutor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [prospective](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prospective) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈspektɪv/` · **US:** `/prəˈspektɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prosp/prospective__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prosp/prospective__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [prosperity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prosperity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prɒˈsperəti/` · **US:** `/prɑːˈsperəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prosp/prosperity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prosp/prosperity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - The country is enjoying a period of peace and prosperity.

### [protective](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/protective) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈtektɪv/` · **US:** `/prəˈtektɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prote/protective__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prote/protective__us_1.mp3)

- **Examples:**

### [provincial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/provincial_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈvɪnʃl/` · **US:** `/prəˈvɪnʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/provi/provincial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/provi/provincial__us_1.mp3)

### [provision](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/provision_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈvɪʒn/` · **US:** `/prəˈvɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/provi/provision__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/provi/provision__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [provoke](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/provoke) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈvəʊk/` · **US:** `/prəˈvəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/provo/provoke__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/provo/provoke__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [psychiatric](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/psychiatric) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsaɪkiˈætrɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌsaɪkiˈætrɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/psy/psych/psychiatric__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/psy/psych/psychiatric__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [pump](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pump_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pʌmp/` · **US:** `/pʌmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pum/pump_/pump__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pum/pump_/pump__us_1.mp3)

  - a foot/hand pump (= that you work by using your foot or hand)
  - a bicycle pump
  - She washed her face at the pump in front of the inn.

### [pump](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pump_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pʌmp/` · **US:** `/pʌmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pum/pump_/pump__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pum/pump_/pump__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pump something (+ adv./prep.) The engine is used for pumping water out of the mine.
  - The heart pumps blood around the body.
  - It took ages to pump out the cellar (= pump the water out of it) after the flood.
  - pump something + adj. The lake had been pumped dry.

### [punch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/punch_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pʌntʃ/` · **US:** `/pʌntʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pun/punch/punch__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pun/punch/punch__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a punch in the face
  - Hill threw a punch at the police officer.
  - a knockout punch
  - He shot out his right arm and landed a punch on Lorrimer's nose.

### [punch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/punch_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pʌntʃ/` · **US:** `/pʌntʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pun/punch/punch__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pun/punch/punch__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - punch somebody/something He was kicked and punched as he lay on the ground.
  - He was punching the air in triumph.
  - punch somebody/something in/on something She punched him on the nose.

### [regime](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/regime) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/reɪˈʒiːm/` · **US:** `/reɪˈʒiːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/reg/regim/regime__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/reg/regim/regime__us_2_rr.mp3)

### [regulator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/regulator) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈreɡjuleɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈreɡjuleɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/reg/regul/regulator__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/reg/regul/regulator__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
