  - banking regulators

### [regulatory](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/regulatory) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈreɡjələtəri/` · **US:** `/ˈreɡjələtɔːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/reg/regul/regulatory__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/reg/regul/regulatory__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - regulatory bodies/authorities/agencies
  - regulatory procedures

### [rehabilitation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rehabilitation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌriːəˌbɪlɪˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌriːəˌbɪlɪˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/reh/rehab/rehabilitation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/reh/rehab/rehabilitation__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a drug rehabilitation centre
  - the rehabilitation of offenders

### [reign](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reign_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/reɪn/` · **US:** `/reɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rei/reign/reign__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rei/reign/reign__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - By the end of his reign, the vast empire was in decline.
  - in/during a reign The house was built during the reign of Henry VIII.

### [reign](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reign_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/reɪn/` · **US:** `/reɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rei/reign/reign__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rei/reign/reign__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was the first visit by a British reigning monarch to Russia.
  - Queen Victoria reigned from 1837 to 1901.
  - reign over somebody/something Herod reigned over Palestine at that time.

### [rejection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rejection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈdʒekʃn/` · **US:** `/rɪˈdʒekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rej/rejec/rejection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rej/rejec/rejection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her proposal met with unanimous rejection.

### [relevance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/relevance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈreləvəns/` · **US:** `/ˈreləvəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rel/relev/relevance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rel/relev/relevance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't see the relevance of your question.
  - relevance to something What he said has no direct relevance to the matter in hand.

### [reliability](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reliability) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˌlaɪəˈbɪləti/` · **US:** `/rɪˌlaɪəˈbɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rel/relia/reliability__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rel/relia/reliability__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The incident cast doubt on her motives and reliability.

### [reluctant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reluctant) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈlʌktənt/` · **US:** `/rɪˈlʌktənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rel/reluc/reluctant__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rel/reluc/reluctant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He finally gave a reluctant smile.
  - They nodded in reluctant agreement.
  - reluctant to do something She was reluctant to admit she was wrong.
  - a reluctant hero (= a person who does not want to be called a hero)

### [remainder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/remainder_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈmeɪndə(r)/` · **US:** `/rɪˈmeɪndər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remai/remainder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remai/remainder__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I kept some of his books and gave away the remainder.

### [remains](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/remains) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈmeɪnz/` · **US:** `/rɪˈmeɪnz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remai/remains__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remai/remains__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She fed the remains of her lunch to the dog.

### [remedy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/remedy_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈremədi/` · **US:** `/ˈremədi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remed/remedy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remed/remedy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - When the reservoir becomes blocked, the only remedy lies in cleaning the entire system.
  - remedy for something There is no simple remedy for unemployment.
  - remedy to something There are a number of possible remedies to this problem.

### [reminder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reminder) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈmaɪndə(r)/` · **US:** `/rɪˈmaɪndər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remin/reminder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remin/reminder__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - reminder of something The sheer size of the cathedral is a constant reminder of the power of religion.
  - The incident served as a timely reminder of just how dangerous mountaineering can be.
  - reminder that… The article concludes with a chilling reminder that the disease can strike anyone at any time.

### [removal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/removal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈmuːvl/` · **US:** `/rɪˈmuːvl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remov/removal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remov/removal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Clearance of the site required the removal of a number of trees.
  - the removal of a tumour
  - Allegations of abuse led to the removal of several children from their families.

### [render](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/render) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrendə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈrendər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ren/rende/render__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ren/rende/render__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to render something harmless/useless/ineffective
  - Hundreds of people were rendered homeless by the earthquake.

### [renew](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/renew) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈnjuː/` · **US:** `/rɪˈnuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ren/renew/renew__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ren/renew/renew__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The army renewed its assault on the capital.
  - We have to renew our efforts to attract young players.
  - The annual dinner is a chance to renew acquaintance with old friends.

### [renowned](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/renowned) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈnaʊnd/` · **US:** `/rɪˈnaʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ren/renow/renowned__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ren/renow/renowned__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a renowned author
  - We asked for advice from the renowned legal expert, Sam Pincher.
  - renowned as something It is renowned as one of the region's best restaurants.
  - renowned for something She is renowned for her patience.

### [rental](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rental) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrentl/` · **US:** `/ˈrentl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ren/renta/rental__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ren/renta/rental__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Telephone charges include line rental.

### [replacement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/replacement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈpleɪsmənt/` · **US:** `/rɪˈpleɪsmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repla/replacement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repla/replacement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the replacement of worn car parts

### [reportedly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reportedly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈpɔːtɪdli/` · **US:** `/rɪˈpɔːrtɪdli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repor/reportedly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repor/reportedly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The band have reportedly decided to split up.
  - The move, reportedly the President’s idea, has come in for a lot of criticism.

### [representation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/representation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌreprɪzenˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌreprɪzenˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repre/representation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repre/representation__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the negative representation of single mothers in the media
  - The snake swallowing its tail is a representation of infinity.

### [reproduce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reproduce) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌriːprəˈdjuːs/` · **US:** `/ˌriːprəˈduːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repro/reproduce__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repro/reproduce__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It is illegal to reproduce these worksheets without permission from the publisher.
  - The photocopier reproduces colours very well.
  - This material can be reproduced without payment.
  - All illustrations are reproduced by kind permission of the Mercury Gallery.

### [reproduction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reproduction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌriːprəˈdʌkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌriːprəˈdʌkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repro/reproduction__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repro/reproduction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sexual reproduction
  - They are studying reproduction in shellfish.

### [republic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/republic) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈpʌblɪk/` · **US:** `/rɪˈpʌblɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repub/republic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repub/republic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - newly independent republics
  - Mary McAleese has been the President of the Republic of Ireland since 1997.

### [resemble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/resemble) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈzembl/` · **US:** `/rɪˈzembl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resem/resemble__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resem/resemble__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She closely resembles her sister.
  - So many hotels resemble each other.
  - The plant resembles grass in appearance.

### [reside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reside) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈzaɪd/` · **US:** `/rɪˈzaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resid/reside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resid/reside__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He returned to Britain in 1939, having resided abroad for many years.
  - Their passports do not give them the right to reside in Britain.

### [residence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/residence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrezɪdəns/` · **US:** `/ˈrezɪdəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resid/residence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resid/residence__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a desirable family residence for sale (= for example, in an advertisement)
  - 10 Downing Street is the British Prime Minister’s official residence.

### [residential](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/residential) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌrezɪˈdenʃl/` · **US:** `/ˌrezɪˈdenʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resid/residential__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resid/residential__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a quiet residential area

### [residue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/residue) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrezɪdjuː/` · **US:** `/ˈrezɪduː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resid/residue__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resid/residue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pesticide residues in fruit and vegetables

### [resignation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/resignation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌrezɪɡˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌrezɪɡˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resig/resignation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resig/resignation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a letter of resignation
  - There were calls for her resignation from the board of directors.
  - Further resignations are expected.
  - The defeat led to his resignation as team manager.

### [resistance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/resistance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈzɪstəns/` · **US:** `/rɪˈzɪstəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resis/resistance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resis/resistance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As with all new ideas it met with resistance.
  - resistance to somebody/something There has been a lot of resistance to this new law.
  - Resistance to change has nearly destroyed the industry.

### [respective](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/respective) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈspektɪv/` · **US:** `/rɪˈspektɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/respe/respective__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/respe/respective__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They are each recognized specialists in their respective fields.
  - the respective roles of men and women in society

### [respectively](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/respectively) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈspektɪvli/` · **US:** `/rɪˈspektɪvli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/respe/respectively__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/respe/respectively__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Julie and Mark, aged 17 and 19 respectively

### [restoration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/restoration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌrestəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌrestəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resto/restoration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resto/restoration__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - restoration work
  - for restoration The palace is closed for restoration.

### [restraint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/restraint) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈstreɪnt/` · **US:** `/rɪˈstreɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/restr/restraint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/restr/restraint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The government has imposed export restraints on some products.
  - social restraints on drinking alcohol

### [resume](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/resume_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈzjuːm/` · **US:** `/rɪˈzuːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/res/resum/resume__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/res/resum/resume__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - resume (something) to resume talks/negotiations
  - She resumed her career after an interval of six years.
  - The noise resumed, louder than before.
  - There is no sign of the peace talks resuming.
  - resume doing something He got back in the car and resumed driving.

### [retreat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/retreat_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈtriːt/` · **US:** `/rɪˈtriːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ret/retre/retreat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ret/retre/retreat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Napoleon’s retreat from Moscow
  - The army was in full retreat (= retreating very quickly).
  - to sound the retreat (= to give a loud signal for an army to move away)

### [retreat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/retreat_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈtriːt/` · **US:** `/rɪˈtriːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ret/retre/retreat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ret/retre/retreat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The army was forced to retreat after suffering heavy losses.
  - We retreated back down the mountain.
  - They retreated to a safe distance from the fighting.

### [retrieve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/retrieve) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈtriːv/` · **US:** `/rɪˈtriːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/ret/retri/retrieve__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/ret/retri/retrieve__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - retrieve something from somebody/something She bent to retrieve her comb from the floor.
  - The dog retrieved the ball from the water.
  - retrieve something The police have managed to retrieve some of the stolen money.

### [revelation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/revelation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌrevəˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌrevəˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/revel/revelation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/revel/revelation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - revelation about/concerning something startling/sensational revelations about her private life
  - revelation that… He was dismissed after revelations that confidential files were missing.

### [revenge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/revenge_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈvendʒ/` · **US:** `/rɪˈvendʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/reven/revenge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/reven/revenge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - revenge for something She is seeking revenge for the murder of her husband.
  - The bombing was an act of revenge for the shooting of two young boys.
  - in revenge for something The bombing was in revenge for the assassination.
  - revenge on somebody/something He swore to take (his) revenge on his political enemies.
  - revenge attacks/killings

### [reverse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reverse_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈvɜːs/` · **US:** `/rɪˈvɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/rever/reverse__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/rever/reverse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to travel in the reverse direction
  - The winners were announced in reverse order (= the person in the lowest place was announced first).
  - The experiment had the reverse effect to what was intended.
  - One year later she made the same journey in the reverse direction.

### [reverse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reverse_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈvɜːs/` · **US:** `/rɪˈvɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/rever/reverse__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/rever/reverse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This problem is the reverse of the previous one.
  - Although I expected to enjoy living in the country, in fact the reverse is true.
  - In the south, the reverse applies.
  - It wasn't easy to persuade her to come—quite the reverse.
  - If you tell children to do something, they will often do the exact reverse.

### [reverse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reverse_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈvɜːs/` · **US:** `/rɪˈvɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/rever/reverse__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/rever/reverse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Falling birth rates may reverse the trend towards early retirement.
  - to reverse a procedure/process
  - The government has failed to reverse the economic decline.
  - It is sometimes possible to arrest or reverse the disease.

### [revival](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/revival) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈvaɪvl/` · **US:** `/rɪˈvaɪvl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/reviv/revival__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/reviv/revival__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the revival of trade
  - an economic revival
  - a revival of property values

### [revive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/revive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈvaɪv/` · **US:** `/rɪˈvaɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/reviv/revive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/reviv/revive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The flowers soon revived in water.
  - The economy is beginning to revive.
  - revive somebody/something The paramedics couldn't revive her.
  - This movie is intended to revive her flagging career.
  - Attempts to revive him failed.

### [revolutionary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/revolutionary_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌrevəˈluːʃənəri/` · **US:** `/ˌrevəˈluːʃəneri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rev/revol/revolutionary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rev/revol/revolutionary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a revolutionary leader
  - revolutionary uprisings

### [rhetoric](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rhetoric) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈretərɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈretərɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rhe/rheto/rhetoric__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rhe/rheto/rhetoric__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the rhetoric of political slogans
  - Her speech was just empty rhetoric.
  - His speech was dismissed as mere rhetoric by the opposition.

### [rifle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rifle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈraɪfl/` · **US:** `/ˈraɪfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rif/rifle/rifle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rif/rifle/rifle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a hunting rifle
  - an assault rifle

### [riot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/riot_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈraɪət/` · **US:** `/ˈraɪət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rio/riot_/riot__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rio/riot_/riot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - One prison guard was killed when a riot broke out in the jail.
  - He was killed in the riots.
  - Shortages eventually led to food riots.

### [rip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rip_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪp/` · **US:** `/rɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rip/rip__/rip__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rip/rip__/rip__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - rip (something) I ripped my jeans on the fence.
  - The flags had been ripped in two.
  - The nail ripped a hole in my jacket.
  - I heard the tent rip.
  - rip something + adj. She ripped the letter open.

### [ritual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ritual_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrɪtʃuəl/` · **US:** `/ˈrɪtʃuəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rit/ritua/ritual__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rit/ritua/ritual__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - religious rituals
  - She objects to the ritual of organized religion.

### [robust](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/robust) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rəʊˈbʌst/` · **US:** `/rəʊˈbʌst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rob/robus/robust__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rob/robus/robust__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was almost 90, but still very robust.
  - He seems to be in robust (good) health.

### [rock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rock_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɒk/` · **US:** `/rɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/roc/rock_/rock__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/roc/rock_/rock__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (+ adv./prep.) The boat rocked from side to side in the waves.
  - She was rocking backwards and forwards in her seat.
  - rock somebody/something (+ adv./prep.) He rocked the baby gently in his arms.

### [rod](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rod_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɒd/` · **US:** `/rɑːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rod/rod__/rod__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rod/rod__/rod__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The concrete is reinforced with steel rods.
  - I finally managed to fix the curtain rods above the windows.

### [rotate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rotate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rəʊˈteɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈrəʊteɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rot/rotat/rotate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rot/rotat/rotate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Stay well away from the helicopter when its blades start to rotate.
  - rotate about/around something winds rotating around the eye of a hurricane
  - rotate something Rotate the wheel through 180 degrees.

### [rotation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rotation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rəʊˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/rəʊˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rot/rotat/rotation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rot/rotat/rotation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the daily rotation of the earth on its axis
  - the earth's rotation around the sun

### [ruling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ruling_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈruːlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈruːlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rul/rulin/ruling__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rul/rulin/ruling__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The court will make its ruling on the case next week.
  - The company intends to appeal against the ruling that it was negligent.

### [rumour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rumour_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈruːmə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈruːmər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rum/rumou/rumour__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rum/rumou/rumour__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to start/spread a rumour
  - rumour of something There are widespread rumours of job losses.
  - rumour about something Some malicious rumours are circulating about his past.
  - rumour that… I heard a rumour that they are getting married.
  - Many of the stories are based on rumour.
  - Rumour has it (= people say) that he was murdered.
  - I thought she was leaving the company, but perhaps it may be just a rumour.


## S

### [sack](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sack_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sæk/` · **US:** `/sæk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sac/sack_/sack__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sac/sack_/sack__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was sacked for refusing to work on Sundays.

### [sacred](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sacred) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈseɪkrɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈseɪkrɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sac/sacre/sacred__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sac/sacre/sacred__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sacred image/shrine/temple
  - sacred music
  - Cows are sacred to Hindus.

### [sacrifice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sacrifice_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsækrɪfaɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈsækrɪfaɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sac/sacri/sacrifice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sac/sacri/sacrifice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The makers of the product assured us that there had been no sacrifice of quality.
  - Her parents made sacrifices so that she could have a good education.
  - to make the ultimate/supreme sacrifice (= to die for your country, to save a friend, etc.)

### [sacrifice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sacrifice_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsækrɪfaɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈsækrɪfaɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sac/sacri/sacrifice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sac/sacri/sacrifice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sacrifice something for somebody/something She sacrificed everything for her children.
  - The designers have sacrificed speed for fuel economy.
  - sacrifice something Would you sacrifice a football game to go out with a girl?
  - sacrifice something to something In her writing, clarity is sometimes sacrificed to brevity.

### [saint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/saint) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/snt/` · **US:** `/seɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sai/saint/saint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sai/saint/saint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - St John
  - St Valentine’s Day
  - The children were all named after saints.

### [sake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sake1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/seɪk/` · **US:** `/seɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sak/sake_/sake__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sak/sake_/sake__us_1.mp3)

### [sanction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sanction_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsæŋkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈsæŋkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/san/sanct/sanction__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/san/sanct/sanction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Trade sanctions were imposed against any country that refused to sign the agreement.
  - The economic sanctions have been lifted.

### [say](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/say_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/seɪ/` · **US:** `/seɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/say/say__/say__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/say/say__/say__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - say (in something) We had no say in the decision to sell the company.
  - People want a greater say in local government.
  - say on something The judge has the final say on the sentence.

### [scattered](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/scattered) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈskætəd/` · **US:** `/ˈskætərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sca/scatt/scattered__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sca/scatt/scattered__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a few scattered settlements
  - sunshine with scattered showers
  - Her family are scattered around the world.

### [sceptical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sceptical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈskeptɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈskeptɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sce/scept/sceptical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sce/scept/sceptical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She looked highly sceptical.
  - sceptical about something I am sceptical about his chances of winning.
  - sceptical of something The public remain sceptical of these claims.

### [scope](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/scope_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/skəʊp/` · **US:** `/skəʊp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sco/scope/scope__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sco/scope/scope__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - scope for something There's still plenty of scope for improvement.
  - Her job offers very little scope for promotion.
  - scope (for somebody) (to do something) The extra money will give us the scope to improve our facilities.
  - within somebody's scope First try to do something that is within your scope.

### [screw](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/screw_2) *(noun)*

### [screw](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/screw_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/skruː/` · **US:** `/skruː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/scr/screw/screw__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/scr/screw/screw__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The bookcase is screwed to the wall.
  - You need to screw all the parts together.
  - Now screw down the lid.

### [scrutiny](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/scrutiny) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈskruːtəni/` · **US:** `/ˈskruːtəni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/scr/scrut/scrutiny__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/scr/scrut/scrutiny__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Foreign policy has come under close scrutiny recently.
  - The documents should be available for public scrutiny.
  - The situation is bound to come under the scrutiny of the public health authorities.

### [seal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/seal_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/siːl/` · **US:** `/siːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sea/seal_/seal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sea/seal_/seal__us_1.mp3)

### [segment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/segment_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈseɡmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈseɡmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/seg/segme/segment__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/seg/segme/segment__us_1.mp3)

  - They divided the bone into equal segments.

### [seize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/seize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/siːz/` · **US:** `/siːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sei/seize/seize__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sei/seize/seize__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - seize something from somebody She tried to seize the gun from him.
  - seize somebody/something He seized her by the arm.
  - She seized hold of my hand.

### [seldom](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/seldom) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈseldəm/` · **US:** `/ˈseldəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sel/seldo/seldom__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sel/seldo/seldom__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He had seldom seen a child with so much talent.
  - She seldom, if ever, goes to the theatre.
  - They seldom watch television these days.
  - (literary) Seldom had he seen such beauty.

### [selective](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/selective) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/sɪˈlektɪv/` · **US:** `/sɪˈlektɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sel/selec/selective__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sel/selec/selective__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - selective strike action
  - the socially selective nature of population changes

### [senator](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/senator) *(noun)*

### [sensation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sensation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/senˈseɪʃn/` · **US:** `/senˈseɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sen/sensa/sensation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sen/sensa/sensation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [sensitivity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sensitivity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsensəˈtɪvəti/` · **US:** `/ˌsensəˈtɪvəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sen/sensi/sensitivity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sen/sensi/sensitivity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [sentiment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sentiment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsentɪmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈsentɪmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sen/senti/sentiment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sen/senti/sentiment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the spread of nationalist sentiments
  - This is a sentiment I wholeheartedly agree with.
  - Public sentiment is against any change to the law.
  - My sentiments exactly! (= I agree)

### [separation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/separation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsepəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌsepəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sep/separ/separation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sep/separ/separation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - separation from somebody/something the state’s eventual separation from the federation

### [serial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/serial_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪəriəl/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪriəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/ser/seria/serial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/ser/seria/serial__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a serial rapist

### [set-up](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/set-up_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈset ʌp/` · **US:** `/ˈset ʌp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/set/set_u/set_up_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/set/set_u/set_up_1_us_1.mp3)

  - What’s the set-up in your family—who does the cooking?

### [settlement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/settlement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsetlmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈsetlmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/set/settl/settlement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/set/settl/settlement__us_1.mp3)

### [sexuality](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sexuality) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsekʃuˈæləti/` · **US:** `/ˌsekʃuˈæləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sex/sexua/sexuality__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sex/sexua/sexuality__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was confused about his sexuality.
  - male/female sexuality
  - the psychology of human sexuality
  - Her overt sexuality shocked cinema audiences.
  - Victorian women were rarely allowed to express their sexuality.

### [shareholder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/shareholder) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʃeəhəʊldə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈʃerhəʊldər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sha/share/shareholder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sha/share/shareholder__us_1.mp3)

### [shatter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/shatter) *(verb)*

- **Examples:**
  - shatter (into something) He dropped the vase and it shattered into pieces on the floor.
  - The mirror shattered into a thousand pieces.
  - the sound of shattering glass
  - shatter something (into something) The explosion shattered all the windows in the building.

### [shed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/shed_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ʃed/` · **US:** `/ʃed/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/she/shed_/shed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/she/shed_/shed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [sheer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sheer_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʃɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ʃɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/she/sheer/sheer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/she/sheer/sheer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [shipping](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/shipping) *(noun)*

### [sigh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sigh_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/saɪ/` · **US:** `/saɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sig/sigh_/sigh__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sig/sigh_/sigh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [sigh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sigh_1) *(verb)*

- **Examples:**
  - He sighed deeply at the thought.
  - sigh with something She sighed with relief that it was all over.

### [simulate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/simulate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪmjuleɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪmjuleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sim/simul/simulate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sim/simul/simulate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Role-playing is a useful way of simulating real-life situations.
  - The device simulates conditions in space quite closely.

### [simulation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/simulation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsɪmjuˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌsɪmjuˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sim/simul/simulation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sim/simul/simulation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - a simulation model

### [simultaneously](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/simultaneously) *(adverb)*

- **Examples:**

### [sin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sin_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sɪn/` · **US:** `/sɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sin/sin__/sin__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sin/sin__/sin__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [situated](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/situated) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪtʃueɪtɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪtʃueɪtɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sit/situa/situated__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sit/situa/situated__us_1.mp3)

- **Examples:**

  - Lambs were skipping about in the fields.

### [slam](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/slam_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/slæm/` · **US:** `/slæm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sla/slam_/slam__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sla/slam_/slam__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I heard the door slam behind him.
  - + adj. A window slammed shut in the wind.
  - slam something He stormed out of the house, slamming the door as he left.
  - slam something + adj. She slammed the lid shut.
  - + adv./prep. She slammed out of the room (= went out and slammed the door behind her).

### [slap](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/slap_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/slæp/` · **US:** `/slæp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sla/slap_/slap__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sla/slap_/slap__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She slapped his face hard.
  - She slapped him hard across the face.
  - ‘Congratulations!’ he said, slapping me on the back.

### [slash](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/slash_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/slæʃ/` · **US:** `/slæʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sla/slash/slash__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sla/slash/slash__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - We had to slash our way through the undergrowth with sticks.
  - One of the men slashed him across the face with a knife.
  - slash at somebody/something He slashed wildly at me with a knife.
  - He slashed at his opponent with his sword.

### [slavery](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/slavery) *(noun)*

### [snap](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/snap_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/snæp/` · **US:** `/snæp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sna/snap_/snap__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sna/snap_/snap__us_1.mp3)

### [soak](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/soak_1) *(verb)*

- **Examples:**
  - soak something (in something) I usually soak the beans overnight.
  - If you soak the tablecloth before you wash it, the stains should come out.
  - soak something with something He soaked the cloth with petrol.
  - soak (in something) Leave the apricots to soak for 20 minutes.

  - Soak the clothes in cold water.

### [sole](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sole_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səʊl/` · **US:** `/səʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/sole_/sole__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/sole_/sole__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [solely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solely) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsəʊlli/` · **US:** `/ˈsəʊlli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solel/solely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solel/solely__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [solicitor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solicitor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈlɪsɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/səˈlɪsɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solic/solicitor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solic/solicitor__us_1.mp3)

### [solidarity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solidarity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsɒlɪˈdærəti/` · **US:** `/ˌsɑːlɪˈdærəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solid/solidarity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solid/solidarity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [solo](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solo_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsəʊləʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈsəʊləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solo_/solo__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solo_/solo__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [solo](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solo_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsəʊləʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈsəʊləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solo_/solo__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solo_/solo__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [sound](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sound_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/saʊnd/` · **US:** `/saʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sou/sound/sound__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sou/sound/sound__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's a person of very sound judgement.
  - He gave me some very sound advice.
  - This gives the design team a sound basis for their work.
  - The proposal makes sound commercial sense.
  - Their policies are environmentally sound.

### [sovereignty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sovereignty) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɒvrənti/` · **US:** `/ˈsɑːvrənti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sov/sover/sovereignty__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sov/sover/sovereignty__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The country claimed sovereignty over the island.
  - This constitutes an attack on the sovereignty of Parliament.

### [spam](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spam_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/spæm/` · **US:** `/spæm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spa/spam_/spam__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spa/spam_/spam__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to send/block spam
  - I get thousands of spam emails each month.
  - Tougher laws and improved spam filters can help cut down on spam reaching your inbox.
  - The guidelines warn against posting spam.

### [span](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/span_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/spæn/` · **US:** `/spæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spa/span_/span__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spa/span_/span__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I worked with him over a span of six years.
  - The project must be completed within a specific time span.
  - a study of the entire span of Arab history

### [span](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/span_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/spæn/` · **US:** `/spæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spa/span_/span__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spa/span_/span__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His acting career spanned 55 years.
  - Family photos spanning five generations were stolen.

### [spare](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spare_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/speə(r)/` · **US:** `/sper/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spa/spare/spare__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spa/spare/spare__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - spare something/somebody I'd love to have a break, but I can't spare the time just now.
  - spare something/somebody to do something Could you spare one of your staff to help us out?
  - spare something/somebody for somebody/something We can only spare one room for you.
  - You should spare a thought for (= think about) the person who cleans up after you.
  - He was late, but he spared a glance for the pretty girl at the desk.
  - spare somebody something Surely you can spare me a few minutes?

### [spark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spark_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/spɑːk/` · **US:** `/spɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spa/spark/spark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spa/spark/spark__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - spark something The proposal would spark a storm of protest around the country.
  - Winds brought down power lines, sparking a fire.
  - The organizers are hoping to spark some interest in young people.
  - spark something off The riots were sparked off by the arrest of a local leader.

### [specialized](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/specialized) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈspeʃəlaɪzd/` · **US:** `/ˈspeʃəlaɪzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speci/specialized__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speci/specialized__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - specialized equipment
  - a job calling for highly specialized skills
  - Our industry is quite specialized.

### [specification](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/specification) *(noun)*

  - The office was furnished to a high specification.

### [specimen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/specimen) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈspesɪmən/` · **US:** `/ˈspesɪmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speci/specimen__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speci/specimen__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈspektəkl/` · **US:** `/ˈspektəkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/spect/spectacle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/spect/spectacle__us_1.mp3)

### [spin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spin_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/spɪn/` · **US:** `/spɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spi/spin_/spin__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spi/spin_/spin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (+ adv./prep.) The plane was spinning out of control.

  - spin something (round/around) to spin a ball/coin/wheel
  - We placed our bets and the croupier spun the roulette wheel.

### [stake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stake_1) *(noun)*

### [stark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stark_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/stɑːk/` · **US:** `/stɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stark/stark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stark/stark__us_1.mp3)

### [stem](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stem_3) *(verb)*
