### [stereotype](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stereotype_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsteriətaɪp/` · **US:** `/ˈsteriətaɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/ste/stere/stereotype__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/ste/stere/stereotype__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cultural/gender/racial stereotypes
  - He doesn't conform to the usual stereotype of the businessman with a dark suit and briefcase.

### [stimulus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stimulus) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstɪmjələs/` · **US:** `/ˈstɪmjələs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sti/stimu/stimulus__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sti/stimu/stimulus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - stimulus for something Books provide children with ideas and a stimulus for play.
  - stimulus to something The new tax laws should act as a stimulus to exports.
  - stimulus (for somebody) (to do something) This development also acted as a stimulus for Britain to modernize her air force.

### [stir](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stir_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/stɜː(r)/` · **US:** `/stɜːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sti/stir_/stir__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sti/stir_/stir__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - stir something She stirred her tea.
  - Stir the paint before you use it.
  - stir something into something The vegetables are stirred into the rice while it is hot.
  - stir something in Stir in the milk until the sauce thickens.

### [storage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/storage) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstɔːrɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈstɔːrɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sto/stora/storage__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sto/stora/storage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tables that fold flat for storage
  - There's a lot of storage space in the loft.
  - food storage facilities
  - We need more storage now.

### [straightforward](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/straightforward) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌstreɪtˈfɔːwəd/` · **US:** `/ˌstreɪtˈfɔːrwərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strai/straightforward__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strai/straightforward__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a relatively straightforward process.
  - It's quite straightforward to get here.

### [strain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strain_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/streɪn/` · **US:** `/streɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strai/strain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strai/strain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The transport service cannot cope with the strain of so many additional passengers.
  - under strain Their marriage is under great strain at the moment.
  - strain on something These repayments are putting a strain on our finances.
  - There are strains in the relationship between the two countries.

### [strand](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strand_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/strænd/` · **US:** `/strænd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/stran/strand__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/stran/strand__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He pulled at a loose strand of wool in his sweater.
  - a few strands of dark hair
  - She wore a single strand of pearls around her neck.

### [strategic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strategic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/strəˈtiːdʒɪk/` · **US:** `/strəˈtiːdʒɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strat/strategic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strat/strategic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - strategic planning
  - a strategic decision to sell off part of the business
  - Cameras were set up at strategic points (= in places where they would be most effective) along the route.

### [striking](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/striking) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstraɪkɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈstraɪkɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strik/striking__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strik/striking__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a striking feature
  - She bears a striking resemblance to her older sister.
  - In striking contrast to their brothers, the girls were both intelligent and charming.
  - The similarities between the two cases are striking.

### [strip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strip_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/strɪp/` · **US:** `/strɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strip/strip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strip/strip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a strip of material
  - Cut the meat into strips.

### [strip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strip_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/strɪp/` · **US:** `/strɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strip/strip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strip/strip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I stripped and washed myself all over.
  - They ordered us to strip and lie down on the grass.
  - strip down to something She stripped down to her underwear.
  - strip (something) off We stripped off and ran down to the water.
  - She stripped off her clothes and got into bed.
  - strip somebody (to something) He stood there stripped to the waist (= he had no clothes on the upper part of his body).
  - strip somebody + adj. He was stripped naked and left in a cell.

### [strive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/straɪv/` · **US:** `/straɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/striv/strive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/striv/strive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - strive for something We encourage all members to strive for the highest standards.
  - strive after/towards something They were always striving towards perfection.
  - strive against something striving against corruption
  - strive to do something Newspaper editors all strive to be first with a story.
  - She strove to find a solution that was acceptable to all.

### [structural](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/structural) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstrʌktʃərəl/` · **US:** `/ˈstrʌktʃərəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/struc/structural__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/struc/structural__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Storms have caused structural damage to hundreds of homes.
  - structural changes in society
  - a structural survey (= an examination of a building to check for any damage to the walls, roof, etc.)
  - There is an urgent need to address structural racism within the organization.

### [stumble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stumble_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstʌmbl/` · **US:** `/ˈstʌmbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/stu/stumb/stumble__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/stu/stumb/stumble__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The child stumbled and fell.
  - stumble over/on something I stumbled over a rock.

### [stun](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stun) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/stʌn/` · **US:** `/stʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/stu/stun_/stun__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/stu/stun_/stun__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The fall stunned me for a moment.
  - The animals are stunned before slaughter.

### [submission](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/submission) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səbˈmɪʃn/` · **US:** `/səbˈmɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/submi/submission__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/submi/submission__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a gesture of submission
  - to beat/force/starve somebody into submission
  - His response was one of resistance, not submission.

### [subscriber](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/subscriber) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səbˈskraɪbə(r)/` · **US:** `/səbˈskraɪbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subsc/subscriber__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subsc/subscriber__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - subscribers to ‘New Scientist’
  - Subscribers to the magazine can take advantage of this special offer.

### [subscription](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/subscription) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səbˈskrɪpʃn/` · **US:** `/səbˈskrɪpʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subsc/subscription__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subsc/subscription__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an annual subscription
  - subscription to/for something a subscription to Netflix
  - a monthly subscription to Oxfam
  - to take out a subscription to ‘Newsweek’
  - to cancel/renew a subscription
  - by subscription Copies are available by subscription.
  - Subscriptions are due annually by the end of January.

### [subsidy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/subsidy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌbsədi/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌbsədi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subsi/subsidy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subsi/subsidy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - agricultural subsidies
  - to reduce the level of subsidy

### [substantial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/substantial) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səbˈstænʃl/` · **US:** `/səbˈstænʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subst/substantial__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subst/substantial__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - substantial sums of money
  - a substantial change
  - Substantial numbers of people support the reforms.
  - He ate a substantial breakfast.
  - There are substantial differences between the two groups.
  - Their share of the software market is substantial.
  - We were able to see a substantial improvement.

### [substantially](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/substantially) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/səbˈstænʃəli/` · **US:** `/səbˈstænʃəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subst/substantially__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subst/substantially__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The costs have increased substantially.
  - The plane was substantially damaged in the crash.
  - The company’s profits have been substantially lower this year.

### [substitute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/substitute_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌbstɪtjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌbstɪtuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subst/substitute__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subst/substitute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a meat substitute
  - a substitute family
  - substitute for somebody/something Paul's father only saw him as a substitute for his dead brother.
  - The course teaches you the theory but there's no substitute for practical experience.
  - The local bus service was a poor substitute for their car.

### [substitute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/substitute_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌbstɪtjuːt/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌbstɪtuːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subst/substitute__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subst/substitute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - substitute for somebody/something Nothing can substitute for the advice your doctor is able to give you.
  - Can you find someone to substitute for you at the meeting?
  - substitute A for B Margarine can be substituted for butter in this recipe.
  - substitute B with/by A Butter can be substituted with margarine in this recipe.
  - substitute somebody/something Kane was substituted in the second half after a knee injury (= somebody else played instead of Kane in the second half).

### [substitution](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/substitution) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsʌbstɪˈtjuːʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌsʌbstɪˈtuːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subst/substitution__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subst/substitution__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Two substitutions were made during the game.
  - substitution of A for B the substitution of low-fat spreads for butter
  - substitution of B with A the substitution of butter with low-fat spreads

### [subtle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/subtle) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌtl/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subtl/subtle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subtl/subtle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - subtle colours/flavours/smells, etc.
  - There are subtle differences between the two versions.
  - The fragrance is a subtle blend of jasmine and sandalwood.
  - She's been dropping subtle hints about what she'd like as a present.

### [suburban](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suburban) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səˈbɜːbən/` · **US:** `/səˈbɜːrbən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subur/suburban__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subur/suburban__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - suburban areas
  - a suburban street
  - life in suburban London

### [succession](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/succession) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səkˈseʃn/` · **US:** `/səkˈseʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/succe/succession__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/succe/succession__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a succession of visitors
  - He's been hit by a succession of injuries since he joined the team.
  - in succession She has won the award for the third year in succession.
  - They had three children in quick succession.
  - The gunman fired three times in rapid succession.

### [successive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/successive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səkˈsesɪv/` · **US:** `/səkˈsesɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/succe/successive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/succe/successive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This was their fourth successive win.
  - Successive governments have tried to tackle the problem.
  - There has been low rainfall for two successive years.

### [successor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/successor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səkˈsesə(r)/` · **US:** `/səkˈsesər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/succe/successor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/succe/successor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who's the likely successor to him as party leader?
  - Their latest release is a worthy successor to their popular debut album.
  - He chose as his successor a relative newcomer to the organization.

### [suck](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suck_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sʌk/` · **US:** `/sʌk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/suck_/suck__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/suck_/suck__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to suck the juice from an orange
  - She was noisily sucking up milk through a straw.
  - He sucked the blood from a cut on his finger.

### [sue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sue_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/suː/` · **US:** `/suː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sue/sue__/sue__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sue/sue__/sue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They threatened to sue if the work was not completed.
  - sue (somebody/something) for something to sue somebody for breach of contract
  - to sue somebody for $10 million (= in order to get money from somebody)
  - to sue somebody for damages
  - He threatened to sue the company for negligence.
  - sue somebody/something The water authority was successfully sued over his illness.

### [suicide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suicide) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsuːɪsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈsuːɪsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sui/suici/suicide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sui/suici/suicide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to commit suicide
  - an attempted suicide (= one in which the person survives)
  - a suicide bomber (= who expects to die while trying to kill other people with a bomb)

### [suite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suite) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/swiːt/` · **US:** `/swiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sui/suite/suite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sui/suite/suite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a hotel/private/honeymoon suite
  - a suite of rooms/offices

### [summit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/summit) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌmɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌmɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sum/summi/summit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sum/summi/summit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We reached the summit at noon.
  - This path leads to the summit.
  - (figurative) the summit of his career

### [superb](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/superb) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/suːˈpɜːb/` · **US:** `/suːˈpɜːrb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/super/superb__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/super/superb__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a superb player
  - The car's in superb condition.
  - His performance was absolutely superb.
  - You look superb.

### [superior](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/superior_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/suːˈpɪəriə(r)/` · **US:** `/suːˈpɪriər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/super/superior__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/super/superior__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - vastly superior
  - superior intelligence
  - Liverpool were clearly the superior team.
  - superior to somebody/something This model is technically superior to its competitors.
  - She felt socially superior to most of her neighbours.
  - The enemy won because of their superior numbers (= there were more of them).

### [supervise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supervise) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsuːpəvaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈsuːpərvaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/super/supervise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/super/supervise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - supervise (somebody/something) to supervise building work
  - Who is supervising?
  - supervise somebody doing something She supervised the children playing near the pool.

### [supervision](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supervision) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsuːpəˈvɪʒn/` · **US:** `/ˌsuːpərˈvɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/super/supervision__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/super/supervision__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Very young children should not be left to play without supervision.
  - under supervision The drug should only be used under medical supervision.

### [supervisor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supervisor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsuːpəvaɪzə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈsuːpərvaɪzər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/super/supervisor__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/super/supervisor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I have a meeting with my supervisor about my research topic.
  - All work is done under the guidance of a supervisor.

### [supplement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supplement_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌplɪmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌplɪmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppl/supplement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppl/supplement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - vitamin/dietary supplements (= vitamins and other foods eaten in addition to what you usually eat)
  - supplement to something Industrial sponsorship is a supplement to government funding.

### [supplement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supplement_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌplɪment/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌplɪment/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppl/supplement__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppl/supplement__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - supplement something with something a diet supplemented with vitamin pills
  - supplement something He supplements his income by giving private lessons.

### [supportive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supportive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpɔːtɪv/` · **US:** `/səˈpɔːrtɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppo/supportive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppo/supportive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a supportive family
  - She was very supportive during my father's illness.
  - a mutually supportive relationship
  - He was strongly supportive of my career.

### [supposedly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supposedly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpəʊzɪdli/` · **US:** `/səˈpəʊzɪdli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppo/supposedly__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppo/supposedly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The novel is supposedly based on a true story.
  - a poem supposedly written by Shakespeare
  - Police have found the supposedly stolen car.

### [suppress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suppress) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpres/` · **US:** `/səˈpres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppr/suppress__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppr/suppress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The rebellion was brutally suppressed.

### [supreme](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supreme) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/suˈpriːm/` · **US:** `/suˈpriːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/supre/supreme__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/supre/supreme__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Supreme Commander of the armed forces
  - the supreme champion
  - It is an event in which she reigns supreme.

### [surge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surge_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sɜːdʒ/` · **US:** `/sɜːrdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surge/surge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surge/surge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She felt a sudden surge of anger.
  - a surge of excitement

### [surge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surge_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sɜːdʒ/` · **US:** `/sɜːrdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surge/surge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surge/surge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The gates opened and the crowd surged forward.
  - Flood waters surged into their homes.
  - He surged past the other runners on the last lap.

### [surgical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surgical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːdʒɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrdʒɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surgi/surgical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surgi/surgical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - surgical procedures
  - a surgical ward (= for patients having operations)

### [surplus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surplus_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːpləs/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrplʌs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surpl/surplus__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surpl/surplus__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - food surpluses
  - in surplus Wheat was in surplus that year.

### [surrender](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surrender_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈrendə(r)/` · **US:** `/səˈrendər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surre/surrender__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surre/surrender__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The rebel soldiers were forced to surrender.
  - surrender to somebody He surrendered voluntarily to his enemies.
  - surrender yourself (to somebody) The hijackers eventually surrendered themselves to the police.

### [surveillance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surveillance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sɜːˈveɪləns/` · **US:** `/sɜːrˈveɪləns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surve/surveillance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surve/surveillance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The police are keeping the suspects under constant surveillance.
  - surveillance cameras/equipment

### [suspension](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suspension) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈspenʃn/` · **US:** `/səˈspenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sus/suspe/suspension__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sus/suspe/suspension__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - suspension from school
  - The two players are appealing against their suspensions.

### [suspicion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suspicion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈspɪʃn/` · **US:** `/səˈspɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sus/suspi/suspicion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sus/suspi/suspicion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They drove away slowly to avoid arousing suspicion.
  - on suspicion of something He was arrested on suspicion of murder.
  - suspicion that… I have a sneaking suspicion that she's not telling the truth.
  - My suspicions were confirmed when police raided the property.
  - She was reluctant to voice her suspicions.
  - His resignation seemed only to fuel suspicions.
  - It’s time to confront him with our suspicions.

### [suspicious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suspicious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səˈspɪʃəs/` · **US:** `/səˈspɪʃəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sus/suspi/suspicious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sus/suspi/suspicious__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They became suspicious of his behaviour and contacted the police.
  - a suspicious look
  - You have a very suspicious mind (= you always think that people are behaving in an illegal or dishonest way).

### [sustain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sustain) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈsteɪn/` · **US:** `/səˈsteɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sus/susta/sustain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sus/susta/sustain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which planets can sustain life?
  - I only had a little chocolate to sustain me on my walk.
  - The love and support of his family sustained him during his time in prison.
  - When she lost her job they could no longer sustain their expensive lifestyle.
  - The soil was so badly eroded it could no longer sustain crop production.

### [swing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/swing_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/swɪŋ/` · **US:** `/swɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swi/swing/swing__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swi/swing/swing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He took a wild swing at the ball.
  - the swing of her hips

### [swing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/swing_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/swɪŋ/` · **US:** `/swɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swi/swing/swing__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swi/swing/swing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His arms swung as he walked.
  - As he pushed her, she swung higher and higher (= while sitting on a swing).
  - swing from something A set of keys swung from her belt.
  - swing something He sat on the stool, swinging his legs.

### [sword](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sword) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sɔːd/` · **US:** `/sɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swo/sword/sword__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swo/sword/sword__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to draw/sheathe a sword (= to take it out of/put it into its cover)

### [symbolic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/symbolic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/sɪmˈbɒlɪk/` · **US:** `/sɪmˈbɑːlɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sym/symbo/symbolic__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sym/symbo/symbolic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He shook his fist in a symbolic gesture of defiance.
  - The ceremony has a deep symbolic meaning.
  - The new regulations are largely symbolic (= they will not have any real effect).
  - symbolic of something The dove is symbolic of peace.

### [syndrome](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/syndrome) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪndrəʊm/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪndrəʊm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/syn/syndr/syndrome__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/syn/syndr/syndrome__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - PMS or premenstrual syndrome
  - This syndrome is associated with frequent coughing.
  - This syndrome mostly affects women in their forties.

### [synthesis](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/synthesis) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪnθəsɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪnθəsɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/syn/synth/synthesis__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/syn/synth/synthesis__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - synthesis of A with B the synthesis of art with everyday life
  - synthesis of A and B a synthesis of traditional and modern values

### [systematic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/systematic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sys/syste/systematic__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sys/syste/systematic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a systematic approach to solving the problem
  - a systematic attempt to destroy the organization
  - The prisoner was subjected to systematic torture.
  - the systematic analysis of the risks involved


## T

### [tackle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tackle_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtækl/` · **US:** `/ˈtækl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tac/tackl/tackle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tac/tackl/tackle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was booked for a late tackle on Torres.
  - He brought the burglar down with a flying tackle.

### [tactic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tactic) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtæktɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈtæktɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tac/tacti/tactic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tac/tacti/tactic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They tried all kinds of tactics to get us to go.
  - This was just the latest in a series of delaying tactics.
  - The manager discussed tactics with his team.
  - Confrontation is not always the best tactic.
  - It's time to try a change of tactic.
  - They used strong-arm (= aggressive or violent) tactics.

### [tactical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tactical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtæktɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈtæktɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tac/tacti/tactical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tac/tacti/tactical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tactical planning
  - to have a tactical advantage
  - Telling your boss you were looking for a new job was a tactical error (= it was the wrong thing to do at that time).

### [taxpayer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/taxpayer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtækspeɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtækspeɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tax/taxpa/taxpayer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tax/taxpa/taxpayer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Hundreds of thousands of pounds of taxpayers' money (= money paid in taxes) have been spent on the project.
  - The proposals will be popular with higher-rate taxpayers.

### [tempt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tempt) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tempt/` · **US:** `/tempt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tem/tempt/tempt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tem/tempt/tempt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tempt somebody I was tempted by the dessert menu.
  - Don't tempt thieves by leaving valuables clearly visible.
  - tempt somebody into (doing) something He was tempted into a life of crime.
  - tempt somebody to do something I was tempted to take the day off.

### [tenant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tenant_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtenənt/` · **US:** `/ˈtenənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ten/tenan/tenant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ten/tenan/tenant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They had evicted their tenants for non-payment of rent.
  - The decorating was done by a previous tenant.
  - tenant farmers (= ones who do not own their own farms)

### [tender](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tender_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtendə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtendər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ten/tende/tender__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ten/tende/tender__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tender words
  - What he needs now is a lot of tender loving care (= sympathetic treatment).

### [tenure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tenure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtenjə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtenjər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ten/tenur/tenure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ten/tenur/tenure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - his four-year tenure as president
  - She had a long tenure of office.

### [terminal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/terminal_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɜːmɪnl/` · **US:** `/ˈtɜːrmɪnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ter/termi/terminal__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ter/termi/terminal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has terminal lung cancer.
  - The illness is usually terminal.
  - (figurative) She's suffering from terminal (= very great) boredom.

### [terminate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/terminate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɜːmɪneɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈtɜːrmɪneɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ter/termi/terminate__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ter/termi/terminate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Your contract of employment terminates in December.
  - terminate something The agreement was terminated immediately.
  - to terminate a pregnancy (= to perform or have an abortion)

### [terrain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/terrain) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/təˈreɪn/` · **US:** `/təˈreɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ter/terra/terrain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ter/terra/terrain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - difficult/rough/mountainous terrain
  - They walked for miles across steep and inhospitable terrain.

### [terrific](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/terrific) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/təˈrɪfɪk/` · **US:** `/təˈrɪfɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ter/terri/terrific__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ter/terri/terrific__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I feel absolutely terrific today!
  - She's doing a terrific job.
  - You look terrific in that dress.
  - He was really terrific when I was in trouble.
  - That’s terrific news!
  - (ironic) So nobody’s coming. Terrific!

### [testify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/testify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtestɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈtestɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tes/testi/testify__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tes/testi/testify__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - testify against somebody/something She refused to testify against her husband.
  - testify for somebody/something There are several witnesses who will testify for the defence.
  - testify about something He was summoned to testify before a grand jury about his role in the affair.
  - testify to something/to doing something Evans testified to receiving $200 000 in bribes.
  - testify (that)… He testified (that) he was at the theatre at the time of the murder.
  - + speech ‘I was approached by a man I did not recognize,’ she testified.

### [testimony](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/testimony) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtestɪməni/` · **US:** `/ˈtestɪməʊni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tes/testi/testimony__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tes/testi/testimony__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This increase in exports bears testimony to the successes of industry.
  - The pyramids are an eloquent testimony to the ancient Egyptians' engineering skills.
  - His thick, swollen fingers bore testimony to a lifetime of toil.

### [texture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/texture) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtekstʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtekstʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tex/textu/texture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tex/textu/texture__us_1.mp3)

  - The method used will vary with the soil texture and climate.

### [thankfully](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thankfully) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈθæŋkfəli/` · **US:** `/ˈθæŋkfəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tha/thank/thankfully__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tha/thank/thankfully__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [theology](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/theology) *(noun)*

### [theoretical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/theoretical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌθɪəˈretɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˌθiːəˈretɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/theor/theoretical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/theor/theoretical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a theoretical approach

### [thereafter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thereafter) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌðeərˈɑːftə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌðerˈæftər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/there/thereafter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/there/thereafter__us_1.mp3)

### [thereby](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thereby) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌðeəˈbaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˌðerˈbaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/there/thereby__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/there/thereby__us_1_rr.mp3)

### [thoughtful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thoughtful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈθɔːtfl/` · **US:** `/ˈθɔːtfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tho/thoug/thoughtful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tho/thoug/thoughtful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He looked thoughtful.
  - They sat in thoughtful silence.

- **UK:** `/θred/` · **US:** `/θred/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/threa/thread__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/threa/thread__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [threshold](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/threshold) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈθreʃhəʊld/` · **US:** `/ˈθreʃhəʊld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/thres/threshold__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/thres/threshold__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He stepped across the threshold.
  - on the threshold She stood hesitating on the threshold.

### [thrilled](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thrilled) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/θrɪld/` · **US:** `/θrɪld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/thril/thrilled__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/thril/thrilled__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Are you pleased?’ ‘I'm thrilled.’
  - thrilled about/at something He was thrilled at the prospect of seeing them again.
  - thrilled with something I'm really thrilled with the results.
  - thrilled to do something I was thrilled to be invited.
  - thrilled that… (British English) She was thrilled to bits (= extremely pleased) that he'd been offered the job.

### [thrive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thrive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/θraɪv/` · **US:** `/θraɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/thriv/thrive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/thriv/thrive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - New businesses thrive in this area.
  - These animals rarely thrive in captivity.

### [tide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tide_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/taɪd/` · **US:** `/taɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tid/tide_/tide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tid/tide_/tide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the ebb and flow of the tide
  - The tide is in/out.
  - Is the tide coming in or going out?
  - The body was washed up on the beach by the tide.

### [tighten](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tighten) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaɪtn/` · **US:** `/ˈtaɪtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tig/tight/tighten__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tig/tight/tighten__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tighten something to tighten a lid/screw/rope/knot
  - She tightened her grip on his arm.
  - The nuts weren't properly tightened and the wheel came off.
  - tighten something up The brake cable needs tightening up.
  - (+ adv./prep.) Her grip seemed to tighten on the door handle.
  - He saw his father's jaw tighten in irritation.

### [timber](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/timber) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɪmbə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtɪmbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tim/timbe/timber__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tim/timbe/timber__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the timber industry

### [timely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/timely) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaɪmli/` · **US:** `/ˈtaɪmli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tim/timel/timely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tim/timel/timely__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - This has been a timely reminder to us all.
  - I was grateful for your timely intervention.
  - All claims must be dealt with in a professional and timely manner.
  - The timely intervention of a teacher meant a fight was avoided.

### [tobacco](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tobacco) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/təˈbækəʊ/` · **US:** `/təˈbækəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tob/tobac/tobacco__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tob/tobac/tobacco__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Several different tobaccos are grown on the land.

### [tolerance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tolerance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɒlərəns/` · **US:** `/ˈtɑːlərəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tol/toler/tolerance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tol/toler/tolerance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/ˈtɒləreɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈtɑːləreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tol/toler/tolerate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tol/toler/tolerate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tolerate something Their relationship was tolerated but not encouraged.
  - This sort of behaviour will not be tolerated.
  - tolerate (somebody/something) doing/being/having something She refused to tolerate being called a liar.

### [toll](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/toll_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/təʊl/` · **US:** `/təʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tol/toll_/toll__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tol/toll_/toll__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [top](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/top_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tɒp/` · **US:** `/tɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/top/top__/top__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/top/top__/top__us_1.mp3)

### [torture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/torture_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɔːtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtɔːrtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tor/tortu/torture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tor/tortu/torture__us_1.mp3)

- **Examples:**
  - toss something + adv./prep. I tossed the book aside and got up.
  - toss something to somebody He tossed the ball to Anna.
  - toss somebody something He tossed Anna the ball.

## U

### [undergraduate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/undergraduate) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌndəˈɡrædʒuət/` · **US:** `/ˌʌndərˈɡrædʒuət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/undergraduate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/undergraduate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a first-year undergraduate
  - an undergraduate course/student/degree
  - She teaches physics at undergraduate level.
  - during her undergraduate years at the University of California
  - memoirs of his undergraduate career at the University of Toronto

### [underlying](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/underlying) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌndəˈlaɪɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˌʌndərˈlaɪɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/underlying__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/underlying__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The underlying assumption is that the amount of money available is limited.
  - Unemployment may be an underlying cause of the rising crime rate.

### [undermine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/undermine) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌndəˈmaɪn/` · **US:** `/ˌʌndərˈmaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/undermine__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/undermine__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our confidence in the team has been seriously undermined by their recent defeats.
  - This crisis has undermined his position.
  - Recent changes have undermined teachers’ morale.
  - The director saw this move as an attempt to undermine his authority.
  - It's all a plot to undermine me.

### [undoubtedly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/undoubtedly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈdaʊtɪdli/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈdaʊtɪdli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/undou/undoubtedly__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/undou/undoubtedly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [unify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːnɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːnɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uni/unify/unify__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uni/unify/unify__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - a unified transport system

### [unprecedented](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unprecedented) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈpresɪdentɪd/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈpresɪdentɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unp/unpre/unprecedented__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unp/unpre/unprecedented__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈʌpɡreɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈʌpɡreɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/upg/upgra/upgrade__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/upg/upgra/upgrade__us_2.mp3)

### [upgrade](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/upgrade_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌpˈɡreɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈʌpɡreɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/upg/upgra/upgrade__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/upg/upgra/upgrade__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - upgrade something We are constantly upgrading our software to meet customers' needs.
  - upgrade (from something) (to something) customers who want to upgrade from version 4.2 to version 4.5
  - Users can easily upgrade to the new version.

### [uphold](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/uphold) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ʌpˈhəʊld/` · **US:** `/ʌpˈhəʊld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uph/uphol/uphold__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uph/uphol/uphold__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - The regime has been criticized for failing to uphold human rights.
  - We are determined to uphold the law.

### [utility](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/utility_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/juːˈtɪləti/` · **US:** `/juːˈtɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uti/utili/utility__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uti/utili/utility__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a privatized electricity utility

  - Legislation will be introduced to regulate the privatized utilities.

### [utilize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/utilize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːtəlaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːtəlaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uti/utili/utilize__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uti/utili/utilize__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The Romans were the first to utilize concrete as a building material.
  - The resources at our disposal could have been better utilized.
  - Vitamin C helps the body utilize the iron present in your body.

### [utterly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/utterly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʌtəli/` · **US:** `/ˈʌtərli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/utt/utter/utterly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/utt/utter/utterly__us_1.mp3)

### [venture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/venture_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈventʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈventʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ven/ventu/venture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ven/ventu/venture__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They ventured nervously into the water.
  - He's never ventured abroad in his life.

### [verbal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/verbal) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɜːbl/` · **US:** `/ˈvɜːrbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ver/verba/verbal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ver/verba/verbal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The job applicant must have good verbal skills.
  - non-verbal communication (= expressions of the face, gestures, etc.)

### [verdict](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/verdict) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɜːdɪkt/` · **US:** `/ˈvɜːrdɪkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ver/verdi/verdict__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ver/verdi/verdict__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Has the jury reached a verdict?
  - The jury returned a verdict (= gave a verdict) of guilty.
  - The coroner recorded a verdict of accidental death.

### [verify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/verify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈverɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈverɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ver/verif/verify__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ver/verif/verify__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - verify something We have no way of verifying his story.
  - verify that… Please verify that there is sufficient memory available before loading the program.
  - Please verify that your password was entered correctly.
  - verify whether, what, etc… I'll leave you to verify whether these claims are true.

### [verse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/verse) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/vɜːs/` · **US:** `/vɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ver/verse/verse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ver/verse/verse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in verse Most of the play is written in verse, but some of it is in prose.

### [versus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/versus) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɜːsəs/` · **US:** `/ˈvɜːrsəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ver/versu/versus__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ver/versu/versus__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It is France versus Brazil in the final.
  - in the case of the State versus Ford

### [vessel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vessel) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvesl/` · **US:** `/ˈvesl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ves/vesse/vessel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ves/vesse/vessel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to burst/rupture a blood vessel

### [veteran](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/veteran) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvetərən/` · **US:** `/ˈvetərən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vet/veter/veteran__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vet/veter/veteran__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the veteran American actor, Clint Eastwood

### [viable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/viable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvaɪəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈvaɪəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/via/viabl/viable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/via/viabl/viable__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a viable option/proposition
  - There is no viable alternative.
  - to be commercially/politically/financially/economically viable
  - If there was any delay then the rescue plan would cease to be viable.

### [vibrant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vibrant) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvaɪbrənt/` · **US:** `/ˈvaɪbrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vib/vibra/vibrant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vib/vibra/vibrant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a vibrant city
  - Thailand is at its most vibrant during the New Year celebrations.

### [vice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vice_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/vaɪs/` · **US:** `/vaɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vic/vice_/vice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vic/vice_/vice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - At the door were two plain-clothes detectives from the vice squad.
  - The bright 21-year-old turned to a secret life of vice after getting bored with her studies at college.

### [vicious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vicious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɪʃəs/` · **US:** `/ˈvɪʃəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vic/vicio/vicious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vic/vicio/vicious__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a vicious attack
  - a vicious criminal
  - She has a vicious temper.
  - Police described the robbery as particularly vicious.

### [villager](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/villager) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɪlɪdʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈvɪlɪdʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vil/villa/villager__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vil/villa/villager__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Some of the villagers have lived here all their lives.

### [violate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/violate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvaɪəleɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈvaɪəleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vio/viola/violate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vio/viola/violate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to violate international law

### [violation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/violation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌvaɪəˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌvaɪəˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vio/viola/violation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vio/viola/violation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They were in open violation of the treaty.

### [virtue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/virtue) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɜːtʃuː/` · **US:** `/ˈvɜːrtʃuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vir/virtu/virtue__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vir/virtu/virtue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He led a life of virtue.
  - She was certainly no paragon of virtue (= her moral standards were very far from perfect).

### [vocal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vocal_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvəʊkl/` · **US:** `/ˈvəʊkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/voc/vocal/vocal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/voc/vocal/vocal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - vocal music
  - the vocal organs (= the tongue, lips, etc.)

### [vow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vow_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/vaʊ/` · **US:** `/vaʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vow/vow__/vow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vow/vow__/vow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - vow to do something She vowed never to speak to him again.
  - vow (to somebody) (that)… He vowed (that) he had not hurt her.
  - vow something They vowed eternal friendship.
  - + speech ‘I'll be back,’ she vowed.

### [vulnerability](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vulnerability) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌvʌlnərəˈbɪləti/` · **US:** `/ˌvʌlnərəˈbɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vul/vulne/vulnerability__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vul/vulne/vulnerability__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the vulnerability of newborn babies to disease
  - financial vulnerability
  - He was intensely aware of his own vulnerability.

### [vulnerable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vulnerable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvʌlnərəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈvʌlnərəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vul/vulne/vulnerable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vul/vulne/vulnerable__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - These offices are highly vulnerable to terrorist attack.
  - Old people are particularly vulnerable to the flu.
  - She looked very vulnerable standing there on her own.
  - In cases of food poisoning, young children are especially vulnerable.
  - The sudden resignation of the financial director put the company in a very vulnerable position.
  - We should protect the most vulnerable members of our society.
  - Animals are at their most vulnerable when searching for food for their young.


## W

### [ward](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ward_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɔːd/` · **US:** `/wɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/ward_/ward__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/ward_/ward__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a maternity/surgical/psychiatric/children’s ward
  - on the ward He worked as a nurse on the children's ward.

### [warehouse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warehouse) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈweəhaʊs/` · **US:** `/ˈwerhaʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/wareh/warehouse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/wareh/warehouse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Police are investigating a fire at a furniture warehouse.
  - He's a warehouse manager for an import company.

### [warfare](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warfare) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɔːfeə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwɔːrfer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warfa/warfare__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warfa/warfare__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - air/naval/guerrilla warfare
  - countries engaged in warfare

### [warrant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warrant_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɒrənt/` · **US:** `/ˈwɔːrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warra/warrant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warra/warrant__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an arrest warrant
  - warrant for something They issued a warrant for her arrest.
  - warrant to do something They had a warrant to search the house.

### [warrant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warrant_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɒrənt/` · **US:** `/ˈwɔːrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warra/warrant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warra/warrant__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - warrant something Further investigation is clearly warranted.
  - The TV appearance was so brief that it hardly warranted comment.
  - They do not consider the case serious enough to warrant a government enquiry.
  - warrant (somebody/something) doing something The situation scarcely warrants their/them being dismissed.

### [warrior](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warrior) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɒriə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwɔːriər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warri/warrior__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warri/warrior__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a warrior nation (= whose people are skilled in fighting)
  - a Zulu warrior

### [weaken](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weaken) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwiːkən/` · **US:** `/ˈwiːkən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wea/weake/weaken__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wea/weake/weaken__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The team has been weakened by injury.
  - The new evidence weakens the case against her.
  - This new story has severely weakened the President’s position.
  - His authority is steadily weakening.

### [weave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weave_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/wiːv/` · **US:** `/wiːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wea/weave/weave__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wea/weave/weave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - weave A from B The baskets are woven from strips of willow.
  - weave B into A The strips of willow are woven into baskets.
  - weave something together threads woven together
  - weave (something) Most spiders weave webs that are almost invisible.
  - She is skilled at spinning and weaving.

### [weed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weed_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wiːd/` · **US:** `/wiːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wee/weed_/weed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wee/weed_/weed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The yard was overgrown with weeds.
  - Routine maintenance of the garden consists of keeping weeds under control.
  - There were weeds pushing up through the gravel.

### [well](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/well_4) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wel/` · **US:** `/wel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/well_/well__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/well_/well__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to dig/sink a well
  - The dog fell down a well.

### [well-being](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/well-being) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwel biːɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈwel biːɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/well_/well_being_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/well_/well_being_1_us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - emotional/physical/psychological well-being

### [whatever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whatever_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wɒtˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/wətˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wha/whate/whatever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wha/whate/whatever__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [whatsoever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whatsoever) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌwɒtsəʊˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌwʌtsəʊˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wha/whats/whatsoever__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wha/whats/whatsoever__us_6.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They received no help whatsoever.
  - ‘Is there any doubt about it?’ ‘None whatsoever.’

### [whereby](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whereby) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/weəˈbaɪ/` · **US:** `/werˈbaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/where/whereby__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/where/whereby__us_1_rr.mp3)

### [whilst](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whilst) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/waɪlst/` · **US:** `/waɪlst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/whils/whilst__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/whils/whilst__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/wɪp/` · **US:** `/wɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/whip_/whip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/whip_/whip__us_1.mp3)

- **Examples:**

  - Jasmine's eyes widened with disbelief.
  - widen into something Here the stream widens into a river.
  - widen out The road gradually widens out.
  - widen something They may have to widen the road to cope with the increase in traffic.

### [widow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/widow_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɪdəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈwɪdəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wid/widow/widow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wid/widow/widow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [width](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/width) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪtθ/` · **US:** `/wɪtθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wid/width/width__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wid/width/width__us_1_rr.mp3)
