  - The carpet is available in different widths.

### [willingness](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/willingness) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɪlɪŋnəs/` · **US:** `/ˈwɪlɪŋnəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wil/willi/willingness__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wil/willi/willingness__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Success in studying depends on a willingness to learn.

### [wipe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wipe_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/waɪp/` · **US:** `/waɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wip/wipe_/wipe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wip/wipe_/wipe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - wipe something (on something) Please wipe your feet on the mat.
  - He wiped his hands on a clean towel.
  - wipe something with something She was sniffing and wiping her eyes with a tissue.
  - wipe something + adj. He wiped his plate clean with a piece of bread.

### [wit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wit) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪt/` · **US:** `/wɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wit/wit__/wit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wit/wit__/wit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to have a quick/sharp/dry/ready wit
  - a woman of wit and intelligence
  - a book full of the wit and wisdom of his 30 years in politics

### [withdrawal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/withdrawal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪθˈdrɔːəl/` · **US:** `/wɪθˈdrɔːəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wit/withd/withdrawal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wit/withd/withdrawal__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You can make withdrawals of up to $250 a day.
  - She made a withdrawal of £250 from her bank account.

### [workout](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/workout) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɜːkaʊt/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrkaʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worko/workout__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worko/workout__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She does a 20-minute workout every morning.
  - The team had a hard workout this morning.
  - When I do a good workout, I feel fine.

### [worship](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worship_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɜːʃɪp/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrʃɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worsh/worship__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worsh/worship__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an act/a place of worship
  - morning/evening worship (= a church service in the morning/evening)
  - The tradition of ancestor worship stretches back thousands of years.

### [worship](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worship_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɜːʃɪp/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrʃɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worsh/worship__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worsh/worship__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The Mayans built jungle pyramids to worship their gods.

### [worthwhile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worthwhile) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌwɜːθˈwaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˌwɜːrθˈwaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worth/worthwhile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worth/worthwhile__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was in aid of a worthwhile cause (= a charity, etc.).
  - a worthwhile discussion/job
  - The smile on her face made it all worthwhile.
  - We all felt we had done something worthwhile for the local community.
  - worthwhile for somebody to do something High prices in the UK make it worthwhile for buyers to look abroad.
  - worthwhile to do something It is worthwhile to include really high-quality illustrations.
  - worthwhile doing something It didn't seem worthwhile writing it all out again.

### [worthy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worthy_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɜːði/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrði/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worth/worthy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worth/worthy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Very few of his ideas are worthy of further attention.
  - A number of the report's findings are worthy of note.
  - No composer was considered worthy of the name until he had written an opera.
  - a worthy champion (= one who deserved to win)
  - He felt he was not worthy of her.


## Y

### [yell](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yell_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/jel/` · **US:** `/jel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yel/yell_/yell__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yel/yell_/yell__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - yell (at somebody/something) He yelled at the other driver.
  - yell at somebody to do something She yelled at the child to get down from the wall.
  - yell with something They yelled with excitement.
  - yell out (in something) She yelled out in pain.
  - + speech ‘Be careful!’ he yelled.
  - yell something (at somebody/something) The crowd yelled encouragement at the players.
  - yell out something He yelled out her name.
  - yell for something He yelled for help.

### [yield](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yield_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/jiːld/` · **US:** `/jiːld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yie/yield/yield__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yie/yield/yield__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a high crop yield
  - a reduction in milk yield
  - This will give a yield of 10% on your investment.

### [yield](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yield_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/jiːld/` · **US:** `/jiːld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yie/yield/yield__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yie/yield/yield__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Higher-rate deposit accounts yield good returns.
  - The research has yielded useful information.
  - These trees no longer yield fruit.
  - The project is expected to yield good returns in future.

### [youngster](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/youngster) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjʌŋstə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈjʌŋstər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/young/youngster__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/young/youngster__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The camp is for youngsters aged 8 to 14.
